[Open] sets a proof field to ⊥ when a field its type mentions is written or borrowed, and an assignment to the proof field is checked against its type at the current contents. That type is computed on a private copy, where reading the lent field ends the borrow with its *current* content. The live borrow can then write a different value. Nothing invalidates the proof again, since the write goes through the borrow, not through a field place, and [Repack] cannot catch it, since a proof field holds `⋆`. With `Pos := MkPos(n : Word, h : IsSucc(n))`:
```
def LieLent (p : &Pos) : Unit := match *p { MkPos(n, h) => (let h0 = h; let r = &n; h := h0; *r := Zero) }
def BoomLent : False := (let p = MkPos(Succ(Zero), refl); LieLent(&p); match p { MkPos(n, h) => h })
```
`GetLent` does the same with the borrow returned, and its caller writes (`BoomGetLent`).
Decision (a): while part of the value is lent (a live loan in its fields), the proof field's assignment is checked on a private copy in which each such borrow holds a fresh abstract value of its type. The proof must hold whatever the borrow leaves. This is the "opaque fill" dllbc used for packed invariants plus returned borrows (dllbc/docs/15). It admits exactly the invariants a borrow cannot break: `CellGet` returns `&Word` into `MkCell(o : Opt(Word), h : IsSome(o))` and re-proves `h` by `refl`, since `Some(σ)` is `Some` whatever `σ` is. That is the shape a user-land `Vec`'s `get : &E` needs (docs/10).
Alternative (b): clear the proof fields that mention a field when a borrow into that field ends, as a write through the field place would. Rejected. When a function returns the borrow, it ends in the caller after the function has returned, so every accessor that returns a borrow into data an invariant mentions would hand back a broken value. (b) also needs to know which field each borrow came through.
Measured: 0 verdict flips in the suite (1,357 declarations, case studies included); the new ledger row (`lentProofs`, class soundness, witnesses `BoomLent`, `BoomGetLent`) flips 5 declarations; the D19 and K4 rows pick up the new controls. Cost: a borrow still live at the assignment is treated as able to write, even if it is never used again (`RefillLentLive` is refused; ending the borrow first, `RefillLent`, is accepted), because the checker has no liveness analysis. The paper describes it in the appendix (open and repack) and in the arrays paragraph of the implementation section.


## D70. Rule switches and the counterfactual ledger removed (user, 2026-10-28) [decided; lane: no-switches]
The checker had 62 `Config` switches, each turning one rule off, read at 132 places (114 in `Machine.lean`), and a ledger (`Examples/Ledger.lean`, 58 rows) asserting exactly which verdicts flip with each switch off, and each row's class (soundness, false lemma, model, policy, completeness). The user's position: which rules make up the checker is an encapsulated working detail; a test says whether a program is accepted, rejected or runs to a given result, never why. Against keeping them:
- every switch is a branch that exists only for counterfactual runs, and about ten keep whole old implementations alive (`leafRule` and `closureConv` modes, `unitNorm`, `inferRecPos`, `blockRefCapture`, `erasureByDecl`, `p5`);
- the consistency statement (`Ochr/Consistency.lean`, branch `consistency-spec`) is about the checker at its defaults, so every lemma about the machine would carry the dead branches, and the totality and no-fallback refactors would have to preserve them;
- the ledger showed each rule necessary for some verdict, never that the rules suffice, which only a proof shows;
- every row asserts an exact flip list, so adding a test or touching a rule often meant editing rows, and the ledger re-ran the suite 58 times per build.
What the ledger bought, and what replaces it:
- *Specificity* (a `reject def` that starts failing for an unrelated reason no longer guards its rule): most counterexamples that were ledger witnesses get an accepted near-miss twin, the same program minus the offending part (merged to main as `bf2d84da`), so the rejection is seen to come from that part. Not all: `ClosingOff.MoveBorrowSplit` pins a known incompleteness and has none, `DepFields.bad`'s would need an inhabitant of an empty type, and a few twins are weak: they restate a reflexive equation or a test already in the suite (`ClearOk`, `CoIndOk`, `ConvPickOk`, `Boom3Ok`, `TYOk`, `QOk`, `InjLenOk`, `N1ClosedOk`, `BoomX4Ok`), which shows the program is well formed but not much more.
- *Minimality* ("every rule earns its place", BRIEF): rests on the paper's table of side conditions (`fig-why`, Figure 15 after this change), which gives each side condition the counterexample that forced it. Whether a rule is still needed is checked when the question arises, by removing it on a throwaway branch and running the suite.
Kept: `trace` and `derivation`, which only record what happened. Deleted: `Ledger.lean`, `CaseStudyLedger.lean`, the flip machinery (`Registry.flipsDetail`, `switches`, `rowClass`; `Test.blockFlips`, `libChanges`, `blockedBy`), the fuzzer's `--switch` and `--diff` and its baseline attribution, and the switch-reading tests. The paper's §8 ledger paragraph and its figure, the contributions bullet, §6's "by principle" paragraph, the claims table and the fuzzer paragraph are rewritten; the fuzzer's per-rule rediscovery (17 of the then 20 soundness rows) is reported as development history. Phase 2, after the no-fallbacks lane landed: `Machine.lean`'s 113 switch reads collapsed to their defaults, the dead alternative implementations deleted (the `closureConv` and `leafRule` modes, `unitNorm` with `mkTInd`'s flag, `blockRefCapture` with the binding field `blockRef`, the value-based erasure reading, `isDataType`, the capture-based copy rule, `footprint`'s single-owner and pre-D68 modes), and `Config` reduced to `trace` and `derivation`. Verdicts: all unchanged (1,558 after rebasing over the near-miss twins).

## D71. Fording: value parameters, equation fields, non-uniform recursion (user, 2026-10-28) [decided; lane: fording]
How Ochr expresses indexed families (`Vec(A, n)`, `Le`, reachability, permutation by swaps, typing judgements) was argued in `ochr/docs/13-fording-vs-indices.md`: the status quo, Fording as an emergent property, native kernel indices, or surface indices translated to Fording. The user chose option B, Fording, as an emergent property of reasonable rules rather than a feature. The `fording-probe` (notes/fording-probe.md on that branch) found that it takes three relaxations of [Ind-decl], not two:
- *Value parameters.* Every argument of `D(ā)` was a type position, so an inductive could declare `n : Word` but never be applied (`Tag(Succ(Zero))`: "written where a type is expected"); no inductive in the suite had one. Now a parameter whose declared type is syntactically a sort is a type parameter, any other a value parameter, whose argument is an erased position (the machine already ran every parameter on a private copy, `evalParams`; only D55's [Type-pos] test changes).
- *Equation fields.* `Eq(T, a, b)` with `T` first-order data is a proof field. The ban had no recorded reason: equations fell outside D36's "first-order data" wording of positivity, and K4 grouped `Eq` with Π, sorts and `&` though its only recorded attack is for Π.
- *Non-uniform recursive occurrences.* At a value parameter an occurrence of `D` takes any erased term over the parameters and earlier fields (`VecF(A, m)` inside `VecF(A, n)`). This also settles the paper/checker mismatch on closed non-uniform type arguments (`Nest(A) := … Deeper(d : Nest(Unit))`, accepted by the checker, forbidden by the paper): both are allowed.
Restrictions, each with what it rejects: no part of an equation field, and no argument of an occurrence of `D`, mentions `D` (`SelfEq`); a parameter an equation field mentions is not nestable, by the existing nesting condition (`Loop`); equations only over first-order data (`FunEq`, `SortEq`: no field equates functions, [Conv-fun]); and, found by the attack pass, no parameter has a borrow type (`RefP`: with the probe's rules `inductive RefP (r : &Word)` was accepted and `RefP(&x)` usable, a borrow recorded in a type and in constructor values). None of the first three is forced by a known proof of `False`; they keep positivity syntactic.
Evidence (`Examples/Fording.lean`, 68 declarations, every rejection asserted with its reason): Forded `VecF` with a total `Head`, `Len` and a three-line `LenIdx`; `LeF`, `Reach`, `Perm` by swaps and a small typing judgement, built and inverted; attacks rejected for the intended reason (length lies directly, while lent, through a non-uniform field, by replacing a tail, through a stale proof captured by a closure; eliminating an impossible arm at an unknown index, in code and in a statement's stuck block; transport under a false equation; a non-subsingleton witness extracted into data; a value parameter whose type only computes to a sort used as a field type). All 1,558 earlier verdicts unchanged; total 1,626.
Costs, confirmed by the probe: constructors take their parameters explicitly with a `refl` per equation, lemmas rewrite along each arm's equation, data transport needs `J`, and an impossible arm is eliminated only once its index is split (docs/13's option D would remove these).
Still open: *induction on proofs*. Recursion is never on a proof (`LeZ … by d` is rejected), so a Forded `Prop` relation gives construction and case analysis but not rule induction; the workaround is a `Type₀` derivation (`LeD`, `DToF`, `DTrans`), and the probe sketches a syntactic guard for real recursion on proofs (docs/13). Found here and fixed by D72 (not Fording-specific): a dependency lie in an arm of a match followed by more code was accepted, because the match is closed off as a stuck block and its arms reach no whole-again point; the same holds for D64's dependent fields on main. With an equation field every concrete call then fails when the borrow ends; with a data field the value has the wrong type. The tail-position lie is rejected.

## D72. A stuck block's arms end its borrows: [Repack] at the end of each arm (2026-10-29) [lane: stuck-block]
Found by D71's attack pass and present for D64's dependent fields since they landed: `match *v { MkV(n, x) => n := Zero }; ()`, with `x : Fin1(n)`, was accepted. A match on an abstract value that is followed by more code is checked by [Split] and closed off as a stuck block; its arms' states are discarded, the place receives a sealed program, and [Repack]'s check on the caller's borrow sees a neutral it cannot look into. The direct path runs the arm and checks nothing ([Repack] is not checked on instances), so it is left with `x` of the wrong type, or, with a proof field, with `⊥` that fails when the caller's borrow ends (`Fording`'s `IdLie`, `SeqLie`). The same lie in tail position was rejected, because tail arms are checked to the function's return.
Rule: a stuck block is a call of an anonymous function of the places it captures ([Block]), and a place it takes by borrow is a borrow parameter, whose borrow ends when the block returns, at the end of each arm. So that is a whole-again point: in the state [Split] checks each arm in, every place the block lends (captured by `&`, or a borrow variable moved in whole, whose borrow the block's frame ends) must be of its type again. The arm's result is held as a temporary there, so a borrow it returns into a lent place is still in Ω (`LieRetOk`). This is the generic path's reading of the paper's existing whole-again point "the end of a borrow of the value"; the direct path, which runs the match without a block, ends no borrow there, so the generic path is the stricter: it rejects a lie a later match would repair (`RepairLater`) and one on a local the direct path drops (`LieDropped`), both pinned as fail-safe incompletenesses.
Evidence: `DependentFields.lean`'s block `StuckBlocks` (data and proof fields, nested blocks, a block reaching `*v` through another block's arm, an owned local, an arm lending or passing the broken value, a lie in a closure's body, a lie before returning a borrow, each with an accepted twin) and `Fording.lean`'s `IdLie`, `SeqLie`, `SeqHonest`; every rejection asserted with its reason. All 1,626 earlier verdicts unchanged; total 1,650.
Found here and fixed by D73 (outside this rule): a lie through a borrow of an index field returned by a call, made by the caller, was accepted: `let r = GetN(v); *r := Zero` with `GetN (v : &V) : &Word := match *v { MkV(n, x) => &n }`, and the same with a block in place of `GetN`. The call closes off, `v`'s loan is filled with a sealed program with a hole for the returned borrow, and the caller's write lands in the hole, so the check at `v`'s end again sees a neutral; the direct path leaves `x : One` under `n = Zero`. Lending an index field past a whole-again point is the root: a lent index field would have to count as an unknown of its type there ([Open]'s reading for assignments), which would reject `GetN` itself.

## D73. A lent part is unknown at a whole-again point (2026-10-29) [lane: getn-gap]
Found by D72's attack pass. `GetN (v : &V) : &Word := match *v { MkV(n, x) => &n }`, with `x : Fin1(n)`, was accepted: [Repack] at its return read the lent `n` as what its borrow held then (a live loan's value is its borrow's content), so `x` checked. The caller then writes any `Word` through the result. At a generic call `GetN` closes off, so the owner holds a sealed program that no later check can look into; at an instance the owner is `MkV(Zero, O)`. A statement that inspects the length without reading the owner whole computes to `False` there, so `BoomI2 : False := Boom2Pf(One1)` was a closed proof of `False` (`LentIndex`, every step asserted). The proof-field version (`GetPN` over `Pos`) was already rejected, because borrowing `n` sets `h` to ⊥; data fields keep their content's type when the value is open, which is what let `GetN` through.
Rule: at a whole-again point, a part of the value that is still lent is checked as a fresh abstract value of the lent content's type (`lentUnknown` in `repackCheck`), since its borrow may still write any such value. This is [Open]'s existing reading of a lent part (a proof assigned while part of its value is lent is checked with the lent part unknown), extended to [Repack]. A borrow of a field nothing depends on is unaffected (`GetW`, `LendFreeWrite`); returning a borrow of an index field is rejected, which is the point: such an API lets the caller break the dependents.
Evidence: `DependentFields.lean`'s block `LentIndex` (the attack chain and its block form `LendIndexWrite`, `BoomB`, with twins over a type with an independent field). One verdict flipped, D72's own `StuckBlocks.LieRetOk` (an arm returning `&n`): it was this pattern in a block, so its body is now `reject def LendIndex`, and `LieRetOk` returns `&x` instead. Every other verdict unchanged; total 1,663.


## D74. Decisions are made where the machine cannot run (2026-10-29) [lane: two-paths]
User request (docs/14, "enforce the two-paths principle in the code's types"): make §6.3's principle, no decision that changes a result is read from a normal form, hold by the checker's construction instead of by hand. `notes/two-paths-census.md` lists the checker's 25 decision sites and classifies them: read from syntax and declared types; read from a value or normal form; or value-dependent by design, with the argument that refinement cannot change the outcome.
Rule (implementation, no change to the calculus): every reader of declared types and every decision made from them (erasure and the pre-pass, a function type's class, proof parameters, [Close]'s row, whether a match is on a proof, the sort of a type) lives in `Ochr/Decide.lean`, which sits before `Ochr/Machine.lean` in the import graph and does not import it. The evaluator is not in scope there, so no decision can run a term: it reads syntax, declarations, the state's tables and the heads of values, which evaluation and refinement preserve (D54, D55). The one site that ran the machine was the sort of a Π-type value, which evaluated its parameter types; it now reads their declared sorts. [T-Pi] still runs them, as typing must (it is what rejects `&Type`, D66), and asserts the two sorts agree (D55); the assertion never fires on the suite. The sites that depend on values by design are marked in the code with their census number: [Split], `Eq` and `J`, conversion, [Rec], footprints, copy-ness, matches on proofs, η at `Unit`, re-observation of a stuck side, and two that read a value's shape but agree with a type test on every well-formed state (`stmtArgIf`, by A.5 condition 5) or disagree only towards not erasing a function unknown at the generic call (`refineDecl`).
Evidence: the full build with every verdict unchanged. A transient finding during the move: with the sort of a Π-type read syntactically everywhere, `Universes.Impred` and `PolyTy` (`Π(x : &Type) …`) were accepted, because computing the sort had been the only place [T-Pi] checked its parameter types; restored by `piSort`.
Not done: decision outcomes with constructors private to `Decide.lean`, so that the evaluator could obtain an erasure flag or a class only from a decider. Lean's `private` is per file, and `closeOffMatch` needs facts only the evaluator has (each arm's end state), so this needs the evaluator to hand facts to pure deciders. Left for later.

## D75. A closed-off `J` ends its borrows where its transported term ends (2026-10-29) [lane: depfields-model]
Found by the dependent-fields model (`notes/depfields-model.md`), which asks of every construct that produces a neutral of a dependent type whether something checked the value whole there. [T-J-stuck] checks `t` on a private copy and closes the `J` off as a stuck block through the same machinery as a stuck match, but passed it no end state, so D72's check never ran on it. `JLie (a b : Word) (h : Eq(Word, a, b)) (v : &V) := match *v { MkV(n0, x0) => J(Word, a, b, λz. Unit, h, match *v { MkV(n, x) => (n := Zero; ()) }) }` was accepted: the block takes `*v` by `&` (the transported term writes through a pattern variable of `*v`), `*v` becomes a sealed program of type `V`, and a later match splits it as a whole `V`, while at `a = b = Zero` the direct path runs the write and leaves `MkV(Zero, O)`. A statement that reads the length then computes to `False` at an instance, so `BoomJ : False := LenPf(One1)` was a closed proof of `False` on main (`4cc0beda`).
Rule: the block's one "arm" is `t`, so its borrows end where `t` ends: the places it takes by borrow are checked whole in the state `t` ends in, with its result held as a temporary, exactly as D72 does for a stuck match's arms. Every other caller of `closeOffMatch` was audited: [Split]'s arms already pass their end states, and a zero-arm match outside a proof position runs no body.
Evidence: `DependentFields.lean`'s block `JBlocks` (the chain `JLie`, `LenStmt`, `LenPf`, `BoomJ`, every rejection asserted, and the twin chain `JSame`, `LenStmtOk`, `LenPfOk`, `PosOne`, where the transported term writes the length back unchanged and a true statement is proved). All earlier verdicts unchanged; total 1,671.

## D76. The producer obligation: one choke point for the producers of neutrals of dependent type (2026-10-29) [lane: depfields-model]
Coordinator's request, from the dependent-fields model (`notes/depfields-model.md`, S2): make D72 and D75 corollaries of one principle instead of two patches, and make forgetting the check impossible in the checker's structure. Both bugs had one shape: the generic path treats a neutral of a dependent type as whole ([Repack] does not look inside one, [Split] splits one by its telescope), and one producer of such a neutral, a stuck block, had not checked whole the value it stood for; first a match's arms (D72), then a closed-off `J`'s transported term (D75), which reached the block-forming function with the arm-end argument left at its default, empty.
Rule (no new restriction; a restatement, and a restructure of the checker): (W) a value may be open only while the code that opened it runs, and wherever it passes to code that relies on its type it must be whole; (P) whatever produces a neutral of a dependent type must have checked whole the value it stands for. An abstract value stands for an argument (whole by W), a field of a refined neutral, a generalised sealed program, or a hole's axiom; a sealed program is a call ([Close]) of a function value, and every function value a checked program calls is its caller's abstract value or was formed by checking its body to its return. New prose rule [Return]: where a checked body returns, its result and every place it holds by borrow must be whole. [Def] and [Fix] check it at the body's return; a stuck block is an anonymous function ([Block]), so it returns at the end of each arm, of a match ([Split]) and of a `J` ([T-J-stuck]), its borrow parameters being the places it takes by `&`. The whole-again points of D64 are derived from W and P (A.4.8, RULES [Repack]).
Code: `closeBlock` is the only place a block is formed. It takes its arms as computations (`BlockArm`: setup, then body) and runs them itself, so no caller can hand it a block without its arms; `closeOffMatch` and the `J`'s hand-written arm are folded into it, and the zero-arm match goes through it with no arms. At each arm's end it calls `bodyReturns`, the function `checkFix` now also calls at a body's return, which fires the new rule [Return]. Two small behaviour changes, neither moving a verdict: a block arm's result is now checked whole too (a function's result is a whole-again point; the arms' results were whole by construction), and a `J`'s arm now drops its generalisation records and takes its erasure class from its arm, as a match's arms do.
Evidence: full build with every verdict unchanged (1,671 before the attack pass). The attack pass on the producers (closures, `Id` sides, a `J` inside a closure, block results, Forded `J`) adds `DependentFields.lean`'s block `Producers` and `Fording.lean`'s `JLieF`, `JLieV`, every lie with an accepted twin; total 1,685; details in `notes/depfields-model.md` §7. Not done (proposals that replace rules, for the user): S1 (proof fields carry the proposition they were checked at, replacing [Open]'s invalidation) and S3 (dependent data fields always typed by their content).

## D77. A binding's class is read from its type's syntax; a statement argument's place too (2026-10-29) [lane: stability]
The stability lane (`notes/stability.md`) attacked the two-paths census's two weakest sites, the two that read a value and were kept on argument only.
- **Site 7, a binding's class (`refineDecl`).** A binding whose declared type is a Π-type only after computing (`p : RefPred(0)`) took its erasure class from the evaluated type, or from the value bound. A sealed function value at an instance has neither, so the instance read `other` (data: calls run) where the generic call read the evaluated Π-type's class (returns types: calls erased). The two paths decided differently. It failed safe, because the run then disagreed with the erasure pre-pass and the definition was rejected with an internal error; but true statements were rejected (`Functions.InstPow`, `DirectPow`: `RunPow(p)` is `Z` for every `p : RefPred(0)`, at the sealed `p := PickP(n)`).
- **Rule.** A binding's class is read from its declared type's syntax alone (`Decide.typeDecl`, `typeFnDecl`, `agreeTypeDecl`): a call of a type function reads as the Π-type its body is written as, through `let`s and match arms, a call of the function itself saying nothing new; a constant type reads as its closed value's Π-type. Only Π-types are read through a body: a body computing to a sort or a proposition keeps the declared reading (D35, D55: `ErasureBySyntax.W`, `Fording.Sneak` unchanged). Arms that differ while one is a Π-type have no class (`conflict`), and a binding of such a type is rejected (`Functions.RunMix`). `refineDecl` now reads nothing but the declared reading.
- **Site 6, a statement argument's place (`stmtArgIf`).** Whether it runs in place or on a private copy was read off its value (does it hold a borrow or a loan), equal to a type test only on well-formed states (A.5 condition 5, no borrows in data), an invariant that is not proved. Now `Decide.stmtArgInPlace` reads its declared type: in place exactly when it is a borrow type.
- **Evidence.** Every earlier verdict unchanged at each step (full build; `tests` and `fuzz` build). New: `PickP`, `InstPow`, `InstPowZ`, `DirectPow`, the constant type `T0` with `RunT0`/`RunT0Gen`, `MixT` with `reject RunMix` and the twin `SameT`/`RunSame`, the recursive `RecP` with `RunRec`/`RunRecGen`, `MixR` with `reject UseMixR`, `LetP`/`RunLet`, `WriteProp`/`AtRec`. Total 1,663 → 1,683.
- **Type parameters (follow-up, same lane).** A type function whose body returns its type parameter (`IdTy (A : Type₁) : Type₁ := A`) first still read `other`, so `p : IdTy(Π(x : &Nat). Prop)` read as data while its calls are erased, and the pre-pass assertion rejected `RunIdTy` with an internal error. `typeDecl` now carries, per variable, what it stands for as a type when syntax says so (`tsc`): a type function's type parameter stands for the call's argument read as a type in the caller's scope, and a `let` naming a type for that type. The argument is substituted into the body's reading, never evaluated. New: `IdTy`, `RunIdTy`, `RunIdTyGen`, `AtIdTy`, `AtIdTySealed` (a sealed argument), `IdTy2`/`RunIdTy2` (through two functions), `LetTy`/`RunLetTy`, `MixArg` with `reject UseMixArg` (a parameter arm against a Π arm of another class) and its twin `UseSameArg`, and `reject UseTyVar` (a definition's own type variable is unknown at the generic call, so a value of it is not known to be a function: "not a function type"). Total 1,683 → 1,696. No binding's class is now completed from a value: what syntax cannot read is `other` on both paths, and a non-data value at such a binding is rejected by the pre-pass assertion.

## D78. A recursive call is recognised by name, not by value (2026-10-29) [lane: rec-model]
Finding (rec-model, modelling [Rec]; a closed proof of `False` on main `4cc0beda`, no holes): `recCheck` found the [Rec] entry for a call by comparing the called function value with the one being checked (`ctx.fn == fv`). A local recursive function's value is a closure over its captured values, and a [Split] on a captured value (equally an [End] or a re-normalisation) substitutes inside those captures throughout Ω, the function's binding of itself included, but not inside the [Rec] entry. So after `match n {…}` on a captured `n`, the refined closure no longer equalled the entry's and its recursive calls went unchecked:
`CapSplit (n x : Nat) : False := (fix go (y : Nat) : False by y := match n { Z => go(y), S q => go(y) })(x)`, `BoomCapSplit : False := CapSplit(0, 0)`. The same held through a closure formed in each arm (`BoomCapLam`), where capturing `go` moves it (a closure over non-copy data is not a copy), and through a capture of a capture (`Nest2`). Top-level functions (`.gfn`) were immune.
Rule: which calls recurse is a decision read from syntax (§6.3). A call is a call of the function an entry is checking when its head is the name that function binds for itself, or a nested function's capture of that name. Implementation: the binding of itself carries its entry's uid (`Binding.recUid`); a closure's capture of a marked binding is marked too, read from the captured bindings before `capture` moves them (`capMarks`); `recFrameOf` is the one place that decides it. Value equality stays only as a fail-safe that can add a check.
Audit of the bug class (identifying something by `==` on values that a refinement can change): only the [Rec] entry's identification skipped a check on a mismatch. The others fail safe: `neutrals` (a missed generalisation record knows less), `convStack` (a missed cycle runs out of fuel; a repeated pair answers "not convertible"), `classCache` (a miss recomputes); and the remaining `==` tests are semantic (reflexivity in conversion, strict subterms), where values equal after a refinement are equal in that world. [Corrected by D83: reflexivity in conversion is not semantic for values holding `⊥`, which two observations can share as values while an instance fills them differently.]
Evidence: `CapSplit`, `BoomCapSplit`, `CapSplitLam`, `BoomCapLam` rejected by [Rec], with twins `CapSplitOk`, `CapSplitLamOk` accepted (Recursion). Every earlier verdict unchanged. Model: `notes/rec-model.md`.

## D79. An η-refinement belongs to its world (2026-11-04) [lane: etaboom-fix]
Finding (EtaBoom, an attacker of docs/18, `docs/reviews/2026-11-02-plan18-simulation.md`; a closed proof of `False` on main `f5b030d4` through `7ebfdb75`, no holes): `movedPlace` η-refines an abstract `σ` of a one-constructor type to `C(σ̄)` with fresh field unknowns typed at `σ`'s type in the current world, and records it in `etaRefs` so that every private copy names `σ`'s fields alike (D68). `exitWorld` kept the record when an arm ended, on the claim that an η-refinement "follows from the type alone". Only its constructor does: its fields' types are the world's. With `inductive DP (n : Nat) := MkDP(x : Bool, y : G(n, x), z : Nat)` and `G(Z, _) = Void`, `G(S _, _) = Nat`, a block that moves `d.z` in the arm `n := Z` records `σy : Void`; the arm `n := S m` reused it, the open-field rules typed `d.y` by its content, and `match y {}` proved `False` (`Bad1`, `Boom : False := Bad1(S(Z), MkDP(true, Z, Z))`). The leak is not about dependent fields: with `MkDQ(y : G1(n), z : Nat)` (not dependent; the field's type mentions only the value parameter) a closure in the second arm types its capture of `y` through its value, `σy : Void`, and `Boom5 : False` was accepted the same way. The same leak rejected true programs (`UseY`, `CapUseY`: "argument 1 (k) has type Void, expected Nat").
Rule: an η-refinement is a world fact, scoped like a generalisation record: kept across private copies (`restoreKeep`; a copy stays within its world), dropped when the arm that made it ends (`exitWorld`). A record could outlive its world only where none of its fields' types depends on anything the arm refined, and there a sibling re-deriving it gets the same record up to fresh names, which nothing tells apart (no sibling names the arm's fresh unknowns); so the exact condition reduces to "never keep" without loss, and the two kinds of record (both maps from a value to abstract values typed in a world) now have one scoping.
Code: `exitWorld` no longer keeps `etaRefs`; its docstring states the argument, and the false claim is corrected in `notes/split-model.md` §3 and §6, `notes/erasure-model.md` (E3), RULES [Split] and docs/20. The paper's A.1 state paragraph and docs/18's worlds section still state it (not edited on this branch).
Evidence: `CaseSplits.lean`'s block `ArmEta`: `Bad1`, `Boom`, `NoReuse` (the referee's twin), `CapY`, `Boom5` rejected, reasons asserted; `UseY`, `OnlyZ` (the `Z` arm's ex falso, legitimate there), `CapUseY` accepted. All six of `Bad1`, `Boom`, `UseY`, `CapY`, `Boom5`, `CapUseY` have the wrong verdict with the fix reverted. Every earlier verdict unchanged, by a before/after dump of all 1,828 rows with their reasons: the one difference is `Moves.N1`'s rejection message, `(⊥, σ5)` → `(⊥, σ8)`, since the outer block now re-derives with fresh names the η-refinement of `*x0` that the arm `n1 := Z` made (a pair of `Nat`s, whose fields do not depend on the arm: the argument above, instanced). Total 1,828 → 1,844.

## D80. Copy-or-move and η at `Unit` are read from declared types (2026-11-05) [lane: declared-copy]
User request (correctness by construction, `ochr/docs/21`): three decisions of the two-paths census read a computed type and were kept on one-directional arguments, each a monotonicity lemma the consistency proof would need (site 16, copy or move on a read: "the generic call is stricter"; site 13, a stuck block's capture modes, as 16; site 9, η at `Unit` for a call's result: "a neutral type is only more stuck"), and a fourth, [Close]'s fill of a borrowed `Unit`, read a value (`u == ()`). They now read only syntax and declared types, as Rust decides `Copy` (a generic `T` is never `Copy` without a bound, whatever it is instantiated at), so both paths decide them identically and the lemmas disappear.
- **One reader.** D77's reader of declared types (`Decide.typeDecl`, `typeFnDecl`) also reads data: `Unit`; an inductive with its type arguments read as types (a field reads as its declared field type at them, `fieldDecl`); the arms of a type function or a match, agreeing, or else the different data types they give (`alt`: the type is one of them, so a pattern variable of `Slice(Word, n)` = `SliceOf(Cells(Word, n))`, whose `Cells` arms are `CellsEnd` and `Cell(Word, …)`, reads at `Cell`'s field type); a call of a global function as its codomain with each type parameter standing for its argument as written (`callDecl`: `IdF(Word, w)` reads `Word`; data and borrows of data only, so the call's class and row stay the callee's); `()`, and an assignment, as `Unit`; a constructor as its inductive at its written parameters. These readings are data to every other decision (no erasure class, row or proof flag reads them). `DeclInfo` gains `unit`, `ind`, `alt`, and its `BEq` is written out (a derived `BEq` of a nested inductive is `opaque`).
- **R1, copy or move.** A read copies exactly when the place's declared type reads as a copy type (`declCopy`: P3's list on readings; a type parameter never; `alt` when every alternative is one). The place's declared type is its binding's reading, through borrows and fields. `copyRead` lost its fallback to the value's constructor (`valType?`) and `isCopyType` is deleted; the same reading decides closure capture, stuck-block captures and the Fn rule, and `copy` declarations' fields (`checkInd`). Closures and Π-types record each capture's binding's reading (`Value.clo cs t rd`, `Value.tPi cs t rd`; the paper's [Capture] already said captures are recorded with their binding's declared type), and a stuck block records each parameter's: the place it takes. Before this, a capture's copy-ness came from its value, and a block parameter's from the place's stored type, which a refinement changes.
- **R2, η at `Unit`.** A value is `()` exactly where its declared type reads as `Unit`, never from a computed type. Positions: a parameter (at the generic call, at every call, at the generic instantiations of conversion and [T-Pi]; a `&Unit` parameter's content); a call's result, by its declared result type read off the call as written, whether it runs or closes off (a stuck block's by its match's declared type, not its computed type; a sealed program's head call keeps its call's decision); [Close]'s fills (R3). Not: fields of a split or η-refined unknown, holes, a `let` annotation, `Eq`/`Id`'s type slot (none is needed for the paths to agree: each keeps the value it was given). `absOf` no longer normalises by its type; `resultIsUnit` and `resultKind` are deleted.
- **R3, [Close]'s fill.** A lent place's fill is `()` exactly when the place is declared `Unit`, read off the borrow argument as written (`&c` with `c : Unit`, or a borrow variable declared `&Unit`), not when its content happens to be `()`: `ExA (A : Type₀) (x : A) (n : Nat) := (let c = x; TouchA(A, &c, n); Mk(c, Z))` fills `c` with the sealed program at `ExA(Unit, (), n)` as at its generic call.
- **Two choices the spec left open.** R2 reads the declared result type *at the call* (the head term's declared codomain), not the function value's own, and R3 reads the lent place's declared type, not the callee's parameter's: a function value's own types can read `Unit` where the type it is used at reads a type parameter (D54 refined lets `λ … : Unit` stand at `Π(…). R`), and a function-typed variable's reading keeps no parameter readings, so either alternative would let an instance decide differently from the generic call.
- **Evidence.** A before/after dump of every row with its reason: after R1 all 1,844 identical; after R2/R3 one verdict changed, `ClosingOff.NeutralKept`, rejected → accepted (true: `G`'s codomain `UU(n)` reads as `Unit` for every `n`, so both of `P`'s arguments are `()`; it was rejected while η read the computed type, neutral there). `RowI`, `ConvUnitRes`, `ConvUnitWritten`, `ComputedUnit` stay accepted (the probe's breakages came from a codomain-only rule without the reader). Decisions over the original suite, measured by logging the old and the new decision side by side: copy or move 275,163 decisions, of which 7,746 copied before and move now (instance runs reading a type-parameter place, and captures whose value was a copy) and 22 moved before and copy now (stuck types such as `CopyT(σ)`); a closed-off call's result 45,046, 6 normalised before and not now, 2 the reverse; [Close]'s fills 2,870, none changed; fresh values made for a declared position 7,047, 4 changed, and 3 made at an evaluated `Unit` with no declared position (split fields) no longer `()`; the new positions (a run call's result, a call's parameter) normalised no value that was not already `()`. 354 declarations had a decision change; one verdict moved. Intermediate runs found three gaps in the reader, not the rule, each fixed before the lane's checkpoints: borrows returned by type-parametric calls (`*GetMut(Word, …)`), pattern variables of a type function with differing arms (`alt`), and Π-types' captures (`tPi`'s readings); each first rejected array, quicksort or hashmap proofs.
- **New tests** (`ClosingOff.DeclaredReadings`, each rule with its twin, reasons asserted): `Dup`/`DupC`/`DupCAt`, `LetW`/`LetA`, `ReadMix`/`ReadCopy`, `SameEta`/`MixEta`, `DeclaredUnit`/`ComputedMix` (true, rejected: `MixT(Z)` is `Unit` only by computation) with `ComputedMixInd` proving it by induction, and `ExAAt` (rejected: the fill above) with `ExUAt`/`ExUGen`. The old checker rejects `ReadCopy` and `SameEta` and accepts `ComputedMix` and `ExAAt`. Total 1,844 → 1,869.
- **Census.** Site 16 and the copy-ness part of site 13 move to class (a), site 9 to (a), and the [Close] fill is listed as a decision, in (a) (`notes/two-paths-census.md`). Follow-up, not done: a `copy` bound on type parameters, if a program needs one.

## D81. `t.i` is the field of any type with one constructor, named by a match (2026-11-05) [lane: proj-sugar]
User's request (2026-11-05), after a proof of `Id(Unit, AddM(x, 0), ())` could not be projected (`AddMZero(&p).1`): generalise `.i` into `let C(x₀, …, xₙ) = t; xᵢ`; and, as a requirement, `Pair` must have no special case. Before, `t.0`/`t.1` were notation for `Pair` only: a `Pair`-only place step and term form (`.fst`/`.snd`, with `g.ty == "Pair"` in `stepEq`, `placeType?`, `Obs.stepV`/`updAt`, the term projection's evaluation and the heap slice), an error on an abstract pair ("no neutral projections") and on a proof ("a proof is `⋆`: match instead", `Propositions.Proj`), and the checker extension C20 (`TermProjection`).
Rule [Proj]: for `t` of a type with exactly one constructor `C(g₀ : A₀, …, gₙ : Aₙ)`, `t.i` is `let C(y₀, …, yₙ) = t; yᵢ`. On a place, `p.i` is the field place `p.gᵢ` its pattern variable names, so it is read, assigned, borrowed and moved out of in place: a term whose place operand (read, `&`, `:=`, a match's scrutinee) has the step `q.i` runs as the one-arm match on `q` naming it (`projIn` finds the root-most such step, `projMatch` builds the match). A proof is then matched by its type (D45), an abstract value is split, or closes off in an untyped run, as any match does, and a value's constructor is selected. Any other term is bound first, `let x = t; x.i` (a surface desugaring, as for `match e {…}`). `t.i` has `t`'s class: of a proof only a proof field (a data field is the D45 error), of data only a data field (a proof field of data is named by a match), so the erasure pre-pass reads `p.i` as a proof exactly when `p` is one (`placeDecl`, `leafProof`) and needs no type. For D80's readings, `p.i` of data reads as field `i` of the one constructor of the inductive `p`'s declared type reads as (`fieldAt`), which replaces D80's `Pair` case. A number's `.0` stays its predecessor place (`Nat` has two constructors and is built in).
Code: `Place.fst`/`.snd` and `Step.fst`/`.snd` become `.proj i`; `Term.fst`/`.snd` are deleted; `stepEq` identifies `.proj i` with a constructor field `i` of constructor 0 (a type with one constructor has only that one); `Obs.stepV`/`updAt` and the heap slice step into field `i` of a constructor value positionally; `placeType?` of `q.i` is that of the field place; `isPropTerm?` and `paramFlags` lose their `Pair` fast path; the rule `Proj` is fired and printed (appendix, *Projection* ([Proj])); `Ext.TermProjection` is deleted. `Pair` remains only in the notation `A × B`, `(a, b)` and its printing.
Evidence: full build, `tests`, `fuzz` and `heapslice` build; every earlier verdict unchanged except two that the rule changes on purpose, `Numbers.SwapPairProj` (`(p.1, p.0)` of an abstract pair, now split as by a match) and `Propositions.Proj` (`h.0` of `h : P ∧ Q`), both now accepted. New, in `Numbers`: a three-field `Trip` read, written, borrowed and projected from a term and a call (`TripLast`, `TermProj`, `CallProj`, `TripWrite`, `TripBoth`), `Nested`, `reject TripTwice` (a field moved out twice), `reject LRProj` (two constructors), `reject TripOut`, and `Tagged` with `TaggedData`, `reject TaggedProof` (a proof field of data, D81's message) and its twin `TaggedProofMatch`; in `Propositions`: `ProjR`, `reject ProjWrong`, `reject ProjOut`, `SndProj`, and `reject SqProj` (a data field of a proof, D45). Total 1,869 → 1,893 (on top of D80).
Note on `Numbers.AddZero`: on main `AddMZero(&p)` already has type `Eq(Nat, …)`, since η at `Unit` makes the result's equation `True` and `Id` drops it (`mkAnd`), so there is no conjunction to project. Without η its type is `Eq(Unit, …) ∧ Eq(Nat, …)`, and `AddMZero(&p).1` is the projection the user wanted: checked on this branch before its rebase onto D80, with η at `Unit` switched off (not committed), where `AddZero` with `AddMZero(&p).1` and `Nats.AddS` with `AddMS(&x, y).1` are accepted, while `AddZero'` without `.1` is not.

## D82. Every binding has its declared type; the machine carries types (2026-11-05) [lane: typed-bodies]
User request (`ochr/docs/22`), after the `typed-bodies` probe. The machine (a called body, a sealed program's re-run) carried no types, so where a decision or typing needed a binding's type it was read off the binding's value (`placeType?` falling back to `valType?`): from a constructor's head, from Δ for an unknown, by a typing run for a sealed program. Refining an unknown to a constructor switches the route, so the same place could get its type differently on the generic call and at an instance, a hazard for (P) that the consistency proof would need a lemma per route for. It also forced `observeTyping`'s machine-first re-observation of an `Id` side (census site 21), a decision on whether a run got stuck.
- **Rule.** Every binding, in every run, has a stored type, its declared type: a called body's parameters at the callee's declared parameter types, evaluated left to right in the frame of its captures and earlier parameters, as at the generic call, and `self` at its Π-type; a closure's or Π-type's captures at their bindings' stored types, recorded when it is formed ([Closure], extending D51 from proofs to all captures and D80's readings to the types themselves); a `let` at its right-hand side's type; a sealed program's cells at the lent places' declared types ([Close]); a pattern variable, a field place, at its field's type; and the checker's internal frames that bind values to compute a type (`fieldTele`'s parameters, `fieldTypeAt`'s and `sealedResultType?`'s known parameters and fields).
- **The machine carries types.** A term's type is computed in the machine as typing computes it: a place's stored type, a call's declared codomain at its arguments (`declCallType`, [Call-type] without its argument checks), a constructor's inductive at its known parameters, an annotation, a Π-type's sort, `symm`/`trans`/`cong` from their proofs' types. Typing's checks (argument and field types, [Repack], D66, `S`'s argument, `Eq`'s sides) stay typing's. So the machine and typing differ only where a run is stuck (the machine closes off, typing splits) and in the checks; the `typed` flag still says both, and is not renamed.
- **Deleted.** `placeType?`'s value fallback; `natPlace`'s and `projMatch`'s (D81) fallbacks to the content; `observeTyping`: a side of `Id` runs with the typing judgement from the start, in every run; `idType`'s owner fallback (the type of the owner's content, or a dummy `Unit` for an owner both sides consumed): an owner's type is its binding's.
- **Representation.** `Value.clo`/`tPi`'s `rd` is `List (DeclInfo × Value)`, each slot's reading and declared type; `substV`, `renormV`, `anyAtom`, `Value.beq` and the fuzzer's `canonV` include the types, and the stability lemmas `substV_tPi`/`onValue_tPi` let a refinement change them. `pushCaps` binds a capture at its recorded type and reading, with no fallback to the value. [Close] writes each cell as `let cᵢ = (uᵢ : Tᵢ)`, `Tᵢ` from the borrow argument's type `&Tᵢ`, and the printer shows a cell without its type, so sealed programs print as before (`Units`' hand-written `N` and `B` carry the annotation).
- **Choices the spec left open.** (1) A `let`'s type is the type the machine computes for its right-hand side, as typing does, rather than a separate syntactic reader: the reader of D80 gives readings, not types, and a call's type needs its arguments' values. (2) A sealed program's cell is typed by an annotation [Close] writes, rather than by its embedded value's own type (`valType?`, route-dependent as above; before the annotation, 4,174 cells over the suite were bound with no type). (3) An unknown slot of a checker-internal frame (`⊥`, which the frame's guard ensures is never read) has no type; every other binding has one.
- **Out of scope, kept as class (c)** (census, D82 section): `valType?` on values that are not bindings: an embedded value typed by the typing judgement, a sealed function's type, and the contents [Repack], [Open] and the lending checks compare against telescope types.
- **Evidence.** At each checkpoint the full build was green with every earlier verdict unchanged (1,893); a temporary counter (not committed) found no `let` bound without a type over the whole suite after the cell annotations; `tests`, `fuzz` and `heapslice` build. New tests (`Functions`, "Called bodies are typed by declaration"), each rule with its twin, the twins' reasons asserted: `ApplyAtUnit`, `SndOf`, `SndOfRun`, `reject SndOfWrong`, `SndOfGen` (a capture projected in a closure's body); `TakeBoth`, `TakeBothRun`, `reject TakeBothWrong` (a pattern variable holding `(⊥, y)`); `StuckIdSame`, `StuckIdSameHolds`, `StuckIdDiff`, `reject StuckIdDiffHolds` (a stuck `Id` side in a type function's body); `Tag`, `MkT`, `TagSame`, `TagSameHolds`, `TagDiff`, `reject TagDiffHolds` (a binding whose value records no parameter, read by a stuck `Id` side); `PropAtUnit`, `CapSame`, `CapSameHolds`, `CapSameAt`, `CapDiff`, `reject CapDiffHolds`, `CapDiffAtZ` (the same value captured by a closure). Main's checker rejects `TagSameHolds`, `CapSame` and `CapDiff` ("cannot infer the type of the place t", "… κ") and rejects `TagDiffHolds` for that reason rather than for its false statement. Total 1,893 → 1,918.
- **Lazy type computation** (no caching or memoisation). Computing every type eagerly made checking 4–5× slower (the `Quicksort` block 1.7 s → 9.1 s compiled), so the machine computes a type only where something reads it, and no decision changes: a verdict dump of every declaration with its message, eager against lazy, is identical (and identical to main's on every earlier declaration). (1) A term's type in an untyped run is computed only where its context reads it (`MState.wantTy`/`wantHere`: a `let`'s right-hand side, an argument declared a borrow, a cell's type if the call closes off, a call's head, and their tails; typing always computes it). (2) A non-borrow parameter's declared type, and the type of a `let` bound to a call that does not return a borrow, are deferred (`Binding.lazyTy`: the Π-type, the arguments as passed, and which parameter or the codomain) and evaluated when read (`bindTy?`, `paramTypes`, which evaluates only the earlier parameters the type mentions); a refinement reaches the deferred inputs as it reaches stored types (`mapState`). A borrow's type stays eager, since the footprint and the stuck-block captures test `ty` for `&`. Of 246,842 parameter types `Quicksort` evaluated, 5,614 were ever read. Timings, compiled harness on one core (main → eager → lazy): `Quicksort` 1.73 s → 9.1 s → 3.12 s; one pass over all 85 blocks with their libraries 32.8 s → 80.9 s → 43.6 s. Remaining, by `perf` (12.5k samples against main's 7.0k for `Quicksort`): parameter types (`paramTypes`, 16% inclusive, eager borrow parameters and deferred ones read), reads of deferred types (`bindTy?`, 9%), declared codomains (`declCallType`, 7%), all through type functions such as `Cells(E, n)` evaluated anew at each read. Memoising a deferred type at its first read changed nothing (3.05 s); making borrow parameters lazy too needs the footprint (`Obs.footprint`, its stability proofs) to test `&` by reading, worth at most ~7%. Caching evaluated types across calls is left to the user.

## D83. A value holding `⊥` is convertible with nothing, itself included (2026-11-06) [lane: bot-conv] [retired by D84]
Finding (`model-slice`, writing docs/27, test 2 of docs/23; spec docs/28; a closed proof of `False` on main `10c22fd9` through `6149ec32`, no holes): `Same (n x : Nat) (h : Id(Unit, B5, B5)) : Id(Unit, B5, B6) := h` with `Bk = match n { Z => (let y = x; ()), S _ => (x := k; ()) }`, and `Boom : False := Same(S(Z), Z, refl)`. At the generic call each side is a stuck block that takes `x` by move (arm `Z` moves it, K1), so every observation leaves `x` as `⊥`, and both types compute to the stuck `Eq(Nat, ⊥, ⊥)` (D68). `conv`'s first test, `v == w`, identified them. At `n = 1` the hypothesis is `5 = 5` and the goal `5 = 6`.
Rule [Conv-bot]: a value in which `⊥` occurs (`Value.hasHole`) is convertible with nothing, itself included; `conv` answers "no" when either side holds `⊥`, before every other test. [Conv-refl] is identity for values without `⊥`. Why: `⊥` is a part the symbolic run has lost track of, which an instance may fill with anything (docs/26), so two values that have each lost information can stand for different values at an instance; conversion may answer "yes" only where supplying the missing information could not make it false (docs/23's (Mono)). D68 applied this to `Eq` computing to `True`; this applies it to conversion and to every identification of values.
Audit: every site where `==` on values, or a cache keyed by a value, can make a type check, a `J`, an `Eq` or a goal succeed.
- **`conv`'s identity tests** (before and after `unitTop`): fixed by the rule. Every recursive comparison returns through `conv` (fields and arguments by `convList`, values embedded in terms by `convT`'s `.val` case, `convPi`'s instantiated domains and codomains, `convFnRun`'s observed results and owned places), and `convT`'s own `t == u` is reached only from a sealed program, Π-type or closure whose whole value `conv` has already checked (`hasHole` looks inside terms).
- **Consumers of `conv`:** `J` computing ([J], `evalCore`) and typed ([T-J]), [Eq-refl] (`mkEqM`, behind D68's guard), `trans`'s middle point, `expectTy` (arguments, assigned values, ascriptions, `J`'s equation, a rewritten term), `packedErr`/`convVal`, a definition's goal, annotated and proof-match arms, [Conv-pi], [Conv-fun]: all go through `conv`, so all are fixed by it. `trans` was a separate route to `False` on main (`TrBotBoom`: `5 = ⊥` and `⊥ = 6` composed to `5 = 6`).
- **[Rewrite]'s occurrences** (`rewriteGoal` → `substV` with `x` the rewritten side, found by `==`): a second instance, independent of `conv`: `RwBot (h : Id(Unit, B5, (x := 5; ()))) : Id(Unit, B6, (x := 5; ())) := rewrite ← h in refl` put `5` for the goal's `⊥` (another observation's), and `RwBotBoom : False` was accepted on main and with the `conv` fix alone. Fixed: a rewritten side holding `⊥` occurs nowhere, so the goal is left as it is (C22). A replacement holding `⊥` is still substituted: the goal then knows less.
- **Generalisation records** (`neutrals`: `generalizeNeutral`, `generalizeFound`, the goal's substitution in `evalMatch`/`evalMatchInd`, `canonNeutral`'s lookup): kept, with an argument. A record's key is a sealed program that a match scrutinises, so data. `⊥` arises only in an observation (a footprint's final content, D68, or a borrow ending over a moved part while observing) and leaves it only inside an `Eq`, a proposition; reading data with a hole, capturing one ([Capture]) or lending one (`wholeBorrowArgs`) is an error, so `⊥` reaches a data program only inside a proposition or a type (written there, or copied from a place of sort `Prop` or a universe), in an erased position. Erased positions leave no trace (P2), so two such programs that are `==` denote the same value at every instance.
- **`classCache`** (`fnClass`, keyed by a Π-type): kept; a memo of a decision read from the codomain term and the captures' declared readings (D35, D77), which `⊥` cannot change (a proposition holding `⊥` is still a proposition).
- **`convStack`** (`convPi`, `convFn`): a hit answers "not convertible", so it only rejects. **`recFrameOf`**'s `ctx.fn == fv` only adds a [Rec] check (D78). **`recCheck`**'s strict subterms are of a refinement of a fresh `σ` (constructors over fresh abstract values), which never holds `⊥`. **`substV`'s `v == x`** for an abstract value or a loan (a refinement, [End]): `x` never holds `⊥`. **[Eq-inj]/[Eq-disj]**: behind D68's guard. The remaining `==` tests compare with constants (`.proof`, `.bot`, `.tNat`), not two values.
- **Measured:** a temporary build with a log at each site above reached with a value holding `⊥`, over the whole suite: the identity test on such values was never what accepted a suite program; `conv` met `⊥` once, in `Equality.MovedBothSides` (`⊤` against `Eq(Nat, ⊥, ⊥)`, "no" either way); no generalisation record, rewrite or [Rec] check met one (`fnClass` could not be logged, since `StabilityDecide` unfolds it).
Code: `conv` and `rewriteGoal` (the rule, tagged `fire .ConvBot`); `convBotNote` adds the reason to the errors of a failed conversion (`expectTy`, a definition's goal, annotated match arms, `trans`); `Rule.ConvBot` is printed by the appendix and fired.
Incompleteness: a type holding `⊥` is not convertible with itself (`SelfBot`: `P → P` for such a `P`), and neither is a closure whose code embeds a captured proof's type holding `⊥`. No suite program is affected.
Evidence: a before/after dump of all 1,893 rows with their reasons: identical but for `Equality.MovedBothSides`'s message, which gains the reason. New block `Equality.BotConv` (reasons asserted): `Same`, `Boom`, `SelfBot`, `TrBot`, `TrBotBoom`, `RwBot`, `RwBotBoom` rejected, all seven accepted by main's checker; twins `SelfKept`, `TrKept`, `RwKept` accepted (the moves replaced by clones, so the block takes `x` by `&` and leaves a sealed program, not `⊥`). Total 1,893 → 1,903. Paper: the appendix's [Conv-bot], [Conv-refl] "for `v` without `⊥`", and [T-Rewrite]'s prose; the body is unchanged. RULES P1, [Rewrite]; census site 19 corrected; D78's audit sentence annotated.


## D84. Moves are definite: drop at the join; `Id` relates sides that leave one type-state (user, 2026-11-09) [lane: d84]
Decision (Charlie, after the viability check on branch `join-drop`, f8b5bd06, reference only; revised the same day after the `Id` adequacy audit, `ochr/docs/30`). `⊥` conflated two things: "definitely moved" and "maybe moved". A stuck block took a place by move when any arm moved it (K1), while at an instance a match on a known constructor ran only its arm and left the place whole. That conflation made D83's `Boom` possible, and D83 patched it by making `⊥` convertible with nothing. Charlie's guiding principle is substitution: equal programs are freely substitutable. Yet `Id(Unit, F(x), F(y))` with `F (n : Nat) : Unit := ()` was provable, while a continuation that reads `y` runs after one side and not the other. The first version of this decision made `Id` observe every owned place a side reads (docs/30's F2). The revision drops that and makes `Id` well formed only between sides that leave the same type-state ([Id-typestate]), with docs/30's F4 as its no-ending clause, and adds docs/30's F1 ([Obs-ref]). Supersedes D68's stuck `Eq` over `⊥` and D83 ([Conv-bot] and the [Rewrite] guard).
- **[Join].** A match leaves moved, on every path, what any of its arms moves: the moved places after it are the lattice join of the arms' end states (moved ⊔ whole = moved), each arm's net of its own refills. They are read from syntax and declared types, never from a run (`Decide.jdMoves`, §6.3).
  - An arm's moves are the places it reads whose declared type is not a copy type, the borrow variables it reads whole, and the borrowed place for a read through a local borrow `let r = &q` (by syntax; the one miss of the viability check, `Moves.ThroughLocal`).
  - Whether or not its run reaches them, they also include every variable a function or Π-type in the arm captures (K1(a), whole variables) and every borrow variable it names whole (K1(b)), as the block takes them.
  - A statement moves nothing outside its own copy (D68).
  - The direct run drops, at the arm's end, what the arm left whole. It does this as a by-move argument is read: [Access] ends the place's borrowers, a borrow is ended, a copy stays. The drop comes after the arm's own drops and before the match's value is used, which is held as a temporary.
  - Dropping a place that the arm's result borrows is an error, D65's [Drop] rule at the join. `ClosingOff.MoveBorrowSplit` and `MoveBorrowCode` now give that reason.
  - The join applies to a match on a known constructor (`evalMatch`, `evalMatchInd`), in tail position (`checkTailAt`), and to `J`'s transported term, its one arm, when `J` computes, typed or not, at runtime included (D85). A stuck block's by-move captures (K1) are the same static set, and the arms' logged moves (`armMoves`) are kept only as an assertion that the two agree (`INTERNAL [D84]`; it never fires in the suite).
- **Grain**, the decision this needed: with moves observable, whether a move is of a field or of a value whole must agree between the generic call and every instance.
  - The viability prototype took the grain from what the generic call knew (`movedPlace`: an unknown of a stuck type, a sealed program or a lent value made it coarsen). It had a closed proof of `False` through `TF (n) : Type := match n { Z => Pr, S _ => Pr }`: `Moves84.G`/`Boom`.
  - The rule: a field step is kept only through a value whose declared type reads as an inductive with one constructor, not computed by a match. Through anything else (several constructors, a type computed by a match, a type parameter, a number's predecessor) the move is of the value whole (`jdGrain`).
  - A place the match uses whole, other than as its scrutinee, part of which an arm moves, is moved whole, as K1(g) takes it. An adversarial pass found a closed proof of `False` without this rule: `Moves84.WholeUse`/`WholeUseField`.
  - The generic path names the field wherever the grain keeps it. An unknown is η-refined (D68). A sealed program is generalised (D34) and then η-refined (new), reusing the record when a side generalised it already (D34/D37: one sealed value, one name; `recordedNeutral`), so that two sides that each project a field of it agree (`Moves84.StTrue`). A lent value is reached by ending its borrowers, as the arm that moves out of it reaches it (new). An adversarial pass found a closed proof of `False` without the last one: `Moves84.LentField`/`LentWhole`.
  - The provenance "computed by a match" is `DeclInfo.throughMatch`. The reader keeps a type-level match's one-inductive reading as a one-element `alt`. Every other decision reads `alt [d]` as `d` (copy, fields, data), and the verdict dump shows no other change.
- **Why option (i), not (ii).** Option (ii) would keep the field wherever the reading is a one-constructor type, η-refining an unknown of a stuck type with that reading's fields first.
  - It needs the fields' types at the stuck type's parameters. The reader gives readings, not values, so this is feasible only for an inductive without parameters.
  - Its η-refinements would be of unknowns whose type depends on what a world refines, the class of EtaBoom (D79). They would be scoped to their world (D79 already scopes every η-refinement), but not world-independent.
  - Option (i) costs only fields reached through a type-level match whose arms agree. No suite program has one.
- **`⊥` is a value of its own.** `Eq(A, ⊥, ⊥)` is `True` ([Eq-refl], `⊥ ≡ ⊥`), and `Eq(A, ⊥, v)` is `False` for any other `v` ([Eq-disj], now fired): an abstract value or a sealed program stands for a value, never for a moved place. Injectivity reaches `⊥` inside constructors.
  - Retired: [Conv-bot] (`Rule.ConvBot` removed, with the paper's item), the [Rewrite] guard, D68's stuck case and `convBotNote`. A new rule, [Join], is printed and fired.
  - Their tests are kept (`Equality.BotConv`, reasons asserted). `Same` is accepted, now true: both sides of both `Id`s move `x` on every path. `Boom` is rejected, as `Same`'s type at `n = 1` is `⊤`. `SelfBot`, `TrBot` and `RwBot` are rejected as ill-formed: one side of an `Id` moves `x` and the other writes it ([Id-typestate]), so `TrBotBoom` and `RwBotBoom` cite unknown constants. Their clone twins (`SelfKept`, `TrKept`, `RwKept`) are unchanged. The first version's `ExFalsoRw` is gone with `RwBot`'s hypothesis. `Equality.MovedBothSides` is accepted: both sides move `x` on every path.
- **[Id-typestate]** (RULES §4, which states it exactly). `Id(A, t, u)` is well formed only if `TS(t) = TS(u)`, read from the sides' syntax and the declared types of the bindings outside them (`Decide.typeState`), at the frame where the `Id` is formed. Its four components are sets of places at [Join]'s grain, compared as sets:
  - *moved* owned places (docs/30's G1): the join's analysis of the side as a whole;
  - *consumed* borrow bindings (G3): moved whole, passed whole to a call, bound by a `let`, returned, captured, or taken by a match (K1(b)); a reborrow `&*y` consumes nothing;
  - *re-pointed* borrow bindings (G2): assigned whole (`y := …`) and not consumed;
  - *left open* places behind a borrow (G5): moved through `*y`, not refilled.
  - The error names the component and the place, with a hint: `[Id-typestate] the left side of Id consumes the borrow x, the right side does not (pass a reborrow, &*x)`.
  - *Departure from the brief.* The brief put "assigned over" under consumption. Here it is a component of its own, since a re-pointed binding still holds a borrow afterwards, which the rest of the frame may use, and [Obs-ref] (below) needs it unconsumed. So a one-sided re-pointing is ill-formed (`Moves84.RePointOne`, `SwapB`), and a two-sided one is well formed and observed. A borrow binding assigned inside a match's arm is consumed, since the block takes it (K1(b)), on the generic call and at an instance alike.
  - *No ending* (docs/30's F4, G4). While a side runs, ending a borrow that a binding or a temporary of the frame held when the `Id` was formed is an error, whether by [Access] or by [Drop]. Assigning over the binding re-points it instead. The temporaries were added by the adversarial pass: `Put(&x, (let h : Id(Unit, (let a = clone(x); ()), ()) = refl; ()))` formed the `Id` while `&x`, an argument evaluated before it, was in flight, and its left side ended that borrow (`Moves84.KillTemp`). This flips no suite verdict.
  - *No static reading found* for the no-ending clause. Which borrows the frame holds when the `Id` is formed is the machine's loan state, and `Decide` sees only the sides and declared types. An `Id` can be formed inside a type at any point a computation reaches, so the code that made the loans is not syntax available to the decision. Making it static would mean a loan analysis of the enclosing body in `Decide`, a borrow checker, which the checker does not have. The check only rejects and is judged on the checked run (census site 28).
- **[Obs-ref]** (docs/30's F1). For each borrow binding in the re-pointed set, both observations write one fresh abstract value of its declared referent type through it after the side runs. So `Id(Unit, (y := &x; ()), (y := &z; ()))` computes to `Eq(Nat, σ3, σ0) ∧ Eq(Nat, σ1, σ3)` (`Moves84.RePoint`, rejected) and the same target on both sides to `⊤` (`SameTarget`). The set is read from syntax and declared types. Docs/30's F1 read "holds a borrow after both sides" from the run, but here it follows statically: a re-pointed binding is not consumed (the type-state), and the no-ending clause keeps its borrow from ending.
- **Why the read footprint was dropped.** Under Target C over typed contexts (docs/30's F0), a move of an owned place shows only as a rejection, so observing it is not needed for adequacy. It was also incomplete there: `Id(Unit, F(x), F(y))` was `False` although the two are equivalent in typed contexts. [Id-typestate] makes the same pair ill-formed instead, a static error with a hint, and it also closes the consumed, re-pointed and left-open channels. I looked for a reason to keep the read clause and found none. Every place a side moves is moved by the other side too (the type-state), so it is `⊥` on both. A place only read is unchanged on both. The join and the grain are still needed. A place in the footprint (written or borrowed in one arm, moved in another: `Moves84.MovesAt`, D83's `Same`) must be moved at every instance where the generic call moves it, or `⊥ = v` would be `False` at an instance and `True` at the generic call. And each run must move exactly the static set, or the resource lemma below would compare a static set that no run has. `Obs.lean` and `footprint_mono` are main's, with no `sorry`.
- **Target C and the resource lemma.**
  - *Target C* (docs/30, typed): if `L : Id(A, t, u)` (or an in-body `Id`) is used at the hole of a context `C` under `θ`, and `C[tθ]` and `C[uθ]` are both accepted, then every closed run of them has the same outcome.
  - *The resource lemma*, which makes "both accepted" the usual case. Suppose `Id(A, t, u)` is well formed and proved at the use: the hole's state is an instance of the generic call, and for a lemma that includes its loans, so no loan of a parameter's place is held at the hole except through the parameters (docs/30's separation, extended from the parameters to the context). Then, if `C[t]` is accepted, `C[u]` is accepted or rejected only because `u`'s values are not convertible with `t`'s. Every error the type-state compares is the same for both fillings: a place moved after one side and not the other, a borrow binding consumed, re-pointed elsewhere or left open by one side, and a borrow held outside the hole ended inside it. Running out of fuel or depth is "I don't know", as everywhere.
  - *Why.* Checking `C[u]` runs the same prefix as `C[t]` to the hole. By D48 a borrow is held only by a borrow binding or a temporary, never inside data, and `A` is borrow-free, so these are all the ways a side changes what the rest of the frame may use:
    - the moved places, the consumed, re-pointed and left-open bindings: equal by `TS(t) = TS(u)`, and the join makes every run move exactly that set;
    - borrows held outside the side: never ended, by the no-ending clause;
    - where each re-pointed binding points: the same, because the proof says the footprint's owners, written through by [Obs-ref], are equal;
    - the result and the footprint's contents: equal by the proof, and convertible when `refl` proves it.
  - *Counterexamples, each checked on this branch* (probes in the session's scratchpad, not committed):
    - (1) The form with equal type-states alone is false. `RePoint`'s sides both re-point `y`, to `x` and to `z`, and `□; let w = clone(z); *y := 5` is accepted after the first and rejected after the second (reading `z` ends `y`). This is why the lemma assumes a proof, and [Obs-ref] is what makes `RePoint` unprovable. The static alternative would add re-pointing targets to the type-state, where they are syntactic.
    - (2) `KillTemp`, above: fixed by extending the no-ending clause to temporaries.
    - (3) A proved `Id` that is not by conversion can still differ by a borrow error, so "only through conversion" must be read as "only because the values are not convertible". `AddOne (n : Nat) : Id(Nat, Add(n, 1), S(n))`, proved by induction, with `PickS (k) (a b : &Nat) : &Nat := match k { Z => b, S _ => a }` and the context `let k = □; let r = PickS(k, &a, &b); let w = clone(b); *r := 1`. With `S(n)` the call runs and `r` borrows `a`. With `Add(n, 1)` the call is stuck and `r` borrows both, so `clone(b)` ends `r` and `*r := 1` is an error. A stuck run's loans over-approximate, which is the same incompleteness as conversion's.
  - *Under Target C* these one-sided differences are harmless: the theorem speaks only where both fillings are accepted. The theorem is typed contextual equivalence plus this lemma.
- **The sources of `⊥`** (viability report, checked on this implementation):
  - **Definite with the join:**
    - [Read] of a place that is not a copy type;
    - a borrow variable read whole (also never observed);
    - [Closure]'s capture (K1(a));
    - a stuck block's by-move captures (the join's set, at its grain);
    - `J`'s transported term (one arm);
    - an observation's borrow ending over a moved part (D68: a move through a borrow inside a block is rejected where the block forms);
    - a callee's moves of its own parameters (popped with its frame).
  - **Maybe, but never observed:** a borrow ended early by [Access] or [Drop] (Pick: the generic call's loan graph over-approximates). Only borrow variables become `⊥` (there are no borrows in data), `Id` does not observe borrow variables, and reading one is an error on the generic call first.
  - **Never observed:**
    - a proof field invalidated by [Open] (a block's [Return] and `Id`'s [Repack] reject it; proofs are not in the footprint);
    - an erased statement's moves (its own copy);
    - a tail-position split (no join: each arm ends the function).
  - **Made definite by the lattice join** (the viability report's only "maybe and observable" candidate): a place moved before a match and refilled by only some arms. It is moved after the match; a stuck block that would refill it is rejected (it cannot borrow `⊥`).
- **Census:** sites 13 (K1's moved places are read from syntax and declared types, (a)), 14 (unchanged), 17, 19, and a new site 28 ([Id-typestate] and [Obs-ref]'s set, (a); the no-ending clause, (c)).
- **Evidence.** A verdict dump of every declaration with its message, main c364faeb against this branch:
  - **Verdicts:** 1,942 → 1,980. Two verdicts change, both to accepted: `BotConv.Same` and `Equality.MovedBothSides`.
  - **Messages:** seven rejections change their message with the same verdict:
    - `BotConv.Boom`, `SelfBot`, `TrBot` and `RwBot`, to the reasons above;
    - `ClosingOff.MoveBorrowSplit` and `MoveBorrowCode`, to the [Join] message;
    - `Functions.PassWrong`, whose message prints its restated statement.
  - **New tests:** 38, all in `Equality.Moves84`.
  - **Rejected tests:** 27 `reject def`s are restated. Twelve are the twins of restated statements, restated with them. Fifteen would otherwise have been rejected by [Id-typestate] before reaching the failure each was written for. Each is again rejected with its message on main, verbatim, `PassWrong` apart (above). The table is in `notes/d84-restatements.md`.
  - **Restated statements:** the first version's 45 are kept. Each was restated because one side consumed a place the other left, which is a type-state difference too (argued from their reasons, not re-measured). The revision restates 49 accepted statements:
    - 39 with an explicit reborrow, `AddM(&*x, 0)` for `AddM(x, 0)`: `AddMZero`, the paper's first lemma, and every lemma that runs an in-place function on a borrow parameter;
    - 6 with a clone or the same move on both sides;
    - 4 in-body statements that end a borrow before the `Id` instead of inside it (docs/30's measured F4 cost).
  - **Intent:** none was weakened, and no proof changed. A reborrow lends `*x` to the call for its duration, so the statement says what it said about `*x` and also that `x` is still a borrow afterwards. The table is in `notes/d84-restatements.md`.
  - **Case studies:** `Array`'s `GetMutSet`, `GetMutRead`, `SwapLtIsSwapS` and `SwapIsSwapS`, which `Quicksort`'s `QSCorrect` cites (its own text is unchanged), and `ArrayBench`'s `B1Join`, `ReadIsNth` and `SwapIsSwapSRefl`. `HashMap` needed nothing beyond the first version's clones.
  - **The paper's body** still states `AddMZero` as `Id(Unit, AddM(x, 0), ())` (`intro.typ`, `overview.typ` §2), and `obs.typ`'s "Unobserved moves are harmless" says the sides may consume different borrow variables. Both are now ill-formed or false, and left for the paper's owner.
  - **`Moves84`'s tests** (twin pairs, reasons asserted):
    - type-state: `FxFy`/`FxFx`, `FxFyLet`/`FxFyLocal`, `Consumes`/`Reborrows`, `LeftOpen`/`LeftOpenBoth`, `RePointOne`, `RePoint`/`SameTarget`, `SwapB`, `KillRead`/`KillReadAfter`, `KillTemp`/`KillTempBefore`;
    - the join at a direct run: `MovesAt`, `AtOneTrue`, `GenTrue`;

  - **Adversarial pass:** no closed proof of `False` remains. The first version's attacks were re-run on this one:
    - a lent place at the join;

  - **Type-state attacks**, all rejected or consistent:

- **Incomplete, fail-safe, no suite program affected:**
  - (1) `Id` observing a dependent value with a moved field hits [Repack] ("open: field y holds ⊥"), where `⊥` is now a definite state.
  - (2) A borrow variable named whole only in a statement inside an arm is dropped at the join (K1(b)), as the block takes it.
  - (3) A field reached through a type-level match is moved whole.
  - (4) A stuck block whose arm refills a place moved before it is rejected.
  - (5) The type-state compares places exactly: a side that moves `x.0` and `x.1` and one that moves `x` are not well formed together.
  - (6) A one-sided re-pointing is ill-formed even when the target is the same place the binding pointed at.

- **Amendment (lane ts-erased, 2026-11-10): a constructor's parameters move nothing.** An adversarial pass on main found that `jdMoves` and `jdAssigns` folded over a constructor's parameters (`MkNBox[n](0)`'s `n`), which the machine evaluates as types on a copy and never moves. So `Id(Unit, (let b = MkNBox[n](0); ()), (let z = n; ()))` was well formed and proved by `refl`, and the context `□; n` was accepted with the left filling and rejected with the right (`[Read] n was moved out`). That broke the resource lemma's premise that every run moves exactly the static set. Consistency was not at stake: neither path moved `n`, and inside a match the join dropped it on both. Both folds now skip the parameters. The other positions that the run evaluates as a statement or on a copy were audited and are already skipped by both the folds and the run:
  - `let x : T` annotations and ascribed types;
  - the type slots of `Eq`, `Id` and `J` (only `J`'s transported term is folded);
  - Π-types' domains and codomains (only their captures count, K1(a));
  - erased calls and erased arguments (`preFlags`);
  - values inside type arguments (probe `A6`).
  - *Tests* (`Moves84`): `CP` is rejected by [Id-typestate]; its twin `CPok` and the context `CL` are accepted; `CR` is rejected by [Read]. `C1`, the same pair with `n` in the footprint, was well formed and `False`, and is now ill-formed.
  - *Evidence:* the verdict dump against main 6b7316cc changes nothing but the six new rows (1,980 → 1,986). The probes are in `ochr/core/probes/d84/`.
- **Known gap of the resource lemma (found 2026-11-10, not fixed): [Obs-ref] cannot see where a borrow of `Unit` points.** Every content of a `Unit` owner is `()`, so the fresh value written through a re-pointed binding marks no owner:
  ```
  def RU (x1 : Unit) (x2 : Unit) (y : &Unit) : Id(Unit, (y := &x1; ()), (y := &x2; ())) := refl   -- accepted
  def RUL (x1 : Unit) (x2 : Unit) (y : &Unit) : Unit := ((y := &x1; ()); let w = clone(x2); *y := ())   -- accepted
  def RUR (x1 : Unit) (x2 : Unit) (y : &Unit) : Unit := ((y := &x2; ()); let w = clone(x2); *y := ())   -- rejected: no such place *y
  reject def RN (x1 : Nat) (x2 : Nat) (y : &Nat) : Id(Unit, (y := &x1; ()), (y := &x2; ())) := refl   -- the control at Nat: rejected
  ```
  The same holds through a type that reads as `Unit` (`y : &UU(n)`).
  - *Error-only:* the two fillings differ only by a borrow error. Consistency and Target C over typed contexts are unaffected.
  - *Fix options, to be decided:*
    - (a) Add the syntactic re-point targets to the type-state, so a re-pointing to a different target is ill-formed.
    - (b) Observe a re-pointed binding's target as a position rather than through contents.
  - Probe: `ochr/core/probes/d84/A4.lean`.

## D85. At runtime `J` is its transported term; the checker keeps its rule (2026-11-09) [lane: runtime-j]
User's go-ahead (2026-11-09) on docs/18's Q5, from test 3 of docs/23 (type safety on the higher-order slice, docs/29). The machine computes `J(A, a, b, P, h, t)` to `t` only when `a` and `b` are convertible ([J], D56) and is otherwise stuck ([J-stuck]). Conversion compares functions by their generic calls, so two functions with one graph and different code are not convertible, and a closed run of a well-typed program can stop at a `J` whose equation is true: `CastFn(IdA, IdB, ⋆, MkFnBox(0))` below, with `IdA (n) := n` and `IdB (n) := match n { Z => Z, S m => S(m) }`, both the identity on `Nat`. docs/18's type-safety target quantifies over proof parameters that are true in the set model, not proved, so it reaches such a `J` (Lean, on docs/27's toy machine: `Model.Safety.cast_fn_counterexample`, `n + 0` against `0 + n`).
Rule [J-run] (RULES §3, "Runtime: the erased program"): in the erased program, the runtime's run of a closed call of an accepted global with its proof arguments `⋆`, `J(A, a, b, P, h, t)` evaluates to `t`, unconditionally. Why: (1) it is what erasure means: `A`, `a`, `b`, `P` and `h` are statements, which the erased program does not have, so it can compare nothing, and an erased cast is the identity; (2) it is sound given membership: at a closed call whose proof parameters are true, `h` means a proof of `Eq(A, a, b)`, so the endpoints mean the same and `t ∈ P(a)` is in `P(b)` (`Model.Safety.castRT_safe`; under a false `h`, `J(Nat, 0, 1, λz. Vec(z), h, v)` would return a `Vec(0)` as a `Vec(1)`, which is why the target asks for true parameters); (3) it reads nothing, so it is not a decision read from a run; (4) it adds one clause to docs/29's erasure lemma: where the machine computes `J` both give `t`, and where it is stuck the erased run gives `t`, which is also the meaning (docs/24, C9).
