**Why it is separate from the checker's rule.** The checker's untyped runs are not the runtime: they run callee bodies, [Seal]'s re-runs of sealed programs and private copies, all at values that may be unknowns, under hypotheses that may be false. There the stuck `J` is what keeps a cast under a false hypothesis from producing a value of the wrong type (no equality reflection for open terms, D56). [J-run] in the checker's untyped runs would change what it accepts, measured by a probe (not committed) that turned the mode on in every state `checkDefs` and `globalsAfterFrom` start from, on this branch's suite: four verdicts change, the rest (1,938 rows with their reasons) are identical. `Equality.Om` goes from accepted to undecided (call depth exceeded: under `Nat → Nat = (Nat → Nat) → Nat`, the casts in `C1`'s and `C2`'s bodies compute and `f(C2(h2, f))` self-applies); `Equality.CastMatch` from accepted to rejected (`[Match] on 5, which is not a value of Bool`: under `Nat = Bool`, an untyped run inside its check computes the cast to `5`); `reject Equality.JT` and `reject RuntimeJ.CastFnRuns` become accepted (the callee's cast computes, at unknown endpoints in `JF`, at endpoints that are not convertible in `CastFn`). Nothing in consistency asks for it: consistency is semantic, and a stuck `J` means transport, which is the identity at endpoints with equal meanings. So the checker keeps [J] and [J-stuck] in every run, and [J-run] holds only in a mode the checker never enters.
Code: `MState.runtime` (default off), read only by `evalCore`'s untyped `J`, which in runtime mode fires [J-run] and evaluates `t` without running the endpoints; `Ochr/Runtime.lean`, new, with `runCall` (the runtime's entry point: the closed call `g(v̄)` of an accepted global, untyped, from a state holding only Σ, in runtime mode) and `closedCall` (the same run with the mode as a parameter, to compare with the checker's). Ochr had no program-execution entry point: every closed run in the repository (`HeapSlice`'s `realRun`, the fuzzer, the stability harness, `Units`) is the checker's own untyped run, used to test the checker, and keeps its rule. Unreachability: `Ochr/Runtime.lean` is the only code that sets `runtime`, and no module `checkProgram` depends on (`Run`, `Surface`, `Check`, `Machine`, …) imports it; every state the checker starts from (`checkDefs`, `globalsAfterFrom`) has it off; `Ochr/Consistency.lean`'s statement, about `checkProgram`, and the stability proofs build unchanged. `Rule.JRun`, printed by the appendix and fired (rule guard).
Scope: the runtime run is otherwise the machine's untyped run. Its statements still run on private copies that are discarded (D68), which leaves the state as skipping them would; skipping them outright, as docs/29's erased run does, is not done here (a statement's run could still fail or run out of fuel where the erased program would not run it). Borrow arguments are not taken (a borrow needs a cell; the caller writes `let c = v; g(&c)`).
Evidence: block `Equality.RuntimeJ` (reasons asserted): `FnBox (k : Π(n : Nat). Nat)`, `IdA`, `IdB`, `reject IdAB` (`refl` does not prove `IdA = IdB`), `CastFn` (accepted: [T-J-stuck] closes the `J` off at its generic call), `reject CastFnRuns` (the checker runs `CastFn`'s body by [J-stuck], so the cast at `IdA`/`IdB` is the stuck call and `refl` does not prove it is `MkFnBox(0)`) and its twin `CastFnSame` (at convertible endpoints [J] computes). Runtime `#guard`s: `runCall` of `CastFn(IdA, IdB, ⋆, MkFnBox(0))` returns `MkFnBox(0)`; the same closed call under the checker's rule (`closedCall … false`) closes off, its result the sealed program `⌈CastFn(…)⌉`; at `IdA`/`IdA` both modes return `MkFnBox(0)`. A before/after dump of every row with its reason: all 1,935 earlier rows identical; the 7 new rows are `RuntimeJ`'s. Total 1,935 → 1,942. The rule guard reports the same issues as on main (none new: [J-run] is printed, has its `Rule`, is fired, and the appendix's pointer `runCall (Runtime.lean)` resolves). Paper: the appendix's [J-run] beside [J] and [J-stuck], with its prose; the body is unchanged and the Conclusion still ends on page 25.

## D86. A Π-type term has its declared sort in every reading (2026-11-10) [lane: pisort]
Finding (`prog-decisions`, instantiating the decisions interface on branch `spine-prog-decisions`: `DecisionsSanity.piSort_nested`; widened by this lane). **Soundness-relevant: wrong acceptances under D66, borrows of proofs and of `Type₁` functions.** The reader of declared types answered `Type₀` for every Π-type term it read without `chk`, the mode in which every decision reads it. `typePos` returned `1` without `chk`, and `declOf`'s `.pi` clause takes a Π-type's sort from its components' `typePos`, so it answered `.sort 1` whatever the components were. So `Π(x : Nat). Prop`, which is in `Type₁`, and `Π(y : Nat). Eq(Nat, y, y)`, which is a proposition, both read as `Type₀`. The shortcut dates from the erasure pre-pass (4a4e1a05), when no reader used the level. The census's phase 2 made `sortOf` of a Π-type value syntactic, reading each component with `declSort`, and so through the shortcut. The answer is right for every Π-type term the suite has in a domain or codomain, all of which are over and into `Type₀`. That is why nothing noticed. On main ebb398b8 (block `PiSorts` below, run there; 7ae8cf00 changes only docs):
- **Wrong acceptances (D66).** A function's type is a Π-type value built from its declaration: a global's by [Def], a closure's from its λ. Neither passes the D55 assertion (below), and `isBorrowable` reads its sort through `sortOf`. These were accepted:
  - `BorrowG`: `let r = g; let b = &r` with `g (f : Π(x : Nat). Prop) : Nat`, a function in `Type₁`;
  - `BorrowQ`: the same with `q (x : Nat) : (Π(y : Nat). Eq(Nat, y, y))`, which is a proof;
  - the closures `BorrowPredClo` (in `Type₁`), `BorrowCurriedClo` (a proof) and `BorrowFamId`, `λ(T : Π(x : Nat). Type) (m : Nat) (a : T(m)) : T(m) => a`. That is an identity over type families read as being in `Type₀`, the impredicative `Type₀` that D48 (1) and D66 exclude;
  - `StuckG`, whose stuck block borrows a place of `g`'s type (`closeBlock`'s D66 check).
  The same types written through `Pow (X : Type) : Type₁ := Π(a : X). Prop` were rejected: `BorrowG2`, `StuckG2`, and `BorrowTypes.BorrowProof` for a proof. No proof of `False` is known. What broke is [T-Borrow]'s premise, borrows only of `Type₀`, on which the set model relies.
- **True programs rejected INTERNAL (fail-safe).**
  - [D55]'s assertion compares a written Π-type's sort by running its components (`Machine.piSort`) with the declared one. It rejected `Pred1 : Type₁ := Π(f : Π(x : Nat). Prop). Nat`, `Curried : Prop := Π(x : Nat). Π(y : Nat). Eq(Nat, y, y)` and the nested variants. So no curried proposition could be written as a type.
  - The [pre-pass] assertion rejected `LetProp` and `LetCod`. There `let A = (Π(y : Nat). ⊤)` recorded `A`'s declared type as `Type₀`, so neither `p : A` nor a closure `λ(x : &Nat) : A => …` read as a proof. `typeDecl` reads a variable as a proposition only when its declared type is `Prop`.
  - The false twins of these (`Pred2`, `CurriedT`, `LetPropWrites`, …) were rejected with the same INTERNAL messages, not with their reasons.
- **The decisions.** The misreading matters only to a reader that tells sorts apart.
  - Not affected:
    - `preFlags` does not tell sorts apart: a term whose declared type is any sort is a type, erased if it is a call.
    - `fnClass`, `paramFlags`, `leafProof`, `typeClass`, `isPropV`, `declKind` ([Close]'s row) and `jErased` read a Π-type term structurally, by `typeDecl`'s `.pi` clause (`.pi r` is a proof iff `r` is), never by its sort. So on main a closure into a curried proposition was already a proof, and its call was erased (`CurriedErased`, `CurriedRuns`).
  - Affected:
    - `typeDecl`'s fallback (`Prop` reads as a proposition), reached through a binding whose declared type was a Π-type term's reading: an unannotated `let`, or a captured Π-type value's `valueDecl`. That is the [pre-pass] case.
    - `sortOf`'s consumers: `isBorrowable`, the field check in `Check` (fields are first-order, D36, so no Π-type reaches it), and the sort that types a type value (`valType?`, the untyped `.pi`, `valueDecl`). That is the D66 case.
- **Untyped runs.** The misreading was a constant, read from syntax alone. So an instance read what the definition's generic, typed, run read, and both assertions caught it there. The site without an assertion, a function's type, is already wrong in typed runs (above). No wrong decision was found that only an untyped run makes.

Fix: `typePos` reads the declared sort in both modes, and `chk` adds only D55's errors (a type position whose declared type is not a sort, or has none). So `declOf`'s answer no longer depends on `chk`. Without `chk`, a component that is not a sort still reads `1`, as before. `sortOf` (through `declSort`), the pre-pass, a `let`'s binding and a captured type's reading then read a Π-type term's sort as typing computes it. They still read only syntax and declared types. RULES P2 already says this ("the largest of the declared sorts of its parameter types and its codomain"), so no rule changes and the paper is unchanged.

Alternative considered: a helper `piDeclSort` for `sortOf` and `declOf`'s `.pi` clause only, with `typePos` left answering `1` without `chk`. That saves the `declOf` per binder in the telescopes that drop the level. But it keeps `telescope`'s third result wrong without `chk`, a trap for the next reader that uses it. And `declSort` fails on a component that is not a sort, which `declOf` without `chk` never did. The chosen fix adds no failure of its own. Its one new exposure is reading a component's declared type (`declOf`), which the same telescopes' `typeDecl` already reads for most terms. Cost, not measured cleanly (the two builds ran beside other jobs, on different cores): in the full build `HashMap` took 59 s on main and 63 s with the fix, `Editor` 66 s and 92 s. A clean measurement is still to do.

Evidence: block `Universes.PiSorts`, 43 rows, reasons asserted:
- universes: `Pred1` accepted; `Pred2` (`: Type₂`) and `Pred0` rejected with the universe mismatch (not cumulative), twin `Fn0`;
- nested: `PredPred1`/`PredPred0`/`FnFn0`, `UnivUniv2`/`UnivUniv1`;
- propositions: `AllPred`/`AllPred1`, `AllPredPred`, `Curried`/`CurriedT`, `CurriedProp`;
- borrows: `BorrowG`, `BorrowG2`, `BorrowQ`, `BorrowPredClo`, `BorrowFamId`, `BorrowCurriedClo`, `RefPredFn`, `StuckG` and `StuckG2` rejected with D66's reason, twins `BorrowH`, `BorrowK`, `BorrowFnClo`, `BorrowFamApp`, `BorrowCurriedDataClo`, `RefFnFn`, `StuckH`;
- proofs: `LetProp`/`LetPropWrites`, `LetCod`/`LetCodRuns`, `CurriedErased`/`CurriedRuns`.

On main the same block gives:
- 6 wrong acceptances: `BorrowG`, `BorrowQ`, `BorrowPredClo`, `BorrowFamId`, `BorrowCurriedClo`, `StuckG`;
- 9 true programs rejected INTERNAL: `Pred1`, `PredPred1`, `UnivUniv2`, `AllPred`, `AllPredPred`, `Curried`, `CurriedProp`, `LetProp`, `LetCod`;
- 9 rejections that were INTERNAL and now give their reasons: `Pred2`, `Pred0`, `PredPred0`, `UnivUniv1`, `AllPred1`, `CurriedT`, `RefPredFn`, `LetPropWrites`, `LetCodRuns`;
- the rest the same.

A before/after dump of every row with its reason (main 7ae8cf00 against the fix): all 1,980 earlier rows identical, reasons included; the 43 new rows are `PiSorts`', all as expected; no row anywhere has an INTERNAL message. Rebased onto acc144e6 (D84 amendment, `jdMoves`' constructor clause): the full build is green, and a before/after dump of the blocks that commit touches and of those near this fix (`Moves`, `Equality`, `Moves84` with its 6 new rows, `Universes`, `Sorts`, `BorrowTypes`, `FnBorrows`) is identical outside `PiSorts`, whose 43 rows are as above. So no existing verdict changed: the suite has no Π-type term over or into anything but `Type₀`. Total 1,986 → 2,029 (1,980 → 2,023 on 7ae8cf00). Census: site 25 and a D86 section.

## D87. A stuck block's arm may not return a borrow of a place the block takes by move (2026-11-13) [lane: joinmv]
Found by the runtime-j lane (probe `ochr/core/probes/runtime/JoinHitRepro.lean`, Target B). Consider `JoinMv (n p q : Nat) : Nat := (let r = match n { Z => (let z = p; &q), S _ => &p }; *r := 1; q)`.
- *At the generic call* the non-tail match closes off as a block that takes `p` by move, since arm `Z` moves it (K1, D84's join). Arm `S` returns `&p`, a borrow of the block's own by-value parameter. The block's [Return] checked only that its result and lent places were whole (D76), not D65's in-flight rule, so it accepted the borrow.
- *At a known constructor* the direct run's [Join] drops `p` at the end of arm `S` while the arm's result borrows it, which is an error.
- So `JoinMv` was accepted, while `AtS : Nat := JoinMv(1, 0, 0)` was rejected, and the closed run of the accepted definition at `n = 1` errs. That breaks the corollary that an accepted definition means an element of its type (`ochr/docs/18`), and the lemma that the derivation is at least as strict as the run (docs/29).

**Rule.** At each arm's end, a stuck block's [Return] also rejects an arm whose result borrows a place the block takes by move (`closeBlock`). The check is `dropAtJoin`'s own: a live loan in the place whose borrow is held by a temporary, the arm's result. It skips copies, proofs, places already moved and borrow variables, whose reborrows land in the owner (D67). Its message is the direct path's, "[Join] p is moved by another arm of the match, so it is dropped at the end of this one, while the arm's result borrows it", so the two paths reject alike. It is a real function's return check (D65) applied to the block's by-value parameters. RULES §3 (stuck blocks) and the paper appendix ([Block]) say so.

**Evidence.** A full lake build and a before/after dump of every row with its reason, main f84669ea against this change:
- **New tests** (`Equality.Moves84`): `JoinMv` is rejected with the message above. Its twin `JoinLent` (arm `Z` clones `p`, so the block takes `p` by `&`) is accepted.
- **One suite program changes verdict:** `ClosingOff.MoveBorrowBlk`, accepted before and rejected now with the [Join] message. It is the hole's exact shape: its `Id`'s side is `match n2 { Z => (*x0 := n1; &*x0), S _ => &n1 }`. Its comment called it a fail-safe incompleteness ("every instance is fine"), which stopped being true with D84, where the direct run at `n2 = 1` drops `n1` under the borrow. It is now a `reject def`.
- **Its dependant** `MoveBorrowSplit` now cites an unknown constant. `MoveBorrowCode` keeps its verdict and message.
- **The probe** asserts the new verdicts. It is silent: `JoinMv` is rejected with the message, and `JoinLent`'s closed runs return at `n = 0` and `n = 1`, directly, through a caller, and in the checker's untyped run.

## D88. A constructor value built by an untyped run records its inferred parameters (2026-11-13) [lane: ctor-params]
Decided by the lead, as step 1 of (B): make values self-describing, for the consistency route.
- **Before.** A constructor term whose parameters are not written (`Some(n)`, `Cons(h, t)`) was given them by the typed check, which inferred them from the fields' types or the expected type, but only in the type it returned. An untyped run (a called body, a sealed program's re-run, the runtime) inferred them only when its context read the type (D82), and even then it kept them in the type, not the value. Its value was `Some(n)` with no parameters, which means different things at different types. Constructor values recorded their parameters (D49) only when they were typed or written.
- **Rule.** In an untyped run, a constructor whose parameters are not written reads its fields' types (D82's inference, now always on there) and records the parameters in the value when the fields determine them all (`Machine.evalCtor`). Where they do not, the value records only the parameters written, as before.

**Measured** over the full verdict dump (main ce6b5e97), counting every untyped build of a parameterised inductive's constructor with no parameters written:
- 17,542 such builds. Before, 17,513 of them had no hint and inferred nothing.
- With the rule, 17,263 (98.4%) record their parameters. By constructor: SliceOf.MkSlice 8,859, Cell.MkC 7,002, ArrayOf.MkArray 569, Opt.Some 544, List.Cons 147, Pair.Mk 141, Box.MkBox 1.
- Statically, 143 constructor terms in the suite omit their parameters, against 49 that write them.
- Verdicts: 0 changes. Messages: 0 changes. The 2,031 earlier rows are identical with their reasons; the 3 new rows are `OptionRuns`' tests.
- Cost: the full dump took 58–62 s on main and 62–64 s with the rule, under 10% more.

**The residual.** These are the classes whose parameters only the context gives, as typed checking gets them from the expected type:
- `Dec.Yes`/`Dec.No`, whose fields are proofs, which untyped runs never evaluate: 269 builds. `Yes(refl)` would give only `P := True` anyway. There are 49 source sites, mostly `Word`'s `LeDec`, which `Array` and `Quicksort` use.
- Phantom parameters: `Functions.Tag` and `Fording.Tag`, 10 builds.
- Constructors with no fields: `None`, `Nil`, `GBucket`'s empty case. No untyped run in the suite builds one bare, but programs can.
- Dependent pairs whose function-valued parameter the field types do not determine: `Sig`, `DP`, `DQ`.

Option (2a), giving untyped runs the typed run's hints, is the follow-up prototype.

**Tests** (`Option.lean`, `OptionRuns`):
- The checker's untyped run and the runtime both build `WrapSome(0)` as `Some[Nat](0)`, where main built `Some(0)` with no parameters.
- `WrittenNone`'s `None[Nat]` records `Nat` as before.
- Its twin `BareNone`, a bare `None`, records nothing: the residual, pinned by a `#guard`.

## D89. An untyped run gives a constructor the type its context declares (2026-11-13) [lane: ctx-hints]
Decided by the lead as step (2a) of (B), after D88. This closes D88's residual, so that every constructor value an accepted program builds records its parameters.
- **Rule.** An untyped run gives a constructor term the hint that typing gives it, where the constructor needs one: its parameters are not written, and its fields do not determine them (`ctorNeedsCtx`).
  - That covers a constructor with no fields, a phantom parameter, only proof fields (`Dec`'s `Yes(h : P)`), and a parameter that only a type function reads (`DP`'s `y : G(n, x)`).
  - A parameter is determined only where it appears as a field's type itself or as an argument of an inductive type former (`Term.rigidVars`).
  - A constructor also needs one when one of its fields does (`Some(None)`).
- **Where the hint comes from.** Whether a term needs one is read from syntax and declarations (`needsCtx`, along the tail spine). It comes from:
  - a called body's declared codomain at the arguments (`declCallType`), for its tail, passed through `let`, `;` and the arms of a match that runs directly (`runBodyCore`, and blocks and sealed re-runs through it);
  - a `let` annotation or ascription (`.ascribe`);
  - a call's parameter type at the earlier arguments (`argHint`);
  - an enclosing constructor's field type (`fieldTypeAt`).
- **Typed runs are unchanged.** The forwarding through `let`, `;` and direct arms happens in untyped runs only. Eval's interface is unchanged: the existing optional hint carries it.

**Measured** over the full verdict dump (main 42fd5a7b, D88):
- Untyped builds of a parameterised inductive's constructor with no parameters written: 17,543. All of them now record their parameters, 15,637 through a hint. Under D88 alone, 279 did not: 269 for `Dec.Yes`/`No` and 10 for `Tag.MkTag`.
- Verdicts: 0 changes. Messages: 0 changes. The 2,034 earlier rows are identical with their reasons; the 13 new rows are `OptionRuns`' tests.
- Cost: the full dump takes 72.5 s, against 61 s under D88 (two alternating runs each), 19% more.
- **Remainder:** none found among positions typing accepts. A bare constructor in a position where typing has no hint either (an arm of a non-tail match, `J`'s transported term) is rejected by typing ("cannot infer the parameter …"), so it never runs.

**Tests** (`Option.lean`, `OptionRuns`; untyped runs, and the runtime for two of them):
- A bare `None` records `Nat` as a body's tail, a call argument, a `let` annotation, a closure's tail and a tail arm. `BareNone` recorded nothing under D88 alone.
- In `Some(None)` both constructors record their parameters.
- A phantom parameter (`TagO`), a parameter only a proof field has (`SigO`), and one only a type function reads (`DPO`) are each recorded.


## D90. When a borrow ends is a decision every run shares (user, 2026-11-19) [lane: static-ends]
Decision (Charlie, after the viability prototype on branch `static-ends`; the orchestrator's go-ahead the same day). D84 made *what is moved* a decision read from syntax and declared types; this does the same for *when a borrow ends*, so a definition's generic call and every instance of it have the same borrows live, lending the same places, at every point. Rust's reading: a returned reference borrows every input it could come from, whatever the runtime values. Being as precise as Rust is the aim, not more; two known gaps leave Ochr less precise (below).

- **What was accepted and wrong.** The checker checks a function once, at its generic call, and runs its body again at every instance (a closed call, a callee's run, a sealed program's re-run). The machine tracked borrows as it ran, so each run found out afresh when a borrow ended, and the generic call, which over-approximates, and an instance, which is precise, disagreed. On main (a869a275), each of these definitions was accepted and its closed call rejected (tests `ReturnedBorrows.StaticEnds`/`StaticEndsRuns`, where all are now accepted with their calls; probes `ochr/core/probes/static-ends/`):
  - `EI`: `let r = Pick(n, &a, &b); let a1 = a; let T = Id(Unit, (let z = clone(b); ()), ()); ()`. At an unknown `n`, reading `a` ends `r` (`a`'s fill holds `r`'s hole), so the `Id`'s side ends nothing. At `n = 1`, `r` borrows `b` alone, reading `a` ends nothing, and the side's `clone(b)` ends `r`: [Id-typestate]'s no-ending clause rejects `EI(1, 2, 3)`.
  - `EJ`: the same set-up with pairs, then `let k = S(Z); match k { Z => (let z = (*r).0; Z), S m => Z }; let bb = b; bb`. The join drops `(*r).0`, which the unrun arm moves: nothing at the generic call, where `r` is dead, and `b.0` through the live `r` at `n = 1`, after which reading `b` ends `r` with its content partly moved ([D53]). `EJ2`: the arm that runs ends `r` (it reads `a`) before the join drops through it.
  - `EIB`, `EJB`: the same two faults through a stuck block. `let r = &a; match k { Z => (let z = clone(a); ()), S _ => () }; …` at an unknown `k` closes off as a block that reads `a` in place, ending `r`; at `k = 1` the arm that runs does not read `a`.
  - `GJI`, `EJJ`: the same through `J`. At an unknown `m`, `J(Nat, m, Z, MN, h, match k {…})` closes off by [T-J-stuck] as a block whose captures read `a`; at `m = 0` `J` computes and its transported term's arm does not read it.
  - `G1`, a third fault, which is not an over-approximation: `let r = PickP(n, &a, &b); match k { Z => (let z = a.0; ()), S _ => () }; *r := (Z, Z); ()`. The stuck block's by-move capture of `a.0` (and [Join]'s drop of it, `movedPlace`) generalised `a`'s content, a sealed fill holding `r`'s hole, without ending the loans inside it, so the hole was lost and `r` stayed live at the generic call, while every instance ended it. `G1(0, 1, (1, 2), (3, 4))` was rejected.
  None of these is known to give a closed proof of `False`. Each breaks the corollary that an accepted definition denotes an element of its type (`ochr/docs/18`) and the lemma that the derivation is at least as strict as the run (docs/29), as D87's did.
- **Root cause.** Two mechanisms made the generic call end borrows that an instance does not:
  - (M1) *a stuck call's fills*: [Close]'s `&T` row puts the result's hole in the fill of every place a borrow argument lends, while a call that runs leaves its loan only where the result points (the callee's join or frame pop ends the other borrow arguments);
  - (M2) *a stuck block's captures*: the block evaluates its arguments (`&q`, `q` by move, `q` read in place), each an [Access], for every place any arm uses (statements, nested arms and the scrutinee, read whole, included), while the direct run touches only the running arm's places. `J`'s [T-J-stuck] block is the same mechanism.
  Over-approximating is safe for a monotone consumer (a use of a borrow the stricter path ended is an error there first). The faults came from two consumers that are not monotone: [Id-typestate]'s no-ending clause (`EI`) and [Join]'s drops through a borrow (`EJ`). The census (below) found no third mechanism.
- **Census** (`notes`-free; the lane's report, condensed). For each site where a borrow is ended or its loans are placed: can the outcome differ between the generic call and an instance at one program point, and through what?
  - [Access] from [Read], [Borrow], [Assign] (the owned part), `clone`, `peek`: yes, through M1 (which places hold the hole) and M2 (the block's argument evaluation).
  - [Access] for matching (`accessNeutralHead`): yes, M1 (a lender holds a fill, a neutral head, at the generic call and a constructor at an instance).
  - [Close]'s fills: M1 itself. `closeBlock`'s captures: M2 itself. `J`'s block: M2.
  - `movedPlace` (the join's drops, K1(a) and (d)): its `.loan` case is M1; its `.sealed` case was the third fault (`G1`); its fallback that re-roots `(*r).0` to a dead `r` consumes M1/M2 (`EJ`).
  - `dropAtJoin` and the join's drop set: static (D84), but what a drop through a borrow reaches depends on that borrow's liveness (`EJ`, `EJB`).
  - Frame pop, `let` scope ends, `dropValue`, D65's end-or-error: static given a binding's kind; which lenders a callee's pop frees is M1 seen from inside; D65's in-flight error is monotone (the generic call is stricter).
  - Re-pointing (`assignPlace`): static, unless the old borrow was ended early (M1/M2).
  - `endBorrowArgs` (erased and proof calls), [Close]'s precondition, `wholeBorrowArgs`: static.
  - Private copies: discarded; inside them [Id-typestate]'s no-ending clause reads the run's live borrows (`EI`, `EIB`, `GJI`).
  - Footprint owners: consume M1 (census site 14: the generic call's footprint contained an instance's).
  - Refinement and re-normalisation of fills: a fill re-run in a split world dropped its hole where the borrow does not point, so the split world agreed with the instance, and the generic call's lending was lost there (`Census3.SplitKeeps`, accepted on main, rejected by Rust).
  - `endAll`, `obsResult`: the whole state, discarded. `lentUnknown`, `lentProofAssign`: read loans only, consumers of M1.
  - Tail matches: the generic call splits them, never closes them off, so an instance's direct arm agreed with the split arm already; this is why the entry below excludes them.
  - A call that runs at the generic call runs at every instance (refinement only adds constructors; `J` and `Eq` decide by conversion, which refinement preserves), so no mechanism runs the other way.
  - Whether a block's captures are static: occurrence kinds, maximal prefixes, the moved set and its grain (D84), copy-ness (D80) and the proof flag are read from syntax and declared types; `movedPlace`'s re-rooting depends on which borrow variables are dead and which places moved, which the rules below make the same on every path (by induction along the run), and K1(g) splits only one-constructor values (the grain), whose constructor is the type's.
- **The rules as built** (`Machine.lean`; RULES §2, §3, §4, §6):
  - *Fills and the lent mark.* A place lent to a borrow `k` that a call returned holds a *fill over `k`*, in one of two forms: a sealed program whose text holds `loan_k` ([Close]'s fill, an unknown function of the hole: main's representation, unchanged), or `lent_k v` (`Value.gloan`), a known function of the hole, `v` the content: `loan_k` itself at the place `k` points into (the identity, `lent_k loan_k`), the content with the hole lower down where `k` points into a part of it, or the content with no hole. `lentTo` marks a value only where it is not already a fill over that borrow at its top (`carriesHole`), so the generic call's state outside split worlds is main's, and the marks appear where the fill's function is known: at instances, and in split worlds after a fill is re-run. The generic call and an instance differ in the values of fills, never in which places are fills over which borrows. The identity stays marked, not collapsed to a bare `loan_k`, so that when `k` ends with a reborrow of a part of `*k` alive, the whole place stays lent to the reborrow (the mark is handed on), as the generic call's sealed fill does; collapsed, only the reborrowed part would hold the reborrow's loan, and (A2) below would hold only up to that part. (The prototype first stacked a mark on every fill, sealed ones included; the orchestrator's steer, at no cost in verdicts, made the mark the known form of fill instead.)
  - *[Access].* A mark counts as a loan of its label where it stands (`firstLiveLoanOnPath`; `Value.loans` and `ownedLoans`), so reading, borrowing, assigning or matching the place, a part of it or a place containing it ends `k`. The holes of a sealed program on the path to `p` (`p` below it, at an unknown position inside it) are on the path, as matching already said (D29): without that, the unified form misaligned (`Sealed.SF`, `SF0`: a known-constructor match's entry over `a.0`, with `a` holding a bare fill, ended nothing at the generic call and ended `r` at `n = 0`).
  - *[End k].* Substitutes `k`'s final content `c` for `loan_k`, and gives each `lent_k w` to the live borrows whose loans `c` holds (`LendOp.ended`), or leaves `w`. A reborrow of `*k` that outlives `k` then keeps every place `k` was lent lent to it, as the generic call's fills then hold its loan (`Census2.G8`, `G8K`; without the transfer, `a` was freed at `n = 1` and not at an unknown `n`).
  - *[Call] (rule 1).* A call whose declared result is a borrow (its [Close] row, `declKind`) lends that borrow every place its borrow arguments lend, whether it runs or closes off (`callFn`). Before the call, every fill over a borrow argument, and every loan of one, is marked for a label reserved then; no borrow holds it while the call runs, and the callee cannot reach its caller's places ([Frame] F4), so the marks are inert there. Afterwards the label becomes the result's (`lentTo` collapses it into a hole or a sealed fill over the result), or the marks go if the result is not a borrow. [Close] removes the marks of each argument it consumes (`LendOp.ended l []`). A stuck block whose match returns a borrow is such a call.
  - *`movedPlace`.* One case for both forms (`fillHoles`): a place holding a fill over a live borrow is reached as the arm that moves out of it reaches it, by ending the borrow first. This is the fix of `G1`.
  - *[Repack], [Open].* `lentUnknown` reads a sealed fill whole (as before) and `lent_k v` as `v`, so a hole inside `v` is an unknown of its type (as before): a mark says which borrow may point at the place, not that its content is gone. Making a marked value an unknown changed `LentIndex.DirectFalse`'s message in the prototype, and was reverted. `hasDependent`, `ctorFields`, `botPlaces` and `loanPath` look through a mark.
  - *`Eq` and conversion* never meet a mark: every value they compare was read (an [Access] ended the marked borrow), observed (an observation ends every borrow) or is a type (formed on a copy, where reading ends every loan). `Value.beq` compares marks structurally, as loans, should one ever reach it.
  - *[Seal].* A sealed program over live holes normalises to a fill over the same holes, `lentTo holes v` (`nfSealed`): the hole where the borrow points, the value marked otherwise. So a fill re-run under a refinement keeps its place lent (`Census3.SplitKeeps`, `SplitKeeps0`: rejected, as by Rust). Since D97 such a program is not run at all: it stays sealed, a fill over the same holes, until its last hole is filled.
  - *The entry (rule 2).* A match not in tail position, run on a known constructor, and `J`'s transported term wherever `J` computes ([J], and [J-run] at runtime), first access every place the stuck block they would close off as captures (`blockEntry`: `blockCaptures` in the current state, accessed in the block's order, oldest binding first, `entryAccess`), ending their borrowers, whichever arm runs (`joinEnds`). If the match's declared type is a borrow, its result is lent what the block's [Close] would lend it: the places the block takes by `&` (marked after the arm) and the places its by-move borrow variables lend (marked for a reserved label before the arm, as rule 1 does). `closeBlock` asserts INTERNAL that the declared type reads as a borrow exactly when the block's [Close] row is the borrow row.
  - *Tail position in every run.* The generic call splits a tail match and never closes it off, so a tail match has no entry. Untyped runs did not know tail position; they now carry it as `checkTail` recurses (`MState.tailNext`/`curTail`: set by `runBody`, passed through a `let`'s body, a sequence's tail, the arms of a tail match and the one arm of a match on a proof). Applying the entry to tail matches would reject what Rust accepts and the generic call accepts (`StaticEnds.G4`: `let r = &a; match k { Z => (a := 1; ()), S _ => (*r := 2; ()) }` in tail position); missing a tail form makes an instance end what the generic call's split did not (`Census2.PT`, the one arm of a match on a proof).
  - *A block accesses all its captures before it takes any* (`closeBlock`, `entryAccess`). Without it the prototype introduced a fault: the block took a read-only capture `*b` before a later capture's access ended `b`, so its arm read a copy, while the direct run's arm reads `*b` after the entry (`StaticEnds.EK`, below: accepted, `EK(1, 0)` rejected; both accepted on main, which had no entry).
- **The one verdict that changed.** `ClosingOff.Naturality.PickEarly0` (`let n = 0; let r = Pick(clone(n), &a, &b); let z = b; match n { Z => *r := 5, S _ => () }`): accepted → rejected ("no such place *r"). `b` is lent to `r` though `Pick(0, …)` points into `a`, so reading `b` ends `r`, as at an unknown `n`. Rust rejects the analogue. Its block's doc comment ("each concrete `n` is fine", the paper's "naturality up to resolution") is rewritten.
- **Evidence.**
  - Full build: every verdict as expected, count 2,047 → 2,081 (34 new rows: `StaticEnds` 19, `StaticEndsRuns` 15; `PickEarly0`'s reason asserted with `PickEarly`'s).
  - A dump of every row with its reason (`run` over `Registry.programs ++ caseStudies`), a869a275 against this change: all 2,047 earlier rows are identical, reasons included, except `PickEarly0` (above); the 34 new rows are `StaticEnds`' and `StaticEndsRuns`', each as expected.
  - The faults on main: `EI`, `EJ`, `EJ2`, `EIB`, `EJB`, `GJI`, `EJJ` and `G1` accepted with their closed calls rejected; newly rejected and also rejected by Rust: `PickZ` (a computed `Pick(Z, &a, &b)` whose result is used after `b` is read), `G1`, `G3K`, `G8K`, `NestK`, `SplitKeeps`, `SplitKeeps0`, `SF`, `SF0` (probes and tests).
  - The fuzzer (`lake exe fuzz`) does not build on main or here: its library block `Ochr/Fuzz/Lib.lean` fails D84's [Id-typestate] (`AddMZeroL` consumes `x` on one side). Not repaired.
  - Build time unchanged (about 2 min 45 s on 15 cores for the full build).
- **The alignment property** (for the consistency proof, replacing its "early ends"). Fix an accepted global `f`, its generic run `G` (`checkFix`), and an instance `I`: an untyped run of `f`'s body at an instance θ of the generic call's unknowns, its borrow parameters lending the places that correspond to `G`'s frame-0 cells.
  - *Corresponding points:* the same occurrence of a sub-term of `f`'s body, outside callee frames, where at each tail match `I` crosses `G` is in the split world of `I`'s constructor; after a non-tail match or a `J` that `G` closes off, `I`'s state after the join corresponds to `G`'s after the block call; inside such a match's arm, `I`'s state corresponds to `G`'s check of that arm; after a call that `G` closes off and `I` runs, the states after the call correspond.
  - *The relation* `Ω_G ≈_β Ω_I`, for a bijection β between the borrow labels live in each:
    - (A1) *Holders:* `ℓ` is held at the same binding or temporary of the same frame as `βℓ` (identifying `G`'s frame-0 cells with the places `I`'s borrow arguments lend).
    - (A2) *Lending:* each live `ℓ` is either *returned* (a call's result, or a reborrow a returned borrow's lending was handed to at its [End]): it is a fill over `ℓ` at the same set of places in both, in either form, and every occurrence of `ℓ` lies inside one of its fills; or *plain*: it has no fills and exactly one loan, at the same place in both.
    - (A3) *Moves:* the same places hold `⊥`, at the same grain (D84), borrow variables included.
    - (A4) *Values:* the existing θ-image relation (`I`'s values are `G`'s under θ, normalised); a fill's form may differ (a sealed program in `G`, a mark in `I`).
  - *Claim:* at every corresponding pair of points, inside the arms of the blocks `G` closes off included, `Ω_G ≈_β Ω_I`, and β extends along the run (fresh labels at the same steps). (As first landed, the claim excepted the arms of such blocks: `G` checked an arm before the match's entry and `I` ran it after, so inside the arm `Ω_G` held the borrows `E` that the entry ended in `I`, and `G`'s check of the arm was argued never to use them. D91 checks the arms after the entry and removes the exception.)
  - *Consequences* (what the proof consumes instead of early ends): [Access] of any place ends corresponding sets, to its fixpoint; [Drop] ends or rejects alike (D65); owners, and so footprints, are equal (census site 14's containment becomes equality); [Id-typestate]'s no-ending clause fires on both or neither; the join's drops through a borrow reach the same places. No borrow ends earlier in `G` than in `I`.
  - *The argument, rule by rule* (induction on `I`'s run, with `G` at the corresponding point):
    - [Borrow]: the same plain loan at the same place, under the same holder.
    - [Access]: by (A2), every occurrence of a returned label lies inside one of its fills, so the labels whose sites overlap `p` (on its path, at it, inside it; a sealed fill's holes count at its place) are the same. [End] substitutes the content under the fills (into sealed fills in `G`, at the one hole, inside its mark, in `I`) and hands the marks to the content's live labels, which correspond, since the content sits at the holder (A1). Each step removes a label and only hands marks to labels inside the ended content, so the fixpoint does not depend on the order.
    - A call whose result is not a borrow: every borrow argument is consumed on both paths: [Close]'s fills replace their loans and drop their marks in `G`; in `I` the callee's join or frame pop ends them, and by F4 it reaches no other caller place.
    - A call whose result is a borrow: both mark every argument's lending sites for the reserved label. `G`'s [Close] puts the holes in those places' fills; `I`'s run ends the arguments it does not return, substituting under the marks. Both rename the label to the result's, held by the same temporary.
    - A non-tail match and `J`: `G` on an unknown scrutinee runs the block: its captures (`blockCaptures`, from syntax and declared types) and `entryAccess` first, then the check of each arm from the state after the entry (D91), then its arguments, then the block call, a borrow-returning one being a rule-1 call. `I` (and `G` on a known constructor) runs `blockEntry` (the same `blockCaptures` in corresponding states, in the same order), the arm, D84's join (the same static set as the block's by-move captures) and, for a borrow-typed match, marks the same sites. The arm interior: `G` checks each arm, and `I` runs the one it takes, from corresponding states after the entry, so the relation holds inside the arm by the same induction.
    - A tail match: `G` splits and `I` runs the arm; neither has an entry, and both apply the join's drops (`joinCont`, `joinDrop`).
    - Refinement and re-normalisation (`G` only): a fill keeps its holes ([Seal]); a re-run fill may change form, never place.
    - Private copies and [Seal]'s runs: the same rules on a discarded state; outer labels are inert in a seal run.
  - *Where it can fail* (obligations of the proof, and invariants the code must keep):
    1. `blockCaptures`' value reads must give the same places in corresponding states: `movedPlace`'s η and generalisation targets (static by the grain), its re-rooting through a dead borrow (by A1, A3), its access of a lent place (by the access case), K1(g)'s split of a one-constructor value, and `isBorrowVar`'s value disjunct (redundant beside the declared type).
    2. The tail flag must follow `checkTail`'s recursion exactly; a gap either way gives an accepted definition whose closed call fails.
    3. Every use of a borrow in an arm is a syntactic occurrence that K1 captures (statements, closures and pattern variables included). Obligation 4 relies on it; the arm interior did too until D91.
    4. The reserved labels must stay unreachable while inert: by F4 for calls; for rule 2's by-move borrow variables, by obligation 3 (an arm that touches a place such a variable lends makes the block's argument read `⊥`).
    5. Every borrow disappears through [End] or [Close], which remove its marks; another way would leave dead marks, harmless to liveness but visible to `Eq` and conversion.
    6. (A2)'s "inside one of its fills": holes are made only by [Close], under the reserved mark, and contents move into fills only at [End], which hands the marks on. A new rule that copies a live loan into a place (a fill, a capture) without its fill breaks it, and with it the order-independence of [Access]'s fixpoint.
    7. A match's declared type reads as a borrow exactly when its block's [Close] row is the borrow row (asserted INTERNAL in `closeBlock`).
    8. Untyped runs never form blocks (a match on an unknown is stuck and its call closes off); a change that makes them must give those blocks the same entry.
  - The interior exception (`E`) of the claim as first landed is removed by D91: the generic call checks a block's arms after its entry.
- **Known gaps against Rust.**
  - *F4, disjoint fields of a known one-constructor value.* `let x = (Z, Z); let r = &x.1; let y = clone(x.0); *r := Z; ()` is rejected: `x.0` is a one-arm match on `x` (D81), not in tail position, whose block would read `x` whole (its scrutinee), so its entry ends `r`. Main already rejected the same with an unknown `x`, where the projection closes off as a block: `F2 (x : Nat × Nat) : Unit := (let r = &x.1; let y = clone(x.0); *r := Z; ())` (and `&x.1` itself, at an unknown `x`, lends all of `x`: `F1`, `F3`). Rust accepts all four. *Fix:* a match on a value whose declared type has one constructor (projections, destructuring `let`s, `And`) refines the scrutinee instead of closing off (D62's reading: η on demand, world-scoped as D79 says), on every path, so it is never a block, has no entry, and lends only the field it names. For the lane that does it (orchestrator, 2026-11-19): consider giving a one-arm match no join at all as well as no entry, since with one arm there is no other path to agree with, and check whether that also removes the fault `probes/static-ends/CloneProj.lean` records (on main before and after D90, `CLg (x : Nat × Nat) : Nat × Nat := (let y = clone(x.0); x)` is accepted and `CLg((Z, Z))` rejected: `clone(x.0)` runs as the one-arm match naming `x.0` inside the `clone`, and that match's static join counts the read as a move). **Closed by D93**, which takes this fix, with no join for a one-arm match either; that also removed CloneProj.
  - *An internal error is reached beside F4.* A stuck block or `J` whose code borrows a FIELD of a captured place, `SB (k : Nat) (c : Nat × Nat) : Nat := (match k { Z => (let t = &c.0; ()), S _ => () }; 0)`, is rejected with "internal: stuck escaped to the top": the block call takes `&c.0` at the unknown `c`, which gets stuck outside every handler (found by the state sweep, 2026-11-20; on main before and after D91). **Closed by D93**: a block's captures are refined in place after its entry, so the field is a place when the block's call takes it.
  - *EK, a borrow read in one arm while another arm touches what it borrows.* `let k = S(Z); let b = &a; match k { Z => (a := 1; ()), S _ => (let z = clone(*b); ()) }; ()` is rejected: the entry accesses `a` (arm `Z` assigns it) and ends `b`, which arm `S` reads. Main already rejected it at an unknown `k`: `EKPlain (k : Nat) (a : Nat) : Unit := (let b = &a; match k { Z => (a := 1; ()), S _ => (let z = clone(*b); ()) }; ())`, where the block takes `a` first. The asserted test `StaticEnds.EK` is the same with `b` re-pointed at a place newer than it, which main accepted at an unknown `k` (its block took the read of `*b` first). Rust accepts all three: its arms are separate paths. *Fix:* end borrows as D84 moves places, by a join: inside an arm only that arm's accesses end borrows, and after the match every borrow that some arm's accesses end is ended, on every path. The direct run already ends per arm; the stuck block would need to take its captures without ending a borrow another capture is reached through, which one anonymous call with one argument list cannot do directly. A design question, not a patch.
- **Alternative not taken: the narrower fix of lane d90-join-dead-borrow** (WIP 56e4ba69, parked). [Join] rejects a drop through a dead borrow (an error naming the borrow), drops nothing behind a borrow variable the join drops whole (`jdDrops`), and stops counting a `⊥` borrow variable as moved at the match's entry. It treats `EJ`'s consumer only: `EI` (the `Id` no-ending clause), the block and `J` forms and `G1` remain, and each further consumer of liveness that is not monotone would need its own patch. It also leaves the alignment property false, so the proof would keep its early ends. This decision removes the cause, the two paths disagreeing on liveness, and every consumer agrees by construction.

## D91. A stuck block's arms are checked after its entry (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-19) [lane: block-entry-first]
Decision (the orchestrator, queued with D90: it serves the consistency proof directly, which the Lean model is about to be rebuilt for). D90's alignment property held at every corresponding point of a definition's generic call and an instance except inside the arms of a block the generic call closes off. This removes the exception.
- **Before.** `closeBlock` checked each arm in its world from the state before the block, then computed the captures, accessed them all (D90's entry, `entryAccess`) and took its arguments. A direct run's arm, at an instance or on a known constructor, starts after the same entry (D90's rule 2). So inside an arm the generic call held live the borrows `E` that the entry ends, which no instance holds there. D90 argued that `G`'s check of the arm never uses a label of `E` (the block takes every argument after the entry, so an arm that uses one fails there), but the generic call was stricter than every instance inside the arm, and the relation needed an exception. On main (46dd2d9a) these are rejected by [Id-typestate]'s no-ending clause, while their closed calls (which run the arm after the entry) would be accepted:
  - `IA`: `let r = &a; match k { Z => (let T = Id(Unit, (let z = clone(a); ()), ()); ()), S _ => () }; ()`;
  - `JA`, the same inside `J`'s transported term at an unknown endpoint ([T-J-stuck]'s block);
  - `IAPick`, the same with `r = Pick(n, &a, &b)` and the side reading `b`, a place lent to `r`.
  Rust accepts all three.
- **Rule.** A stuck block computes its captures first: the static join (`staticJoin`) and `blockCaptures`, which read syntax and declared types and do not depend on the arms' runs. It accesses them all, in order ([Entry]), and only then checks its arms, each from that state in its own world. The arms' moves are still checked against the static join (an assertion), the closure-capture and [D53] checks and [Return] at each arm's end follow as before, then the arguments and the block call. `movedPlace`'s η-refinements and generalisations, made while the captures are computed, now come before the arm worlds instead of after; where one is of the scrutinee itself (a one-constructor scrutinee one of whose fields an arm moves), the arm's [Split] setup takes that refinement (its `etaRefs` record, D68, D79) instead of a fresh one, so the arm is checked at the value the state holds (`EtaScrut`).
- **Consequences.** D90's alignment claim now holds at every corresponding point, inside arms included: each arm is checked, and run, from corresponding states after the entry. D90's statement is updated (the claim, the argument's case for a block, obligation 3, which now serves obligation 4 only). RULES §3 (stuck blocks), §5 ([Split]) and §6; the paper: §4's sentence on blocks and the appendix's [Block], [Split] and note 18.
- **Evidence.**
  - Full build: every verdict as expected; no existing verdict changes. Count 2,081 → 2,094 (13 new rows: `ReturnedBorrows.BlockEntry` and `BlockEntryRuns`, reasons asserted).
  - A dump of every row with its reason (`run` over `Registry.programs ++ caseStudies`), main 46dd2d9a against this change (before the new tests): all 2,081 rows have the same verdict; 2,079 have the same reason, and 2 differ only in the number of a fresh abstract value in the message, since `movedPlace`'s η-refinements now run before the arms: `Moves.N1` (`(⊥, σ8)` → `(⊥, σ3)`) and `Moves.ThroughLocal` (`(⊥, σ6)` → `(⊥, σ4)`), both "[D53] a borrow ends while its content is partly moved out". No asserted message changes.
  - Probes (`ochr/core/probes/block-entry-first/`): `ArmInterior.lean` (`IA`, `JA`, `IAPick` and their closed calls accepted, rejected on main; `IAUse`, `UseEnded` rejected on both; `EtaScrut`, `EtaScrut2` and their runs accepted on both); `DepFields.lean`, the dependent-field and `J` blocks around the new order (`LieThenBlock`: a lie through a borrow of the index field that the entry ends; `LieArm`, `LieJ`: D72's and D75's arm lie behind a borrow the entry ends; all rejected with the same message on main and here, and the twin `LieThenBlockOk` accepted on both).
- **Cost.** Not measured cleanly: a full build including the machine took 3 min 39 s, against 2 min 45 s to 5 min 40 s for D90's under varying load.

## D92. A private copy that types a call's arguments takes a borrow argument from its holder (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-20) [lane: state-sweep]
Decision (the orchestrator, after a viability check; found by the state sweep, `lake exe sweep`). Each label has one holder: the meaning spine's (WF1), which the consistency proof uses throughout. The checker now keeps that true inside the private copies that type a call, instead of the clause gaining an exception.
- **Found.** The sweep records the whole state at each located term of every example and case study and evaluates the spine's syntactic `Good` clauses on it. (WF1) failed in 52 states of 5 declarations (Arrays.FillFrom, Quicksort.Scan, ScanPerm, HashMap.MoveFrom, DepVec.VMoveCells). Inside the private copy that evaluates the next parameter type to give an argument its hint (`argHint`, D89), the earlier arguments are bound by value while they are still in the caller's temporaries, so a borrow argument was held twice: by its temporary and by the copy's parameter binding. Smallest program:
  `def R (s : &Nat) (n : Nat) (h : Eq(Nat, n, n)) : Unit by n := (match n { Z => (), S r => R(s, r, refl) })`
  (frame 1 `t1 = borrow_0 σ0`, frame 2 `s : &Nat = borrow_0 σ0`, privateDepth 2). From reading the code: `paramTypes` (a deferred parameter type, D82, read through `bindTy?`) binds the same way while the callee's parameter binding still holds the borrow.
- **Rule.** A private copy that binds a call's arguments to evaluate a parameter or result type takes a borrow argument from the position holding it, which is left `⊥` in the copy, rather than binding a second copy of it (`Obs.takeArgBorrow`, in `argHint`, `callType`, `declCallType`, `paramTypes`). An argument in flight (popped from the temporaries, as `callFn`'s are) has no holder in Ω, and nothing is taken. The other private bindings bind no borrow argument: constructor parameters and fields (`fieldTele`, `fieldTypeAt`, `checkParams`), fresh borrows (`piSort`, the Π-conversion, the observation), a sealed program's values (`sealedResultType?`). A type evaluated in the copy reads only the copy's frame and its captures (no borrow, WF4), so it cannot see the emptied holder. RULES §5 [Call-type] already said "a borrow argument moved into `xᵢ`" and the paper's appendix "A borrow argument is moved into its parameter"; the checker now does that when the argument is still held. RULES gains one clause saying the holder is left `⊥` on the copy; the paper is unchanged.
- **Consequences.** Only discarded copies differ. No verdict, message or decision changes.
- **Evidence.**
  - Full build: every verdict as expected (2,094).
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and log size, in both located modes (the `ochr` command's and the tests'), before and after: byte-identical.
  - `lake exe sweep`: (WF1) from 52 violating states to 0; every other count unchanged (68,541 states). `lake exe sweep --probes`: 19/19 verdicts, (WF1) 0.
  - Checked by reading, not measured: the deferred parameter types (`paramTypes` through `bindTy?`) run at call depth above 0, where the sweep records nothing.
- **A related reading of the sweep, for the spine.** (WF12)'s premise, a live borrow's site strictly under another live borrow's fill, never arises: at a position holding a live fill, no other live borrow has a site, at any path (1,786 states of the suite and 212 of `SweepProbes.lean`'s adversarial programs). Positions without a fill were not measured.

## D93. A match on a value of a one-constructor type never closes off (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-20) [lane: eta-one-ctor]
Decision (the orchestrator, from four directions at once). (1) D90's known gap F4: `&x.1; clone(x.0)` was rejected, because the projection `x.0` (D81) ran as a one-arm match whose stuck block, or whose entry on a known value, read `x` whole and ended the borrow of `x.1`. (2) CloneProj: `clone(x.0)` left `x` partly moved, because that match's static join counted the clone's read as a move. (3) An internal error: a stuck block whose arm borrows a field of a captured place (`SB`) got stuck taking `&c.0` at an unrefined `c`. (4) The consistency proof: the model reads a field directly, while the checker ran a field read as a match whose entry reads the whole value, so the two disagreed on which borrows are live after `x.0`. The model is not made to imitate a behaviour meant to be removed.
- **Rule.**
  1. A match on a value of a type with one constructor never closes off in a typed run. That covers a projection read, borrow or assignment (D81), a destructuring `let` and an explicit one-arm match. An unknown scrutinee `σ` is refined in place, `σ := C(σ̄)`: an exhaustive [Split] with one arm, whose fields are the ones this world already named (`etaRefs`, D68), else fresh ones, recorded for this world (D79). A sealed scrutinee is generalised first (D34). The one arm then runs directly, as at an instance, in tail position too.
  2. Such a match has no entry (D90's rule 2) and no join (D84), on every path. With one arm there is no other path to agree with, so it leaves exactly what its arm leaves.
  3. A stuck block's captures are refined in place after its entry and before its arms (`etaPlace`, in this world, as `movedPlace` already refined a moved capture, D91). So the field of a captured place is a place when the block's call takes it. A fill over a live borrow is never generalised: the entry has ended that borrow first (D90's `G1`).
  4. Untyped runs are unchanged. A match on an unknown is stuck there, and the call closes off at its caller, as before.
- **CloneProj: the join's removal alone, without the `clone` clause.** The prototype also gave `clone(p.i)` its own desugaring, so that the clone sat inside the match. Without a join the inner match drops nothing, and an enclosing match's static join reads `clone(x.0)` as the clone of a place, which moves nothing (`jdMoves`). So `CL`, `CLg` and `CLrun` are accepted without the clause. It is dropped as the larger change: a second desugaring for one form, fixing what rule 2 removes at its cause.
- **Alignment (D90's property), rerun.** Claim: at every point of a definition's own code, the generic call and an instance hold the same borrows live, lending the same places. A one-constructor match on both paths runs its one arm directly, with no entry and no join.
  - In the generic call the scrutinee is `C(σ̄)` after the in-place refinement (or already a constructor). At an instance it is `C(v̄)`. So both run the same arm from corresponding states, and D90's argument for straight-line code applies to it unchanged.
  - The entry and the join existed only to align a direct run with the generic call's block. With no block on either path there is nothing left for them to align.
  - An instance can meet an unknown only in an untyped run, under its caller's generic call. There the match is stuck and the call closes off. That run is the caller's closed call, which D90's rule 1 already aligns ([Lend]), not a point of this definition's code.
  - Rule 3 runs only where the generic call forms a block. It makes a capture's place exist where an instance's constructor value already has it, so both take the same arguments. It runs after the entry, which has ended every borrow over a fill on the capture's path, and `etaPlace` refuses to generalise a fill over a live borrow. Without that refusal the fill's hole was lost: `static-ends/Sealed.lean`'s `SF` was accepted at an unknown `n` and rejected at `n = 0` while the refinement ran before the entry. Asserted: `SFB`, `SFBUse` and its closed call.
  - A match with several arms keeps its join at the declared types' grain. When it encloses a one-arm match, its static join still covers what that inner arm moves (a field is covered by its parent), so its block and its direct run drop the same.
- **Dependent fields: which check catches each lie now.** Removing the block removes [Return]'s check at the end of a one-constructor arm (D72). That check was the generic path's alone: the direct path never had it. Now both paths run the arm directly, and a value the arm breaks stays open after it, as after any write outside a match (D64: `*v.0 := 2; *v.1 := …`). D64's principles apply to the code after the arm unchanged:
  - (W): every way the open value reaches code that relies on its type is a whole-again point, checked on the generic path. These are: a whole read (including `clone`) or move; a borrow variable passed on; a whole borrow; a block's, closure's or Π-type's capture; `Id`'s observation; a borrow of it ending (a borrow parameter at a return, a block's at an arm's end, every borrow at an observation's end); a function's or arm's result.
  - A field of an open value is typed by its content ([Open]). So a match on the dependent field of a broken value sees the content's type, not its telescope's.
  - (P), the producer obligation: a one-constructor match no longer produces a neutral at all, since the block was what produced the sealed program. The fields its refinement creates stand for fields of a whole value (the scrutinee is refined before any write).

  Asserted, each with its reason:
  - At the borrow parameter's end at the return: D72's `LieSeq`, `LieSeqProof`, `LieRet`, `RepairLater`, `Producers.ArmResult`, `FordAttack.SeqLie`, and D73's `LendIndexWrite`.
  - At a closure body's return: `LieClosure`, `ClosLie`.
  - At `Id`'s observation: `IdSideLie` ("Id observes it"), and `FordAttack.IdLie` ("a borrow of it ends": the observation ends every borrow).
  - Still at the enclosing block's return, unchanged: `LieNested`, `LieOther`, `LieOwned`; and every `J` that closes off (D75: `JLie`, `JInClos`, `JLieF`, `JLieV`).

  The adversarial pass (block `DirectArm`, every reason asserted) carries a broken `V` past the end of a directly run arm in 25 ways:
  - read by the field's telescope type (`ProjAfter`, `MatchAfter`, `InArm`, `LenSPf`: [Match] sees the content's type `Fin1(σ)`, not `Void`);
  - copied or moved (`CloneAfter`, `CloneThrough`, `MoveAfter`, `PassAfter`: read whole);
  - returned whole, reborrowed, as a field or as the index (`RetWhole`, `RetReborrow`, `RetField`, `RetIndex`);
  - captured by a closure (`ClosAfter`);
  - observed by `Id` (`IdAfter`);
  - through `J`, direct and closed off (`JDirect`, `JStuck`);
  - into a stuck call, given the value whole or its index field (`StuckCallWhole`, `StuckCallIdx`);
  - through a join, in and out of tail position (`JoinLie`, `JoinLieTail`), and through a partial repair (`RepairOnePath`);
  - into a statement whose proof would be a closed `False` (`LenS` accepted, as a false proposition may be stated; `LenSPf` and `BoomS` rejected).

  Nothing got through. Two programs are accepted and harmless: `EqSide` (an `Eq` side's lie is discarded with its copy, unobserved) and `LenS`. One is a pinned fail-safe incompleteness: `StuckCallIdxNoLie`, where a stuck call given the index field leaves a sealed program there, which `x`'s type cannot be checked against.
- **Consequences.**
  - The three D72-era fail-safe incompletenesses that this removes are accepted. `StuckBlocks.LendIndex` returns `&n` from the arm and writes nothing through it (Rust accepts `let r = &mut v.n;`). `StuckBlocks.LieDropped` is a statement's broken local, dropped. `LentIndex.BoomB` is a proposition false on every path; its proof `BoomBPf` and the closed `BoomIB : False` are rejected (new rows).
  - D90's known gaps F4 and the internal error are closed, and so is CloneProj.
  - `idaudit/R02_DeclGrain.lean`'s counterexample to D84's resource lemma is gone. `let a = v.0; v.1` is accepted whether `v`'s declared type is computed by a match or not, since the projection has no join that would take `v` whole.
  - `SweepProbes.TwoParts` is accepted: two borrows of disjoint parts through one returned borrow, as in Rust.
  - RULES §3 ([Join] and [Entry], [Proj], stuck blocks) and §5 ([Split]), and the paper's [Proj], [Join], [Entry], [Block] and [Split], say so.
- **Evidence.**
  - Full build: every verdict as expected. The count goes from 2,094 to 2,162, with 68 new rows:
    - `ReturnedBorrows.OneConstructor` and `OneConstructorRuns`: `F1`–`F4`, `TwoBorrows`, CloneProj's `CL`, `CLg`, `CLrun`, the SB family `SB`–`SJ` with their closed calls, the fill captures `SFB`, `SFBUse`, `SFR`, and controls;
    - `DependentFields.DirectArm`: the adversarial pass;
    - `LentIndex.BoomBPf`, `BoomIB`.
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message and location, in the `ochr` command's located mode and the tests' unlocated one, main against this change. 3 verdicts change (above, each intended). 11 rows keep their verdict with a new reason, each the later whole-again point named above: `StuckBlocks.LieSeq`, `LieSeqProof`, `LieClosure`, `LieRet`, `RepairLater`, `LentIndex.LendIndexWrite`, `Producers.ClosLie`, `IdSideLie`, `ArmResult`, `FordAttack.IdLie`, `SeqLie`. Every other row has the same verdict and message. In the located mode, 36 rows of the two edited example files keep verdict and message and move by the bytes their new comments add; 3 of them are reported at a larger term, the enclosing block that now catches the lie (`StuckBlocks.LieNested`, `LieOther`, `Producers.JInClos`, same message).
  - `lake exe sweep`: every clause holds as before on 69,949 states (2,162 verdicts as expected with the recording on), and the two clause faults already passed to the proof lead ((WF8) and clause (iii)'s ⋆ half) have the same kinds and no new ones. `--probes`: 19/19 as expected; (WF12)'s premise still never arises, though another live borrow now has a site at the position of a live fill in 18 of its states (`TwoParts`'s two disjoint parts), never below it.
  - Hand-run probes: `static-ends/Fields.lean` and `CloneProj.lean` flipped to the new verdicts (intended); `idaudit/R02_DeclGrain.lean` updated (its counterexample is gone); every other probe file reports exactly what it reported on main. The lane's working notes and probes (`probes/eta-one-ctor-*`) are folded into this entry and the asserted blocks.

## D94. The join drops a place that exists, or rejects (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: moved-place-exits]
Decision (the orchestrator, from two findings of the meaning spine, docs/31 D41). `movedPlace` makes a place the join drops exist: it refines an unknown of a one-constructor type in place, generalises a sealed one, and reaches a lent parent by ending its borrowers. Where it could not, it returned the longest existing prefix, and `dropAtJoin` dropped that whole (fuzz-port M1's reading: "some arm moves a part of it").
- **Found.**
  1. A match on a known constructor runs one arm, and the others are read for their moves (`Decide.jdMoves`) but never checked. So the join's set can hold an ill-typed place: `(c.0).5` where `In` has two fields, or `*(c.0)` where `c.0` is not a borrow. `jdGrain` checks only that a field step goes through a one-constructor type. The join then dropped `c.0` whole. `BadIdx` was accepted, and `BadIdxRead` and `BadDerefRead` were rejected for reading the dropped `c.0`, while the reference drops nothing for a place that does not exist (spine `Regress/UnrunArmPlace.lean`).
  2. When a strict prefix of the parent was lent, the inner re-rooting accessed it whole. The parent then existed and the place existed through it, yet `movedPlace` returned the parent (`Regress/ReRootExists.lean`).
- **Rule.** After `movedPlace` has done what it can, there are four outcomes:
  1. If the place exists, it is returned, whatever made it exist. The join drops the place, as an instance does. This fixes 2.
  2. If it does not exist and its longest existing prefix holds `⊥` or `⋆`, that prefix is returned and the drop does nothing, on every path, as before.
  3. Otherwise the definition is rejected where the match is: "[Join] an arm of the match moves p, which does not exist: its parent holds …". The parent holds an unknown or a stuck program of a type with several constructors, a constructor value without that field, or something that is not a borrow under `*`. This fixes 1. Rejecting is the fail-safe that needs no argument, and an arm that moves a place its type does not have is ill-typed whether or not it runs.
  4. The exit after a fill the access does not clear is kept as it is. A fill whose borrow `[Access]` did not end is dropped whole, not generalised, because generalising would lose its hole (D90's `G1`). That is the conservative direction. The access ends every borrow lent at the parent, so no run is known to take this exit.

  The proof lead proposed a different rule: return the place in every fallback, so that the drop does nothing. It was not taken. Where the fallback dropped more than an instance would (an unknown of a several-constructor type at the parent), "drop nothing" is the unsafe direction, and the argument that it is unreachable rests on invariants that are swept but not proved.
- **Reach.** The places the join drops are at the declared types' grain (`jdGrain`), so a field step goes through a value whose declared type has one constructor. Its content is then a constructor, an unknown or sealed program of that type (refined), `⊥`, `⋆` or a fill (reached). So only an ill-typed place in an arm that is not run reaches the rejection. An adversarial pass of 11 well-typed programs (`probes/moved-place-exits/Adversarial.lean`, run by hand) does not reach it. It tries joins through nested fields, a type parameter, a computed type, a stuck call's result, a several-constructor value, a borrow lent before the match (non-tail, and in tail position with no entry), a statement's side, and a callee run at the generic call. The ReRootExists shape is reached by `JoinPlaces.LentTail` (tail position, `c` lent whole, the join's access ending it). No program can observe it: in tail position nothing follows the join, a result can borrow the dropped part only through a borrow parameter, and moving out under a borrow parameter is rejected at the return (D53).
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message and location, in both located modes, main (ca91d72b) against this change: all 2,162 rows identical.
  - Full build: every verdict as expected. The count goes from 2,162 to 2,173 with the new block `ReturnedBorrows.JoinPlaces`:
    - `BadIdx`, `BadIdxRead` and `BadDerefRead`, rejected with the new reason;
    - the controls `GoodIdxRead` and `GoodIdxGen`;
    - `LentTail`;
    - ReRootExists' `LentRead`, rejected (the arm that lends `c` uses it whole, so the join moves `c` whole), and `NotLentRead`, accepted.
  - `lake exe sweep` (70,207 states) and `--probes`: every clause as before, with the same kinds for the two clause faults already with the proof lead.
  - Every hand-run probe file reports what it did on main.
- RULES §3 [Join] and the paper's *Join* paragraph gain the rule. The paper's body is unchanged.

## D95. The decision procedures write no cache (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: no-decision-caches]
Decision (the orchestrator, after a measurement). The decision procedures in `Ochr/Decide.lean` wrote two caches into the checker's state. `constDecls` held what each global's declared type says (`declOf` on a constant), and `classCache` held each Π-type's erasure class (`fnClass`). The consistency proof must show after every step that the model's decisions are still the checker's, and a decision procedure that writes the state makes that a proof about cache coherence. A plain checker is also part of the paper's claim.
- **Rule.** Both caches are removed, along with their `MState` fields and their keeping in `restoreKeep` and `exitWorld`, and the procedures recompute. The stability proofs lose their hypothesis about the cache: `ClassCacheSound` and `lookup_mem` are deleted, and `step_fnClass_shape` holds at any state, leaving it unchanged.
- **Evidence.** `lake build` is green with 2,173 verdicts. A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and log size, in both located modes, is identical to main (8035f7ed). `lake exe sweep` (70,207 states) and `--probes` give the same counts.
- **Cost.** Three interleaved runs each, on 15 pinned cores. Nothing else was busy: other Claude sessions at 1–3% CPU, an oversight web app and one Lean editor worker, load average 1.1–2.4 on 20 cores.

  | | with the caches | without | change |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `lake build` of the examples (outputs deleted, every verdict re-checked) | 162.9 / 159.7 / 160.8 s | 192.0 / 187.0 / 187.0 s | +16% |
  | `lake exe tests`, total check time (sum of per-declaration medians of 21 runs) | 9.86 / 9.69 / 9.82 s | 11.13 / 11.07 / 11.30 s | +13% |

  The slowdown is spread over every call, because `fnClass` runs at each call and now recomputes the callee's telescope and codomain reading:
  - The slowest declarations grow 9–13%: `Quicksort.ContractRun2` goes from 2.14 to 2.32 s and `ContractRun1` from 1.53 to 1.69 s.
  - The largest relative growth, about 1.3x, is in call-heavy code over long dependent telescopes: `Quicksort.Scan*` and `ArrayLemmas.SwapIsSwapS`.
- **Available, not taken.** The smallest pure change that would buy the time back is to compute a global's type reading and its Π-type's class once, when the global is added, and store them with its declaration (`GDef`). That is declaration data, not run state, and it covers constants and most calls. It is to be taken only when a workload needs it.
- **Still written.** `liveScope`, the reader's mode flag, is still set and restored around a reading by `withLive`. RULES and the paper never described the caches, so they are unchanged.

## D96. A decision reads an unknown by its frozen reading (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: frozen-readings]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the proof lead's finding). The decision procedures also read the table of unknowns' types (Δ): `valueDecl (.abs σ)`, `isPropV`, `sortOf?`, `sortOf`, `typeClass` and `sealedSort?` looked up the type of an unknown embedded in a term. A refinement rewrites Δ, and a sealed program built later embeds newer unknowns, so the model's decisions could not be fixed once: the answer about an old input could change, and a later input's answer depended on a table the run keeps rewriting. The standards say decisions are made from syntax, declarations and declared types.
- **Rule.**
  - Each abstract value's declared reading is frozen when the value is made (`MState.absDecl`, written by `freshAbs`, never refined). It is what its type says as a declared type at that moment (`Decide.valTypeDecl`, `freshAbsR`), read from the type and the frozen readings of the unknowns it names.
  - The six readers above read it through `absReading`, and none of them reads Δ.
  - A closure's or Π-type's captures are read by the readings it recorded when it was formed (`rd`, D80, D82; `capReadings`), not by reading the captured values: `sortOf` of a Π-type, `valueDecl` of a closure, `valTypeDecl`, `paramDecls`, `paramFlags` and `fnClass`.
  - The table is kept whole by `restoreKeep` and `exitWorld`, since its entries are written once.
  - A hole's unknowns carry their readings with the global that made them (`GDef.absDecls`), as their types are carried (`seedHoles`).
  - The one error that printed an unknown's type, `sortOf` at a non-sort, now says that the type, when it was made, did not read as a sort. No example reaches it.
  - The model can carry the frozen table as it carries Δ, so "the term alone" is not needed.
- **What can change a verdict, and what did.** A frozen reading differs from the reading of the current type only where a refinement rewrote an unknown's type to one that reads differently. By D55 a type's declared sort is syntactic: a type function's calls read as its declared codomain whatever their arguments. So for checked programs a refinement does not change a type's reading (`StabilityFootprint`'s probes show it changing only at an unchecked global). Nothing changed:
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message and location, in both located modes, against main (fc762af7): all 2,173 rows identical.
  - The adversarial block `Inductives.FrozenReadings` (31 rows) takes the same verdicts and reasons on main and here. It holds unknowns whose type is a type parameter or a stuck type: `TP(n)` refined to a proposition, `TS(n)` to a sort, `TF2(n)` to a function type, and `IdT` at `Prop` and at `Type`. They are read where a decision reads them (the proof-ness and erasure of `KeepTP`'s block, a call's class through `CallTF2Split`'s function, the sort of `KeepTS`'s result, an `Id` over `KeepTP`), at the generic call and at the closed calls (`…Run`, `…Split`).
  - A function type whose arms differ in class (`TF`) is rejected by D77, and a call through a stuck function type (`CallTF2`, `CallTQ`) is rejected by typing, before any decision reads them.
  - A borrow type cannot be a type argument (D48 (2)), so it cannot be the type of such an unknown.
- **What the decision procedures read now.**
  - Syntax: terms, with the values embedded in them read by their heads, meaning an inductive type's declaration, a global's name, a closure's code, a sealed program's head call's declared codomain.
  - Declarations.
  - The readings in scope: bindings' recorded readings and proof flags, and the frame's shape.
  - The capture readings that closures and Π-types record.
  - The frozen readings of the unknowns a term names.

  No refinement or run changes these for a given term. A refinement can change the term itself: it substitutes into a sealed program embedded in it, which may then normalise to a different head. The decision on the new term is a function of that term, and that the old and new answers agree is D55 preservation, now needed for sealed programs only, no longer for unknowns. Still written by a reader: the `liveScope` mode flag, set and restored by `withLive` (D95).
- **Evidence.**
  - `lake build` green: 2,173 → 2,204 verdicts, with the new block.
  - `lake exe sweep` (70,526 states) and `--probes`: every clause as before.
  - Every hand-run probe file reports what it does on main.
  - RULES P2 and the history line, the paper's §6.3 ("The two paths must agree") and the appendix's *Erasure*, and `notes/two-paths-census.md` say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D97. A sealed program whose text holds a loan is never run (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: sealed-holes]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the proof lead's finding). Re-running a sealed program that still holds a hole breaks the invariant every step relies on. The run writes the hole (a fill's `*r := loan_k`), so its state holds a loan whose borrow only the outer state holds. And what the run computes cannot be observed: a fill sits at a lent place, and under D90 any access of a lent place ends the borrow first, which fills the hole and runs the program again anyway. Rust's view is the same: a mutably borrowed place has no readable content until the borrow ends.
- **Rule.** A sealed program whose text holds a loan is not run. Such a loan is a hole by construction, whether or not its borrow is in Ω yet. A substitution into such a program is made on its text, and the program stays sealed (`nfSealed`, `Term.hasLoanAtom`). It runs when its text holds no loan, which is [End]'s re-run once it has filled the last hole. [Seal]'s "a sealed program over live holes normalises to a fill over the same holes" (`lentTo`) becomes "is left alone".
- **Every site that runs a sealed program's text, and what happens there now** (docs/31 D63 on the spine):
  - **`endBorrow`'s substitution of `l`'s content.** When the content holds a live loan (a reborrow of `*l` that outlives it), the programs over `l` now hold that loan and stay sealed. A program with a second hole stays sealed too. They run when the last loan is substituted.
  - **A refinement in a split world** (`refine`'s substitution and its `renormAll`): fills stay sealed, with the refinement in their text.
  - **The neutral substitutions** (`generalizeNeutral`, `generalizeFound`, `evalMatch` and `evalMatchInd` on a recorded neutral, `etaPlace`, `movedPlace`): the same.
  - **`closeCall`'s `&T` row.** It substitutes the new fill, which holds `loan_k`, into earlier calls' fills over `loan_l` before `borrow_k` is in Ω. The test is on the text, so `loan_k` counts as a hole there and the order needs no change.
  - **`sealedType?`, which types a program by running its text.** It does meet such programs on accepted runs. In `HashMap.Get`, `MapGetMut` and `DepVec.VGet`, [Repack] at the return reads the type of a borrow parameter's part, which holds a fill over the returned borrow. It no longer runs such a text, and reads the type off it instead:
    - a fill reads a cell (`peek(c)`): the type the cell was declared at (`(u : T)`), else its initial value's type;
    - a borrow result reads through the call's result (`peek(*r)`): the content of the head's declared codomain `&T`;
