    - a result form: its head's declared codomain (`sealedResultType?`);
    - anything else: no type.
    These are what the typed run returned, since its result is the cell's stored type or the call's [Call-type]. With a temporary assertion in place of this, those three functions and their 15 dependent rows were the only rows to fail. That is how the observer was found: the typing run inside [Repack], not a value read.
  - Not sources: a substitution into a goal or an expected type (they hold no loan, (WF11)), and `lendEnv` (marks only).
- **Alignment (D90's property), rerun.** In a split world the generic call now keeps a sealed fill, unrun, where an instance has the place's content marked `lent_k`. The fill's text holds the holes of exactly the borrows the instance's marks name: rule 1 lends the same places on both paths, and the sites of a sealed fill are the borrows of its loans. So the same places are lent to the same borrows. An access ends them on both paths (`fillHoles` of a sealed fill is its live loans). [End] fills the hole at the same point on both, after which the generic call runs the program as the instance already has the content.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message and location, in both located modes, main (ed4676eb) against this change, with the new block on both sides: all 2,220 rows identical. In the located mode, 6 rows have a different number of editor notes, all in hovers:
    - `HashMap.Get`, `MapGetMut`, `DepVec.VGet` and `VGetP` lose the notes of the typing run they no longer make;
    - `HoleSites.SplitTail` and `ObsLent` gain one or two.
  - New asserted block `ReturnedBorrows.HoleSites` (16 rows; count 2,204 → 2,220). It hits each site with a hole in the text: [Close]'s fills, and `Second` and `SecondSplit`, a stuck call over a returned borrow; a split after a stuck borrow-returning call (`SplitAfter` and its runs, `SplitTail`, `EqLent`); [End] with a reborrow that outlives its borrow (`Reb`, `RebRun`); a neutral's generalisation into a fill (`NeutralIn`). It also tries to observe a lent place without ending its borrow, through `Id` (`ObsLent`, rejected by [Id-typestate]), `Eq` and a call (`CallLent`; `CallLentUse` rejected). A trace confirmed that each program reaches a skipped re-run. Every verdict and reason is main's.
  - One unit test changed, because it asserted the old reading: `Units.lean`'s `B(0)[loan_9] ≡ loan_9`, a program over an inert loan, which ran. It now stays sealed, and the next test, which fills the hole, runs it as before.
  - `lake exe sweep` (70,856 states) and `--probes`: every clause as before. The 24 recorded probe files under `probes/`, run by hand on both: identical output.
  - Build time, the examples' build with every verdict re-checked, three interleaved runs: 193.4 / 192.0 / 190.5 s before, 190.9 / 190.3 / 190.0 s after. That is about 1% faster, within noise and not slower.
  - RULES [Seal] and the history line, and the paper's §4 sentence on holes and its appendix [Seal] (new rule [Seal-hole]), say so. D90's [Seal] bullet points here. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D98. A borrow never ends over a moved-out content, in an observation as anywhere (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: obs-moved-ends]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the proof's finding). D68's fuzzer acceptance let an observation end a side's borrows over a part the side had moved out, reading it as `⊥` (the state flag `observing`, set only by `Id`). Everywhere else [End] requires the content to be whole (D53), and so did RULES and the paper's [Obs], whose ends are [End]'s: only the checker had the exception. The semantic model cannot follow it through a borrow a call returned, since a backward function is defined only on whole contents. The witness, on spine `Meaning/Regress/ObsReturned.lean`, is `ObsBorrowThrough`: both sides of an `Id` reborrow through a borrow `IdL(&y)` returned and then move its content out. It was accepted, with the checker's observation reading the lender as moved where the model reads the error value. No false proposition followed, since both sides behave alike, but the two disagreed on what an observation reads.
- **Rule.** A borrow never ends over a moved or partly moved content, in an observation's final ends as anywhere. A side of `Id` that leaves a borrow's content moved out when the observation ends it is rejected, with the message the same code gets when it runs (`[D53] a borrow ends while its content is partly moved out`). The `observing` flag is removed (`Env`, `obsResult`'s parameter, [Conv-fun]'s pass-through): `Id` and [Conv-fun] now observe alike, as the paper already said.
- **What the exception was for.** It came in with D68's fuzzer acceptance (54fa605e, 2026-10-01), recorded in the bullet "A moved place's equation is stuck". That bullet's reason is that a stuck block took a place by move if any arm moved it, so an abstract run could have a place moved where the ground run's arm left it. An observation then read such a part as `⊥`, whose equation was stuck, which was fail-safe. (The commit says only "ending a side's borrows over a consumed part is not an error"; the link to that reason is my reading.) Both premises have gone since D84: moves are definite (a match drops at its join what any arm moves, on every path), and `⊥ = ⊥` is `⊤`, not stuck. D68's own third consequence already said there is no exception for an `Id` side moving out through a borrow: such a side writes `clone(*x)`, as runtime code must.
- **Who relied on it.** No case study and no book example. Of the 2,220 rows on main, one verdict changes: `Moves84.LeftOpenBoth`, `Id(Unit, (let a = *y; ()), (let b = *y; ()))` over `y : &Nat`, a test of [Id-typestate]'s "left open" component. It is now rejected, and its rewrites are accepted and asserted beside it: `RefilledBoth` (`let a = *y; *y := a; ()`) and `ClonedBoth` (`let a = clone(*y); ()`). The book's `Id` examples read through a borrow only at a copy type (`IncTwice` over `&Word`) or with `clone`.
- **Rust.** Each changed side is rejected by Rust as ordinary code: `let a = *y;` with `y: &mut T` and `T` not `Copy` is E0507 (cannot move out of `*y`, which is behind a mutable reference), and so are `(*r).0` and a result `*r`. Ochr is more permissive than Rust only where the place is refilled before the borrow ends (D53 (h)), and that stays accepted in observations too (`ObsAfterCallRefill`, `ObsRRefill`, `FieldRefill`).
- **[Id-typestate]'s "left open" component** is now non-empty only in an `Id` that is rejected anyway: when the sides differ it names the place (`LeftOpen`), and when they agree the observation rejects (`LeftOpenBoth`). It is kept for its message.
- **Not affected.** A statement that ends no borrow: each side of `Eq` runs on its own copy and its move is undone with it (`EqSideVal`, `Eq(List(Nat), *r, *r)`, accepted on both). A borrow that ends inside a side (`ObsInSide`, `ThroughW`) was always rejected, by the same message.
- **`Eq` against `Id` (recorded for later; not changed).** `Eq(List(Nat), *r, *r)` is accepted while `Id(List(Nat), *r, *r)` is rejected (`EqSideVal`, `TakeBoth`). My reading is that D68's model intends this, and that the difference is `Id`'s, not `Eq`'s. Under D68 a side of `Eq` is a statement: it runs on its own copy, its move of `*r` happens there, and the copy is thrown away without ending any borrow, so nothing ever asks whether `*r` is whole. That is the runtime's own reading: `let z = *r;` is an error only once `r`'s borrow ends unfilled. `Id` asserts more, that the two computations' effects agree, and observing effects ends every borrow ([Obs]). A computation that leaves a borrow's content moved out has no observable final state, just as the same code has none when it runs to the borrow's end. What could be worth its own question is the book's one-mental-model yardstick, since a reader may expect `*r` in a statement to behave as in `Id`. The type-safety fragment (paper §tf, "No move out through a borrow") already forbids such reads in statements as in code, so the proof does not depend on the answer.
- **The narrow alternative**, rejecting only an observation whose footprint holds a place lent to a returned borrow whose content a side left moved, was not needed. It would save no program in the suite or the book. It would keep a mode, a decision about which places are lent to a returned borrow, and a checker that differs from RULES' and the paper's [End].
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message and location, in both located modes, main (03740f8d) against this change, with the new tests on both sides (2,243 rows): 9 rows change, all from accepted to rejected with `[D53] a borrow ends while its content is partly moved out`, located at the `Id`. They are `Moves84.LeftOpenBoth` and eight rows of the new block (the witness and its changed neighbours). The other 2,234 rows are identical, editor-note counts included.
  - New asserted block `ReturnedBorrows.ObsMoved` (21 rows), plus `RefilledBoth` and `ClonedBoth` beside `LeftOpenBoth` (count 2,220 → 2,243).
    - The proof's witness file, rows and verdicts: `ObsBorrowThrough`, `ObsAfterCall`, `ObsAfterBorrow`, `ObsR` (now rejected); `ObsAfterCallCtl` (rejected by [Id-typestate], as before); `ObsInSide` (rejected by [D53], as before); `PartMove` (accepted, as before).
    - The adversarial rows, which try to read a moved-out part through an observation: a result that carries the content out (`TakeBoth`), a field (`FieldOpen`), a side split on an unknown (`SplitSide`), and an `Id` nested in a side (`Nested`). All are now rejected. A reborrow that ends inside a side (`ThroughW`) and the same code run rather than observed (`RunAfterCall`) were already rejected.
    - The rewrites, all accepted: `clone` (`ObsBorrowThroughClone`, `ObsAfterCallClone`, `TakeBothClone`) and refilling (`ObsAfterCallRefill`, `ObsRRefill`, `FieldRefill`). A side of `Eq` (`EqSideVal`) is unaffected.
  - The 24 hand-run probe files of earlier lanes under `probes/`, run on both: one other verdict changes, `probes/d84/A7.lean`'s `L1` (and the two rows that use it). `L1` is D84's adversarial `Id(Unit, match k { Z => (let a = (*y).0; ()), S _ => () }, (let a = (*y).0; ()))`, where both sides leave `(*y).0` moved. It is the same class, now rejected.
  - `lake exe sweep` (71,264 states) and `--probes`: the same clause kinds as on main, `--probes` byte-identical.
  - The fuzzer does not build on main (`Fuzz/Harness.lean` predates `freshAbs`'s declaration argument, and `Fuzz/Lib.lean`'s `AddMZeroL` predates D84), so it was not run.
  - RULES [End] (the borrows an observation ends) and the history line, the appendix's note under [Obs], and D68's bullet (marked removed) say so. `notes/id-model.md` §6's "one flag, one rule" is marked resolved. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D99. A call's returned borrow is always fresh (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: fresh-result]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the proof's finding, docs/31 D69). A call whose declared result is a borrow reserves a label `g` for [Lend]'s marks (D90, rule 1). When the call closed off, its result was [Close]'s fresh `k`. When the body ran, the result was whatever borrow the body returned, which for `IdP (x : &Nat) : &Nat := x` is the argument's own label, made before the call. The call's meaning in the model returns a fresh label. So the three paths disagreed on which label the result has. The proof's simulation keeps the renaming of every label issued before a step, and that was false at the accepted `UseIdP` (`Meaning/Regress/ReturnParam.lean` on spine). This was not unsound, but the checker's own paths were inconsistent.
- **Rule.** The result of such a call is always `borrow_g`, whether the body ran or the call closed off. The borrow the body returned, or [Close]'s `k`, is *ended into* `g`: every `loan_k` and mark `lent_k` becomes `g`'s (`LendOp.into`, `callFn`). Where a place is then marked `lent_g` over a value that already carries `g`'s hole (marked once by rule 1 and once as `k`'s), one is kept, as `lentTo` keeps one. Since it is a renaming, no value changes. After the call the argument's label appears nowhere, as after a stuck call. The comment "`g` becomes the result's label" is now true.
- **The cases.**
  - *A borrow argument returned* (`IdP`, or `LetP`, which first binds it with a `let`): its label, made by `&y` before the call, is renamed: `y ↦ lent_1 loan_1`, `r ↦ borrow_1 2` where main had `lent_0 loan_0`, `borrow_0 2`.
  - *A reborrow made in the callee* (`Reb (x) := &*x`): a label made during the call, ended into `g` the same way; the parameter's own borrow ends at the pop as before.
  - *One of several, computed* (`PickN(n, &a, &b)` at a known `n`): the returned argument's label is renamed. The other argument's places stay lent to the result by rule 1, now as `lent_g`.
  - *Through two calls* (`IdP(IdP(&y))`, and `Via (x) := IdP(x)`): the inner call's result is its own `g₁`, and the outer call ends `g₁` into `g₂`. The place's two marks `lent_g₂ lent_g₂ loan_g₂` collapse to one.
  - *Closing off*: [Close]'s `k` is ended into `g`, and its fills' texts hold `loan_g`. They are not run (D97), and they are filled when `g` ends. *Stuck, then refined* (`UsePickSplit`): the generic call closes `PickN(n, …)` off, and the split on `n` re-runs the sealed result program in a private run, where the inner call returns its own fresh label.
- **Not changed: a match.** A borrow-typed match not in tail position that runs (`joinEnds`) still returns its arm's borrow, an older label, while the stuck block it closes off as on an unknown returns the call's fresh one. I left it because the reference runs a match's arm in place and returns the arm's label, so changing the checker's match would open, for matches, the disagreement D99 closes for calls.
- **D90's alignment, rerun.** The generic call's stuck form and an instance's run now give the result the same kind of label: the label the call reserves, made at the call point after every argument's. The places lent to it are the same on both paths (rule 1 is unchanged), and so are its fills' holes (`loan_g` in the generic call's fills, `lent_g` marks at the instance). So the bijection on labels between the two runs maps the call's `g` to `g`. It needs no case for a body that returned an argument's label, which the instance used to have and the generic call never did. At `let a = 1; let b = 2; let r = PickN(n, &a, &b)` both the stuck call and the run at `n = 0` leave `r ↦ borrow_2 …` (0 and 1 are `&a` and `&b`), with `a` and `b` a fill over `loan_2` or marked `lent_2`. Here the two paths give not only the same kind of label but the same label.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message and location, in both located modes, main (911a730d) against this change, with the new tests on both sides (2,261 rows): identical, editor-note counts included.
  - New asserted block `ReturnedBorrows.FreshResult` (18 rows; count 2,243 → 2,261), with the same verdicts on main. `UseIdP` is 5 by `refl`, and 2 is rejected. `Reb`, `LetP`, `IdP(IdP(&y))` and `Via` give 5. `PickN` at 0 and 1 gives 7 and 6, and 6 at 0 is rejected. `UsePickSplit` proves the stuck-then-refined case by a split.
  - The property itself, asserted on the checker's state at a hole (`FreshResultAt`, `freshResultAt`). After `let r = IdP(&y)` the state is `y ↦ lent_1 loan_1`, `r ↦ borrow_1 2` (`&y` made 0, the call reserved 1). Main gives `lent_0 loan_0` and `borrow_0 2`, so these guards fail there. Through two calls the result is the outer call's label 2, and a reborrow made in the callee is ended into the call's label 1.
  - The 24 hand-run probe files under `probes/`, run on both: identical output.
  - `lake exe sweep` (71,526 states), against main with the same tests: the same states and the same violation counts in every clause. `--probes`: the same clauses, differing only in label numbers (a probe's `lent_0 loan_0` and `borrow_0` are now `lent_1 loan_1` and `borrow_1`; [Close]'s `borrow_7`, ended into the call's label, is `borrow_6`).
  - RULES [Call] (the reserved label is the result's; the returned borrow is ended into it) and the history line, and the appendix's [Lend], say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D100. Every proposition is a copy; a proof function's own binding is a proof (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: prop-copy]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the proof's sweep, slice1). `Decide.declCopy` had no case for a Π-type, so a place declared `Π(x : A). P` with `P` a proposition read as not a copy. Erasure, the rest of the checker and the model all treat its value as a proof, `⋆`. In the suite 6,447 states had a non-copy place holding `⋆` (smallest: `Recursion.Apply`'s `f : Π(x : Nat). False`), and a function's own binding of such a type was a non-copy place the join could drop. Two statements of the proof were false at reachable states.
- **Rule 1, copy-ness agrees with erasure.** Every declared reading that is a proposition is a copy (`DeclInfo.isProof`: `Prop`, or a Π-type whose codomain is one, by impredicativity), as its values are proofs, freely reusable as in Lean. A Π-type into data or types is still not a copy (`TyFnTwice`). The same holds for a data closure (`DataCloTwice`).
- **Verdicts.** Reading a proof never moved it before either, since a proof place holds `⋆`, so rule 1 changes no verdict. It changes what the declared readings say, which the join's analysis and the model read. In every case the lead asked about, a proof function used twice was already accepted and still is, each with a closed instance where the function returns data:
  - called twice;
  - passed as an argument twice;
  - bound by two `let`s;
  - captured by two closures;
  - used by one arm of a non-tail match and after it.
  `&f` stays rejected by D66 (a proof is never borrowed). A data field cannot hold one: fields are first-order data (D36), so `MkPB(u : Unit, h : Π(x : Nat). Eq(Nat, x, x))` is rejected as a declaration.
- **A closure into proofs** is erased where it is made: it is formed on a private copy, its captures untouched. So reading it as a copy duplicates nothing. `CapNC` captures `n` and still returns `n` afterwards, which is 3 at 3. Nothing can be taken out of it as data: `DupThrough` (a match on a proof of a non-subsingleton) stays rejected by [D45].
- **Rule 2, the internal fault.** `C1`, a proof by recursion whose recursive call is in a match that is not in tail position, failed "INTERNAL [pre-pass] #1: erased/proof = (true, true) by its declared type, (false, false) after running".
  - *Rule 1 alone does not fix it.* The cause is the function's binding of itself (`runBodyCore`, `checkFix`), and a closure's capture of it, which hold the function, not `⋆`, so that [Rec] sees recursive calls through them (D78). Their `proof` flag is `false`, while their declared reading (`.pi` of a proposition) is a proof. The stuck block captures the binding as an argument, and that read was classified by the declared reading before running and by the flag after.
  - *Fix:* `leafProof` reads a variable's declared reading, as the pre-pass does. That is what the paper's appendix ("Erased terms") already says: "one flag per binding, saying whether the binding was declared a proof".
  - *Rejected alternative:* setting the binding's flag to `true` instead would have been unsound. A closure's capture flagged a proof has its reads inlined as `(⋆ : T)`, which hides the recursive call from [Rec]. On that variant `BadClo (x : Nat) : False by x := (let c = (λ(u : Unit) : False => BadClo(x)); c(()))`, a non-decreasing proof of `False`, was accepted. On this change it is rejected by [Rec], as on main.
  - `C1` and its accepted controls `C3` (a tail match) and `C2` (a non-recursive lemma in the arm) are asserted, with `SelfArm` (the same shape at `Eq`), `GoodClo`, and the rejected `BadBlk` and `BadClo`.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message and location, in both located modes, main (5dac883c) against this change, with the new tests on both sides (2,293 rows): two rows change, `ProofCopies.C1` and `ProofCopies.SelfArm`, from the INTERNAL rejection to accepted. The other 2,291 rows are identical, editor-note counts included. Nothing goes from accepted to rejected.
  - New asserted block `Recursion.ProofCopies` (32 rows; count 2,261 → 2,293).
  - `lake exe sweep`: the same violation counts in every clause as main with the same tests (71,950 states against 71,937; the 13 more are `C1`'s and `SelfArm`'s checks, which now run to the end). `--probes`: identical. slice1's measurement of rule 1 alone: the place-type clause holds on all 71,526 states, and no non-copy place holds `⋆`.
  - The 24 hand-run probe files under `probes/`, run on both: identical output.
  - The StabilitySig proof of `declCopy` and the rest of the build go through unchanged.
  - RULES P3 (a Π-type into proofs is a proposition, so a copy type; copy-ness agrees with erasure) and the history line say so. The paper needs no change: its copy types already list "propositions", which by impredicativity include a Π into proofs, and the appendix's "Erased terms" already reads a binding's class as "whether the binding was declared a proof".

## D101. The join rejects an arm that moves through `*` of a place not declared a borrow (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: join-deref]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the slice prover's finding). An arm the run never takes is never checked, but the places it moves feed the join's drop list. D94 made the join reject at run time a move of a field the type does not have (`movedPlace`). A dereference of something that is not a borrow still slipped through: the checker re-rooted it to a `⋆`, or read through a borrow's mark, and did nothing. Both `DP1` (`*h` of a proof) and `DP4` (`*(x.1)` of a proof field, under a place lent to a returned borrow) were accepted. In `DP4` the proof's model and the checker disagree on whether `r`'s borrow is still live after the join. No fix on the model's side is honest: the place is ill-typed, and Rust rejects the arm, since it type-checks every arm.
- **Rule.** In `Decide.jdGrain`, decided from declared types before any run, each `*` step of a moved place must be through a place declared a borrow (`placeDecl` reads `.ref`). Otherwise the definition is rejected: "[Join] an arm of the match moves a place through * of a place not declared a borrow".
- **(1) A borrow reading only through a type function or a match.** Through a type function there is none: `&` is only ever written, at the top of a declared type (D48), and `TF (b : Bool) : Type := match b { true => &Nat, false => &Nat }` is rejected by the parser (`&Nat` is read as a borrow of a place). Through a match there is, and the static check reads it by the same reader the live arm uses (`placeDecl`, `.ref`): a live dereference (`ViaMatchLive`) and a dead one (`ViaMatchDead`) of `let r = match b { Z => &x, S _ => &y }` are accepted.
- **(2) Unchanged and accepted.** `*r` in a dead arm, for a borrow variable `r` moved earlier by a statement (`MovedBefore`) or by an arm of an earlier match (`MovedByArm`). A reborrow `&*r` in a dead arm (`RebDead`). `clone(*r)` or a refilled move (`ParamDeadClone`, `ParamDeadRefill`). A dead arm that moves out through a borrow and does not refill is rejected, as on main, because the join drops the content and the borrow ends moved out ([D53], `ParamDead`); Rust rejects the same with E0507.
- **(3)** No accepted program became rejected. The one expected message that changes is `JoinPlaces.BadDerefRead`, whose `*(c.0)` in a dead arm is now rejected by this check instead of D94's run-time one. `DData` (`*x` of a number) was already rejected at run time by D94 and is now rejected statically.
- **(4) D94's run-time rejection is still reachable, for a field.** A dead arm that projects a field index the declared one-constructor type does not have (`JoinPlaces.BadIdx`, `(c.0).5` of a two-field `In`) is rejected by `movedPlace` at run time, as before. With `*` now static, I believe that field case is the only way it is reached. `jdGrain` keeps a field step only through a place whose declared reading is an inductive with one constructor, whose content at the join is that constructor, an unknown or sealed value that `movedPlace` η-refines, `⊥` or `⋆` (nothing to drop), or a fill, which is ended first. So it could be decided in `jdGrain` as well, by checking the projection's index (or the named field's constructor) against the declaration. Not done, per the brief.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message and location, in both located modes, main (70ad9364) against this change, with the new tests on both sides (2,311 rows). Six rows change. `DP1` to `DP4` go from accepted to rejected, the intended change, on new rows. `BadDerefRead` and `DData` change only their message, from D94's run-time one to this static one. The other 2,305 rows are identical, editor-note counts included.
  - New asserted block `ReturnedBorrows.JoinDeref` (18 rows; count 2,293 → 2,311). `BadDerefRead`'s asserted message is updated.
  - `lake exe sweep`: the same violation counts in every clause (72,076 states against 72,081, the five fewer being the rejected rows' unreached states). `--probes` and the 24 hand-run probe files under `probes/`: identical.
  - RULES [Join] and the history line, and the appendix's [Join] paragraph, say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D104. Every value the machine holds has a stored type (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-20) [lane: checker-temps]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the proof lead's design, docs/31 D81 on `spine-lead`). D82 gave every binding, in every run, a stored type. The one exception was a *temporary*: a value the machine holds between computing it and storing or consuming it (a call's head and earlier arguments while later ones are evaluated, a constructor's earlier fields, an assignment's right-hand side while the old content is dropped, a discarded value, a result across a `let`'s drop, the join or a frame's pop, an `Id` side's result while it is observed). The consistency proof's invariant says that the value at every typed position is a member of its type, so it said nothing of a value in flight. Three faults with that one root are checked in Lean's kernel (`Meaning/Regress/EvalMemGap.lean` on `spine-lead`): no statement concluded that an evaluated value is a member of its type; the statement about a function's return check was false (it returns at once on a value with no dependent constructor, so `0` passes at `Unit`); and the statements about calls held only vacuously when an argument is a borrow, since the borrow's content type, which constrains what may later be written through it, was recorded nowhere while the borrow was in flight.
- **Rule.** A temporary has a stored type, as a binding has: `Frame.tempTys`, beside `Frame.temps`, pushed and popped with it (`pushTemp v T`, every push site saying which `T`). It is the type the machine has in hand when it pushes the value, never one computed for the purpose: the type the value's evaluation returned, or the declared type of the position it is headed for, which the caller holds. In an untyped run it is `none` where the run computed none (D82: an untyped run computes a type only where its context reads it). In a typed run every temporary but a proof token has one. A refinement reaches a temporary's type as it reaches a binding's (`mapState` with types).
- **What it is.** State the checker carries and does not read. `tyAt` of a temporary is still `none`; no decision, message or located error reads `tempTys`. It is kept so that the invariant of the consistency proof is a predicate on the checker's state alone, covering values in flight as it covers bindings. The alternative, the proof carrying the temporaries' types beside the state (`Good` taking them as a parameter, the lead's prototype `TempTys`), was weighed in D81: it threads through 28 files of the proof and states the same thing, with the types supplied from outside the run that has them.
- **The push sites** (18). At the site, the type the evaluation just returned: a `let`'s result across its binding's drop, a constructor's fields, a call's head and arguments, an arm's result across the join (`joinDrop`; `joinCont` in tail position), an `Id` side's result (`observe`), a stuck block's arm result (`closeBlock`), a tail `let`'s result across its drop (`checkTail`, twice), a function body's result across its frame's pop at the generic call (`checkFix`). As a parameter from a caller that holds it: `assignPlace` (the assigned value's type, and [Obs-ref]'s fresh value at the borrow's content type), `dropValue` (the discarded value's), `obsResult` (the observed result's; a borrow result's content at the content of its type `&C`), `endBorrowArgs` (the call's argument types), `runBodyCore` (the call's type `B`, from `callFn`: a known function's body runs untyped, so its result is held across the frame's pop at the declared type of the position it is headed for, the call's result; in an untyped call `B` where the call computed it), and `evalCall` (`given`: a stuck block's call, which `closeBlock` makes untyped, holds its head and its arguments at the block's function type and domains, which `closeBlock` assembled to form the block; an argument's own evaluated type comes first).
- **A proof token may have none.** `lentProofAssign` ([Open]: a proof field assigned while part of its value is lent) checks the proof on a private copy where the lent parts are unknowns; the proposition computed there names that copy's unknowns and is not stored outside it, so the `⋆` is held with no type. A proof token holds no borrow, and its proposition's truth has its own source in the proof.
- **Untyped runs.** A borrow held with no type does occur there, so the proof keeps its separate record for those: counted over the whole suite and the probes (`lake exe sweep --pushes`), 1,384 pushes of a body's returned borrow called from an untyped run where the call computed no type (`runBodyCore`), 167 of an arm's borrow result across the join, 43 of a `let`'s borrow result across its drop, and 17 of an assigned borrow. The smallest: `def LetB (x : &Nat) : &Nat := (let k = 0; x)`, called as `let r = LetB(&y)`, whose body holds `x`'s borrow with no type across `k`'s drop.
- **Why a census of every push.** The sweep's states are recorded only at located terms at call depth 0, so it never sees a callee's body, a re-run or the checker's own scaffolding (a stuck block's call), where two of the typed-run gaps above were. `Config.recordPushes` (dev tooling, off in every verdict) logs every push with no stored type in a typed run, or of a borrow with none, at every call depth; `lake exe sweep --pushes` counts them by site and fails on one in a typed run other than `⋆`. Before `runBodyCore` and `evalCall` took the caller's types it found 12,812 such pushes of a known function's result in typed calls (97 of them borrows: `def RetB (x : &Nat) : &Nat := x`, `let r = RetB(&y)`), 209 heads and 273 arguments of stuck blocks' calls (`def Blk (n : Nat) (x : Nat) : Nat := (let r = match n { Z => x, S _ => Z }; r)`), and the 3 proof tokens above; now only the 3.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main (c4628e33) against this change: identical on all 2,311 rows.
  - New asserted block `Functions.InFlight` (13 rows; count 2,311 → 2,324), one program per kind of value in flight, `RetB`'s and `Blk`'s shapes included. Its guards read the recorded states (every temporary typed, a borrow exactly at a borrow type; exact `value : type` lists for a call two deep, a returned borrow and a constructor's field) and every push at every depth (`inFlightPushes`: none untyped in a typed run).
  - `lake exe sweep`: every clause's counts as on main (72,076 states there; the 111 more and their 4 (WF8) and 4 planted hits are the new block's). Its new clauses hold on every state: in a typed run every temporary but `⋆` has a stored type (47,688 states hold a temporary in a typed run); a temporary's stored type has its value's shape (a borrow at `&C`: 5,344 states; a constructor value at its own inductive: 8,540; a number at `Nat`: 1,724; 3,711 states with a neutral type, not checked); no temporary's stored type holds a loan. `--probes`: the same clause counts as main. `--pushes`: as above.
  - Cost: instructions retired, compiled, on the two heaviest blocks, main against this change: `Quicksort` 168.9G → 169.8G (+0.6%), `HashMapTests` 60.4G → 60.8G (+0.7%). (Wall-clock on the shared machine varied more than that between runs of one binary.)
  - RULES (the stored-types bullet and the history line) and the appendix's environments paragraph and well-formedness condition 5 say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D102. The join's drop decides "do nothing" by the copy decision and the place's existence only (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: drop-proof]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the slice prover's finding). `dropAtJoin` skipped a place whose own value was `⋆` ("a proof: nothing") before asking the copy decision. Since D100 every proof is a copy, so a place the copy decision does not call a copy never holds `⋆` of its own at the join's grain, and the test was dead code that put the drop's "do nothing" on the value a run produced.
- **Change.** A place's own `⋆` goes to the data branch like any value: the copy decision first (a proof is a copy, so it stays), then, for a non-copy place, access and `⊥`. Only a place under a `⋆` ancestor (a part of a proof, `underProof`) is skipped, by its position. A place that does not exist, or holds `⊥`, stays as before. So the drop's "do nothing" is decided by the shared copy decision and by whether the place exists, never by the value it holds. No rule's text changes: RULES [Join] already says a copy stays.
- **Dead, checked.** With a temporary assertion in the data branch (a place's own `⋆` that the copy decision does not call a copy), the assertion never fired: not in the suite, every row in the build, `lake exe sweep` and its `--probes`, or the 24 hand-run probe files.
- **Evidence.** A dump of every row's verdict, message and location, in both located modes, main (c4628e33, D101) against this change, 2,311 rows: identical, editor-note counts included. `lake exe sweep` (72,076 states) and `--probes`: byte-identical from the first state line on. `lake build` is green.

## D105. A constructor's parameter is inferred from its fields only where the model can follow (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-20) [lane: checker-param-infer]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the proof's data layer, docs/32 "Inference of a constructor's parameters"). The checker solves a constructor's unwritten parameter from the types of its fields (`unifyParams`, D46, and in untyped runs D88): it matches each field's declared type against the type of the value given for it, descending through the arguments of inductive type formers. The semantic model reads a parameter back off a value's type only where its records keep it: a type parameter at any depth, and a number-like parameter (`MkW(t)` with `t : Tag(3)` gives `Wrap(3)`), both now followed by the model (`Meaning/DataModel/ParamSolve.lean` on `spine-datamodel`: `sw_ctor`, `so_ctor`). It cannot follow a parameter that stands in the fields' types only at positions of a higher universe: `SH`, `let w = MkHW(t)` with `t : HL(F0)`, solves `HW`'s parameter `f : Π(x : Nat). Type` from `HL(f)`, and the model's run fails there (`sh_fails`). So the checker accepted programs whose meaning the model could not give.
- **Rule.** An unwritten parameter is inferred from a field's type only where it stands as the field's whole type (`x : A`, at any level), or, at any depth, as an argument at a position of an inductive type former declared `Prop` or `Type₀`, or declared a type in `Prop` or `Type₀` (a proposition or a type of data, a proof, a number, a data value). A parameter that stands only at positions declared `Type₁` or above, a value of a type in `Type₁` or above, or a function into a universe, is not inferred: it must be written, or come from the type the context expects. Otherwise the definition is rejected: "cannot infer the parameter f of MkHW: it stands in its fields' types only at positions of a universe above Type₀ (…), where a parameter is not inferred (D105): write it, MkHW[…](…), or annotate, (MkHW(…) : HW(…))".
- **A decision.** Which positions count is read from the declarations, never from a run: `Decide.inferablePos` (a parameter's declared type is a sort `Prop`/`Type₀`, or a type whose sort, `typePos` in the scope of the earlier parameters, is `Prop` or `Type₀`), and `Decide.inferFrom`, which cuts a field type's arguments at every other position before `unifyParams` matches it. A descent stops at the first position that does not count; with no cumulativity, nothing below such a position is an inductive type of data anyway, except a value of a type in `Type₁` (`LV(Type, Sl(A))`), which is cut with it. The same cut is made wherever the checker infers parameters from fields: typing and untyped runs alike (`evalCtor`), the type of a constructor value that records none (`indValType?`), and D89's test of whether a constructor's fields determine its parameters (`ctorNeedsCtx`), so that an untyped run takes its context's type exactly where typing needs it.
- **D89's test, at every field (the same change).** `ctorNeedsCtx` decides whether an untyped run needs the type its context declares to give a constructor its parameters (D89): when some parameter is determined by no field. It read field `i`'s parameter `k` as `var (i + np - 1 - k)`, but in every field's type the parameters are `var 0 … var (np - 1)`, the last `var 0`, whatever the field's position (the fields come after them, `IndDecl.fieldRefs`); the reading was right only for the first field. So with `inductive R (A : Type) (B : Type) := MkR(x : A, y : A)` the second field, of type `A`, read as determining the phantom `B`, the run took no context type, and `MkIt(1)` (`def MkIt (a : Nat) : R(Nat, Bool2) := MkR(clone(a), a)`) built `MkR(1, 1)` recording no parameters inside the untyped run of its body, a bare constructor value at call depth > 0, where the sweep never looks ((WF10) broken; printed from the state at a hole). No verdict was affected (`Eq` does not read the missing parameters; `UseMkIt` was accepted before and is now). It now reads `var (np - 1 - k)` at every field, and the proof test of a field's type likewise. The other direction of the same mistake made the test ask for the context type where the fields did determine the parameters, so untyped runs computed a declared codomain they did not need: the fix makes checking about 13% cheaper.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main (9eea122f, D110) against this change: identical on every earlier row (locations later in `Inductives.lean` shift by the inserted block).
  - New asserted block `Inductives.ParamInference` (31 rows; count 2,348 → 2,379): `SH`, `ST`, `SV` rejected with the D105 message, each beside its written (`…w`) and annotated (`…a`) rewrite, accepted; `SC`, `SW`, `SO` and a nested `SN` accepted; `R`, `MkIt`, `UseMkIt` and the correctly indexed `R2`, `MkIt2`. A guard reads the state at a hole after `let v = MkIt(1); let w = MkIt2(1)`: both values record their two parameters (before: `v` recorded none).
  - `lake exe sweep`: every clause's counts as before (the 290 more states are the new rows'; they add only to the planted clause). `--probes` the same; `--pushes` clean.
  - The model's side (`Meaning/DataModel/ParamSolve.lean` on `spine-datamodel`): `SC`, `SW`, `SO` run to the checker's answer; `SH` fails there (`sh_fails`) and is now rejected.
  - Cost: instructions retired, main against this change, `Quicksort` 169.8G → 147.1G (−13%), `HashMapTests` 60.8G → 52.5G (−14%), from the index fix (fewer declared codomains computed in untyped runs).
  - RULES (§9 declarations; the history line) and the appendix's [T-Ctor] say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D106. No access ends a borrow a value in flight holds (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: checker-join-inflight]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the proof's slice prover, `Meaning/Regress/JoinPrefixBorrow.lean` on `spine-slice`). At a match's join the checker drops the places another arm moved while the arm's result is in flight as a temporary. D65's check in `dropAtJoin` rejected a drop whose own value held a loan of a borrow a temporary holds, but when the dropped place lies under a prefix lent to such a borrow, `movedPlace` reaches it by accessing the prefix, which ended the borrow with no check. In tail position on a known scrutinee (`TK`: `let r0 = &*x; let k = Z; match k { Z => r0, S m => (let a = (*x).1; y) }` over borrow parameters) the join's access ended the result's borrow and the function returned `⊥ : &P`; it was rejected only later, by D53, when the parameter's borrow ended over a partly moved content. One rule's soundness rested on an unrelated rule firing afterwards. The proof needs the guarantee stated once: no step that accesses places while a value is in flight ends a borrow that value holds.
- **Measured first.** Each of the three steps that access places with a value in flight (the join's drop, the drop of a `let`'s binding while its result is in flight, a frame's pop while the body's result is parked below it) was instrumented to log any end of a label a temporary of any frame held when the step began (a scratch build, not committed), over the suite, the sweep's probes and the slice's thirteen programs (2,357 rows). Exactly two definitions did it, both rejected already: `TK` (the join; D53) and `DepVec.VGetP` (the generic call's pop; its [Repack] reason). The `let`'s drop never did. `TU`, the same as `TK` on an unknown scrutinee, is split at the generic call, not joined: D53 rejects its moving arm itself. A sweep clause then found a fourth path, an argument: `f(&x, x)`, whose second argument's read ends the first argument's borrow, which [Call] rejected only at the call point, after the later arguments.
- **Rule, at the one place every access ends a borrow.** `endTopLoan`, through which every [Access] (the path, the content, a neutral head; the join's drop and `movedPlace`'s access of a lent prefix; a later argument's read) ends a borrow, rejects ending one that a temporary holds, outside a private copy: `[Access] reading or writing p ends a borrow that a value in flight holds (an earlier argument of a call, or a result on its way out): it would hold a borrow that has ended`. Outside private copies because a statement's run on its copy reads the current content and ends a borrow on the copy only, which is discarded: `SubM(&*x, 0, LeZero(*x))`, whose proof argument reads `*x` while the first argument borrows it, is ordinary accepted code and stays accepted. The ends that do not go through an access end the very value being dropped (a `let`'s borrow binding, `t; u`'s discarded value, a call's unrun borrow arguments, an overwritten borrow) and are not such steps.
- **One rule, one place.** The earlier checks, each against `endTopLoan`'s:
  - D65's check in `dropAtJoin` ("[Join] q is moved by another arm …, while the arm's result borrows it") and in `endLoansOfTopBind`/`endLoansOfTopTemp` ("[Drop] x dies while a value in flight borrows it"): kept. Each is the same condition on the loans inside the dropped value (a loan whose borrow `findBorrow` finds at a temporary, which the drop's access would pass to `endTopLoan`), tested just before that access with the drop's own message, which asserted rows name (`JoinPrefix.NK2`, `Borrows`, `Drops`). `endTopLoan` covers what they do not: a borrow lent at a prefix of the dropped place.
  - The checks after a join's drop and a `let`'s drop (built first, as post-checks of the step), and after each argument in `evalCall`: removed. `endTopLoan` fires first, during the step, so they could never fire.
  - [Call]'s check at the call point ("argument i is ⊥ at the call point"): kept, now reached only inside a private copy, where the access is not rejected: a statement call's later argument ending an earlier one's borrow on the copy (`Borrows.attack'`, still rejected by it).
- **Messages that change** (no verdict changes): `JoinPrefix.TK`, from D53's to [Access]'s; `Borrows.Dead` and `DeadTwice` (`f(&x, x)`), from [Call]'s at the call point to [Access]'s, located at the read that ends the borrow (inside the later argument) rather than at the whole argument, with fewer editor notes (the later arguments are no longer run).
- **The sweep clause** (`tempLive`): outside a private copy, no temporary at a borrow type (D104) has lost its borrow. Inside copies 54 states hold one (in `CurrentState.TwoPhase`, `Arrays.FillFrom`, `Quicksort.Scan`, `Quicksort.ScanPerm`, `HashMap.MoveFrom`, `DepVec.VMoveCells` and the rejected `Borrows.attack'`), counted by a premise.
- **Early ends** (the proof lead's question after D110, asked here): with D110 in place, no path that ends before its return can leave a caller's value open at an instance. Opening a caller's value through a borrow parameter and then ending on a zero-arm match is accepted only on something empty the function was given, unreachable at every instance (`EarlyEnd.E1` to `E3`, `E3C`); on the opened field itself, moved out or an invalidated proof field it is rejected (`E4` to `E6`), and a tail call passing the borrow on, or a normal end, meets [Repack] (`E8` to `E12`). Only a manufactured emptiness, an opened field read at its declared type, made an early end dangerous (D110's `ViaParam`).
- **Evidence.**
  - Measured at the new place, at every call depth: the check is in the checker, so `lake build` runs it on every definition of the suite and the case studies at every depth (callee bodies, re-runs), and on the sweep's probes; no accepted definition reaches it (the build is green with every accepted verdict as before).
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main (2a1c31c5, D105) against this change: identical but for the three rows above (locations later in `ReturnedBorrows.lean` and `DependentFields.lean` shift by the inserted blocks).
  - New asserted blocks: `ReturnedBorrows.JoinPrefix` (the slice's thirteen programs; 16 rows) and `DependentFields.EarlyEnd` (the twelve early ends; 20 rows); count 2,379 → 2,415.
  - `lake exe sweep`: the new clause holds on every state (73,129; 5,416 with a temporary at a borrow type); every other clause's counts as before (the new blocks' states aside). `--probes` the same; `--pushes` clean.
  - Cost: instructions retired on `Quicksort` and `HashMapTests` within 0.01% of before.
  - RULES ([Access], [Drop], [Join], [Call] and the history line) and the appendix ([Access], [Drop], [Join], the arguments) say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D110. A strong update stores only a value whose content determines its type (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: checker-strong-update]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the agent attacking dependent values, `dep1`, reproduced on main f34cfe8a). An assignment to a dependent data field is a strong update ([Open], D64): the value is stored unchecked, and the field is afterwards typed by what it holds (`depFieldType`), until [Repack] checks the value where it must be whole again. Any value could be stored. `x := refl` stored `⋆` in a data field; `depFieldType` gave a field holding `⋆` its declared type at the current earlier fields with no check, so after `n := Z` the field read at `Fin1(Z) = Void`, and a zero-arm match on it closed the goal. The value was a local that is dropped, so no [Repack] point was reached: `Bad : Void` and `BoomF : False := AbsurdV(Bad)` were accepted, a closed proof of `False`. A borrow could be stored inside the value too (`Leak : Nat`, whose value was a loan): nothing looks for borrows inside data, so the write through it was lost and its end never wrote back.
- **Rule.** A strong update may store only a value whose declared type reads as first-order data, the class a field may be declared at (D36): never a proof, a borrow, a type or a function (`Decide.strongStorable`, from the right-hand side's declared type, `declOf`, never from its value or computed type). Any other value is an ordinary checked write against the field's declared type at the current contents of the earlier fields (`fieldDeclType`), and fails as a type mismatch ("the assigned value has type ⊤, expected One"). "Typed by what it holds" is meaningful only for a value whose content determines its type, which `⋆`, a borrow, a type and a closure do not.
- **`depFieldType`.** A data field never holds `⋆` after the rule, so a dependent data field holding `⋆` is an internal error, not given its declared type; a proof field holds `⋆` at its declared type, as before, and a moved field (`⊥`) keeps its declared type (any read of it is an error).
- **The class, hunted before fixing** (each a row of `DependentFields.StrongUpdate`, all accepted before, all rejected now):
  - any proof: `refl` (`Bad`, and with the two writes swapped, `BadSwap`), a proof parameter (`ViaProofParam`, with its own closed `False` at `refl`), a proof of an arbitrary proposition (`ViaAnyProof`);
  - through a borrow parameter, with no local value: `ViaParam (r : &V) : Void := ((*r).1 := refl; (*r).0 := Z; match (*r).1 {})`, and its closed instance `AbsurdV(ViaParam(&c))` was a third proof of `False`: the zero-arm match ends the only path, so the borrow's [Repack] at the return is never reached (a path that returns is rejected by it). Likewise through a borrow a call returns (`ViaReturned`);
  - through the borrow `Leak` stores: overwriting it ends a borrow `findBorrow` cannot find inside the value, so its lender keeps a loan nothing will fill (breaking (WF2)); read back as data of the field's type (`x := w`), whose type `valType?` cannot read, it took the declared type, and a fourth closed `False` followed (`ViaLoan`). The rule closes it at the borrow's store;
  - a type (`AType`) and a function (`AFunction`) were stored too; no `False` was found through them (a match on the field is rejected: not an inductive type).
  Not reachable before either: a moved field matched after its index changed ("[Match] on c.x, which was moved out"), `⋆` in a `Nat` field matched ("[Match] on a non-Nat value ⋆", found by the run).
- **Every other write of a typed run is checked or writes no new value.** Listed from every `setPlace`, `setAt` and `mapState` site: assignments (refills after a move, a stuck block's arms, `J`'s transported term) go through `.assign`'s check, a write through a borrow `*r := v` against the content of `r`'s stored `&T`; the rest write `⊥` (reads, the join, invalidated proof fields), a loan or a mark, or a value of its own type by construction (refinements, η, generalisation, [Obs-ref]'s fresh value, [Close]'s fills). [End]'s write-back is re-checked by [Repack] when the borrow's holder is a binding; a temporary holding a borrow is never written through (it is not a place root). Untyped runs check nothing by design (D82).
- **Kept, flagged.** `depFieldType`'s fallback for a content whose type cannot be read (`valType?` none) is still the declared type; the one such content found, an inert loan, can no longer reach a data field.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main against this change: identical on every earlier row (locations later in `DependentFields.lean` shift by the inserted block). The suite never reaches the new internal error. No case study stores a forbidden value.
  - New asserted block `DependentFields.StrongUpdate` (24 rows; count 2,324 → 2,348), every rejection with its message.
  - `lake exe sweep`: every clause's counts as before; the 247 more states and their hits on (WF8) and the planted clauses are the new block's. `--probes` the same; `--pushes` clean.
  - Cost: instructions retired on `Quicksort` and `HashMapTests` within 0.01% of before.
  - RULES [Open] (what a strong update may store) and the history line, and the appendix's [Open] paragraph, say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D103. A comparison keeps nothing but names (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: conv-restore]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the rules trial's finding, amended by the proof lane). D37 said two things: fresh names are never reused, and records survive a private copy. D103 keeps the first for every private computation and withdraws the second for comparisons, whose result is a boolean and can mention nothing they made.
- **The leak.** `conv`, `convPi` and `convFn` run on a private copy, and they kept what the copy made (`restoreKeep`): new unknowns, the counters, and also η- and generalisation records and changes to [Rec]'s candidate lists. A typed run of a parameter type at the generic argument refines an unknown of a one-constructor type in place (D93) and records `σ := C(σ̄)`. Comparing `Π(x : E1). match x { MkE1(e) => Nat }` with `Π(y : E1). Nat`, over `E1 := MkE1(e : Empty)`, therefore left a record naming an unknown of type `Empty` (`Meaning/Rules/EmptyDomain.lean` on `rules-trial`). After it, no assignment of the unknowns is typed, which falsifies an invariant of the consistency proof at a reachable state.
- **Rule.** On every exit from the three, failure included (`onFail`), the state is put back to the one saved on entry, except for the names made inside (`restoreNames`): `nextAbs`, `nextLoan`, `nextRecUid`, the frozen readings `absDecl`, and the tail of `absTy` (the types of the unknowns made inside, which nothing can reference afterwards). The records and the candidate lists are the saved ones. Fuel and the log are outside the machine state and untouched.
- **Not a full restore** (the trial's first patch). Rewinding `nextAbs` and `absDecl` would reuse an unknown's index, made inside the comparison, later with another reading. The proof relies on one table of frozen readings answering for the whole run.
- **`mkEqM`, `evalType` and the other private computations** keep `restoreKeep`, since their result can name what they made. They are untouched, and no verdict that involves them changes (below).
- **Stability proofs.** `refSP_succ`'s cases for the three are re-proved with no `sorry`, by one new lemma, `EndsAt.restoreNames_bind`: a run that ends with `restoreNames s₀` ends with `s₀`'s signature, since it changes neither the inductives nor the globals. Each case is `get_ends`, `bind_any` and that lemma.
- **How often a comparison's copy made a record or changed a candidate list: never.** A temporary assertion at each exit of the three compared what the old code would have kept (the state after the copy's `restoreKeep`) with the saved one: η-records, generalisation records, and [Rec]'s candidate lists. It never fired in the dump of all 2,324 rows, `lake exe sweep`, `--probes` or the 24 hand-run probe files. That agrees with the trial's count of zero, so in the suite the leak occurs only through a direct call of `conv`, as below.
- **Regression row** (`Units.lean`, `CompareEmpty`, on the checker's state at a hole after the block's declarations). `conv` of `F`, `G : Π(x : Empty). Nat` answers `true` and adds one unknown and no record, as before. `conv` of `Π(x : E1). match x { MkE1(e) => Nat }` and `Π(y : E1). Nat`, and of `F3`, `G3` (a later parameter's type matches on the generic argument), each answer `true` and add their unknowns. They now leave no record; on main each left one, naming the unknown of type `Empty`, and these guards fail there.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message and location, in both located modes, main (b71a0da1) against this change, 2,324 rows: identical, editor-note counts included, so messages and located errors raised inside comparisons are unchanged. `mkEqM` and `evalType`, which keep `restoreKeep`, change no verdict.
  - `lake exe sweep` (72,187 states) and `--probes`: byte-identical from the first state line on.
  - Check time, the examples' build with every verdict re-checked, interleaved on an idle machine: 186.7 and 186.7 s on main, 188.2 and 187.7 s with D103, about 0.5%. A third pair (191.3 s against 232.5 s) is not comparable: the load rose from 1.1 to 2.8 as the second of the pair started. The cost is one state copy per comparison, as `privately` already makes.
  - RULES P1 and the history line, and the appendix's note on generalisation records surviving private copies, say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D107. The nesting condition covers the type being declared; nestable parameters are a greatest fixed point (2026-11-21) [lane: positivity-own-occurrences]
Found by the data-model existence lane (`exists1`), trying to build a set model for every accepted declaration: the checker accepted a closed proof of `False`. Smallest form (every declaration accepted, no holes):
```
def Neg (X : Type) (n : Nat) : Prop := (match n { Z => False, S _ => (Π(x : X). False) })
inductive Q (A : Type) (n : Nat) := MkQ(z : Q(Q(A, n), 1), e : Eq(Nat, n, 0)) | Stop(f : Neg(A, n))
def Delta (x : Q(Unit, 0)) : False := (match x { MkQ(z, e) => match z { MkQ(z2, e2) => e2, Stop(f) => f(x) }, Stop(f) => f })
def Boom : False := Delta(MkQ[Unit, 0](Stop[Q(Unit, 0), 1](Delta), refl))
```
`Q(Unit, 0)` is `Q(Unit, 0) → False` (Curry's paradox). Two conditions, each allowed alone, let `Q` through. `Neg(A, n)` is a function type only at a successor and stuck at the generic `n`, so K4's "a type-function field does not compute to a Π" does not fire, and `Q`'s parameter `A` is used negatively, which is harmless on its own (D64). And `Q(Q(A, n), 1)` nests `Q` at `Q`'s own parameter `A`. K4's nesting condition, which would have refused that (a parameter passed to a type function is not nestable), was skipped for the type being declared (`m != n` in `nestViolationArgs` and `firstOrderArgs`), because while `Q` is checked its entry in Σ has no constructors, so asking `paramNestable` about it answers "nestable" vacuously. `Delta` is a proof, so `Boom`'s call on its own encoding is never run. The finder's original (with `Bit`, an option type whose "none" is empty at `B0`, and a lemma) and three variants reaching the same hole through a chain of inductives, a parameter that is a type function, and a proof field are asserted as found (`Inductives.PositivityCurry`, `PositivityChain`, `PositivityTyFn`, `PositivityPropRoute`).
- **Finding 1 of the hunt, a second hole.** For a declared inductive, `paramNestable` counted every own occurrence as nestable whatever position it carried the parameter to (`if m == d.name then …`). `E (A : Type) (B : Type) (n : Nat) := MkE(z : E(B, A, 1), e : Eq(Nat, n, 0)) | StopE(f : Neg(A, n))` uses `B` only as `E(B, A, 1)`, at `A`'s position, and `A` goes to `Neg`; `B` was judged nestable, so `Bad := MkBad(e : E(Unit, Bad, 0))` was accepted, `Bad ≅ Bad → False`, with a closed `Boom` (`PositivitySwap`).
- **Finding 2, the same root, no proof of `False`.** The constructor-less entry was also read by the zero-arm match: a value argument in a field type is any term not mentioning `D`, and `match b {}` names no constructor, so `E := Leaf | Node(b : E, w : W(let k : False = match b {}; k))` (`W (h : False) := MkW`) was accepted with a field type that is ill-typed in the final Σ. It failed safe in use (building a `Node` re-evaluates the field type and is rejected), but the declaration has no set model as written. The same through a proposition being declared (a match by type) and through a list built without naming the type (`Cons(b, Nil)`, then a match with no arms on its head) were accepted too (`PositivityDeclaring`).
- **Rule.** *The nestable parameters* `N(M)` of an inductive `M` are the greatest set of its type parameters such that each `i ∈ N(M)` occurs in `M`'s field types only as a whole field type, or inside an argument of an inductive `M'(…)` at a parameter in `N(M')`, nestably again inside that argument (`M' = M` included, at the set itself). A parameter passed to a type function, an equation, a value argument or a `Π`, or carried to a parameter outside the set, is not nestable; a value parameter never is. `Check.nestableSet` computes it from "every type parameter" by removing, each round, those that occur elsewhere, until a round removes none (at most one round per parameter); [Ind-decl] stores it with the declaration (`IndDecl.nestable`), read by every later check. *The nesting condition*: the type being declared `D` may occur in `M(…)` only at a parameter in `N(M)`, `M = D` included, with `N(D)` computed from the fields being declared, before `D` enters Σ. Every reader goes through one table (`NestTable`): `nestViolation` (the message) and `firstOrderArgs`. *While `D` is declared* its entry is marked (`IndDecl.declaring`): a term that reads `D`'s constructors is rejected with one message, "… reads the constructors of D, which are not known while D is declared (D107)", and a decision that reads them and cannot reject answers conservatively. The walk is structural over terms and the declaration list is never re-walked, so the old fuel bound (`paramNestableF`) is gone: nesting can no longer be `undecided`.
- **The readers of the constructor-less entry** (finding 2's class, closed as the lead asked: every reader of an inductive's constructors that can run while `D`'s fields are checked). Field types are evaluated before the syntactic checks (`firstOrderTerm`, the nesting condition), so the whole machine is in play; but a term that names `D` other than as a type former at a type parameter is rejected by those checks whatever the evaluation answered. What matters is what an erased term over an earlier field of type `D` reaches without naming it. Each reader, what it answered for the entry before, and now (one asserted program each in `PositivityDeclaring`):
  - a match with no arms, on data or by type on a proposition: vacuous, accepted (`E`, `EP`, `EL`). Now rejected in `scrutType`, which every typed match on a place goes through.
  - a match with arms, and [Split] on an unknown of `D`: zero worlds, or "internal: a match with no arms" (it names `D`, so the declaration was rejected anyway). Now the same message (`EA`).
  - a field projection `b.0`: "D has 0 constructors". Now the same message (`EJ`).
  - a constructor of `D` (it names `D`): "D has no constructor 0". Now the same message (`EC`). `indValType?` and `split f` at a result of type `D` take it too, though neither is reachable without naming `D`.
  - the copy decision (`declCopy`): a copy, vacuously (no constructors, so no non-copy field). Now not a copy unless declared `copy` (`ECopy`: the type itself counts, D53). Not reachable through a read today: an earlier field's reading is `other` while the fields are checked, so a second read of it is a use after move (`ED`, rejected on main and here).
  - large elimination (`largeElim`): a subsingleton, vacuously. Now not one; reached only through `scrutType`, which rejects first.
  - η at a one-constructor type, the join's grain (D84, D93, `jdGrain`: `ctors.length == 1`), a field's declared reading (`fieldDecl`, `fieldAt`): "not one constructor", `other`. Unchanged: already the conservative answer.
  - readers of a constructor value of `D` (`Eq`'s disjointness and injectivity, `hasDependent`, `loanPath`, the join's children, a value checked against a type): unreachable, since no value of `D` can be built without naming it.
  - the sort, `abstract`, `unsized`, the parameters: kept in the entry, and correct.
  - the nestable sets: no longer read from the entry (`ownSet`).
  No reader gave a proof of `False`. Accepted twins: a match with no arms on a proof of `False` that is a parameter (`EOk`), one read of the earlier field (`EOnce`), `clone` of it (`EClone`).
- **Why the greatest fixed point, and why stored.** "An own occurrence carries parameter `i` only at position `i`" is a sound under-approximation and passes the whole battery, but the greatest set is what the model's condition names and is no more code; it also accepts a swap that keeps both parameters positive (`Alt (A) (B) := ANil | ACons(a : A, rest : Alt(B, A))`, then `Zig := MkZig(z : Alt(Zig, Nat))`). The lead asked for one function for both uses and the answer computed once at declaration: a declared inductive's set cannot change (it reads only earlier declarations), so storing it is declaration data, not a cache of a run (D95's distinction), and it removed the fuel.
- **Against the model's condition** (`exists1`, `DataModel/Exists/Positivity.lean` on `spine-exists`): the condition is "every occurrence of `D` is a whole field type or an argument of a former `M'(…)` at `j ∈ N(M')`, `M' = D` included, `N` the greatest set as above". The implemented rule is that condition, read on paths: every former between the root of a field type and the occurrence must be at a nestable position (the spec's "argument of a former" read innermost-only would also allow `F(List(A))` under a call; that is rejected here, and by `firstOrderTerm` anyway). Neither optional relaxation is taken: no program of the suite needs either. Both are known to be sound for the model: an `Eq` field's type argument as a nestable position (the model covers `G1 (A) (a : A) := MkG1(x : A, e : Eq(A, x, a))` nested at `A`), and value arguments, which cannot mention `D` anyway. The model also confirms the accepted positive case `G1 (A : Type) (a : A) := MkG1(x : A)` nested at `A` through `T1 := Leaf1 | Node1(t : T1, g : G1(T1, t))`: a parameter that types a value parameter is not a field-type occurrence.
- **Hunted and correctly rejected** (asserted in `PositivityRoutes`, with twins): nestability through a chain of inductives, at a computed value parameter, under `Box`; a type function at the declared type directly, under `Box`, at an index field, at a nested occurrence; the type under `Eq`, under `&`; a parameter that is a type function applied in a field, applied by a global (`H2(NegT, Bad10)`), or given a lambda mentioning the type. Curry in `Prop` (`QP (A : Prop) (n : Nat) : Prop`) went through the same hole and is now rejected (`PositivitySelf`). Accepted and positive: a value parameter typed by a type parameter with the type nested at it (`G1 (A : Type) (a : A)`, `T1 := Leaf1 | Node1(t : T1, g : G1(T1, t))`). No forward shape exists: a type function or inductive must be declared before it is used, and an inductive's set reads only earlier declarations.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict and message, main (6d2cc84e) against this change (2,379 rows on main): two rows change, only their message, `DepFields.Bad` and `FordAttack.Loop` (K4's message now says "which is not nestable in M", and names D107; it said "passes to a type function", which for `Loop`'s equation was not even true). The other 2,377 are identical; no verdict changes.
  - The 195 new rows, run on main's checker: 29 flip from accepted to rejected, every one intended: the paradox declarations `Q`, `QP`, `QG`, `QN`, `Bad` and the definitions over them (`Delta`, `Lem`, `Boom` in each block), and the constructor readers `E`, `EP`, `EL`. Every other new row has the same verdict on both.
  - New asserted blocks in `Inductives.lean` (191 rows): `PositivitySelf` (the smallest form, its Prop twin, accepted twins), `PositivityCurry`, `PositivityChain`, `PositivityTyFn`, `PositivityPropRoute` (the finder's four programs, verbatim), `PositivitySwap` (finding 1, with `Alt`/`Zig` for the greatest set) and `PositivitySecondHole` (finding 1 as the model's battery states it), `PositivityRoutes` (the hunt's rejected routes and accepted twins), `PositivityDeclaring` (finding 2 and its class, one program per reader), `PositivityKeep` (the battery that must stay accepted: `DD(DD(A))`, `RN`, `BB`, `RP`, `NN`, `HN`, `Ev`/`WithEv`, `TF`, `FBox`, and declarations nested at their nestable parameters), `PositivityProbesNest`, `PositivityProbesLarge`, `PositivityProbesAdv` (the data model's probes, verbatim from `spine-datamodel`), `PositivityControls`. In `DependentFields.lean`, the paper's form of the paradox over `NegIf` and `IsSucc` (`QN`, `QNOk`, `DeltaQ`, `BoomQ`; checked to be a closed proof of `False` on main). Count 2,379 → 2,574.
  - `lake exe sweep` over main's 107 blocks: every clause's counts as on main except DepFields'. DepFields alone accounts for every difference: its four new rows add 74 states, all in clauses already violated or holding on main, with no new violation kind. `--probes`: identical to main.
  - RULES §8 and §9 (and the history line), the paper's appendix ([Ind-decl]'s nesting sentence and the side-condition table, a new row), `notes/inductive-model.md` (its "no hole" corrected), `notes/totality-census.md`, the checker README. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D112. A dependent field that cannot be typed by what it holds is a leaf, treated as a proof field (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: dep-leaf-fields]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the attack on dependent values, `dep1`; route B of its report). While a value of a dependent constructor is open ([Open], D64), a field whose content does not fit its declared type at the current earlier fields is typed by what it holds. The set model cannot follow that for every content. A constructor value of a data inductive is a tree that records its declaration and record, so it says which type it is a value of, and the model reads it at that type. A number, the unit value or a function records only its Lean carrier (`Code0.enc (.pi C _) f = .el0 C f`), which does not determine its type's code, so the model reads such a LEAF at its field's declared type at the current earlier fields. Kernel-checked on the proof's line (`Meaning/Dep/Records.lean`, branch `spine-dep`): at EVERY data model the model's read type of a leaf field ignores the leaf (`leaf_read_type_blind`), while the checker read `5` at `Nat` and `()` at `Unit` under the same index, both in accepted programs; so at one of the two the simulation's type agreement was false in every model. Making leaves self-typed would put a new form of meaning for functions, at three levels, into proved code, for a freedom no program of the suite or the case studies uses. The checker changes instead, and the language's rule becomes the model's design: only trees describe themselves.
- **Rule.** A field whose declared type mentions earlier fields is a *tree field* when that type, read through the bodies of the type functions it calls (`Decide.typeDecl`), is a data inductive at every instance (`DeclInfo.isTree`: `Fin1(n)` reads as `Void` or `One`, `Array(E, n)` as `ArrayOf`), and a *leaf field* otherwise (a proof field aside): `Payload(b)` that is `Nat` or `Unit`, `NegIf(n, A)` that is `Unit` or a function type, a type parameter (`Decide.fieldIsLeaf`, decided from the declaration alone).
  - A tree field is unchanged: typed by what it holds while open, and assigned by a strong update. A strong update stores unchecked only a value whose declared type reads as a tree (`strongStorable`, refining D110's "first-order data"): a number, the unit value or a function is now a checked write too.
  - A leaf field is treated as a proof field: a write or borrow through an index field its type mentions invalidates it (`openWrite`: `⊥`, a lent leaf's borrows ended first, as a write to it would end them); an assignment is checked against its type at the current contents, on a private copy with each lent part an unknown while part of the value is lent (`lentLeafCheck`, as `lentProofAssign` for a proof); it is never typed by its content (`depFieldType`, where a leaf holding a value not of its declared type is an internal error).
  - The fallback D110 left flagged is closed: a tree field whose content fits neither its declared type nor has a readable type of its own is an error, no longer given the declared type.
- **Invalidation at a match's entry, on every path.** A stuck block's capture by `&` of an index field ran `openWrite` (the capture is evaluated as a borrow); a direct run's [Entry] (D90, D91) only accessed the place. At `TPDrop (k : Word) (c0 : Pos)`, a match whose one arm writes `c.n := c.n`, the generic state after the match was `MkPos(⌈…⌉, ⊥)` and the direct run at a known `k` left `MkPos(σ1, ⋆)`; the model runs the arm and could not follow the generic path. [Entry] now invalidates for every capture by `&` (`entryAccess`), on both paths: a proof (or leaf) read after a match one of whose arms writes or borrows its index is rejected whichever arm runs (`EntryKnown`), as it already was when the match closed off (`EntryStuck`).
- **What it rejects, and the rewrite** (each pair in `DependentFields.LeafFields`, accepted on main before):
  - reading a leaf after its index changed (`ReadAfter`, `CallAfter`): take it out first (`TakeOld`, `CallBefore`);
  - writing a leaf before its index (`PayloadFirst`): write the index first (`TagFirst`); a tagged value's tag and payload are still changed in place in that order, and a whole write is unaffected;
  - a borrow of a leaf used after its index changed (`LentUsedAfter`): use it first (`LentUsedBefore`);
  - a number parked in a tree field (`ParkNumber`): leave the field moved out (`ParkMoved`);
  - a proof read after a match one of whose arms writes its index, at a known scrutinee (`EntryKnown`): read it before (`EntryKnownBefore`).
  Not changed: a tree field read, borrowed or strongly updated while open (the vector's `SetTwoRev`, `buf := Two` before `cap := W(2)`), proof fields, whole writes.
- **The uniform variant was refuted** before choosing: invalidating every dependent field, trees included, on an index write (an open value is then only a partly moved one) changed at least 22 verdicts of `DependentFields.lean` in a prototype: every `n := n` twin, every restore-the-index twin, `LendIndex`, and the either-order writes the user ruled for in D64, among them the vector's `SetTwoRev`.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main (43ae3961) against this change (4,696 and 4,964 lines): every earlier row identical but `StrongUpdate.OtherData`, accepted before and now rejected ("the assigned value has type One, expected Nat": `Fin3(n)` is `Nat` at every instance, so `x` is a leaf field; D110's row asserted that data of another type is stored, which now holds of a constructor value, `OtherTree`), and `OtherDataWhole`, rejected before at the [Repack] of the whole value and now at the write. `Leak`, `AType` and `AFunction`, in the same leaf field, keep their verdict and message with two fewer editor notes; locations later in `DependentFields.lean` shift. No case study changes.
  - New asserted blocks `DependentFields.LeafFields` (35 rows: each rejected shape beside its rewrite, the tagged-value programs and their runs by `refl`, a leaf assigned while its index is lent (`LentIndexLie`: checked only at the borrow's current content, `x := 4` would pass at `Nat` and leave `4` under the index `B1` the borrow then writes, where the field's type is `Void`; `BoomLent`), a function in a leaf field, the entry pair) and `OpenAttacks` (96 rows: the dependent-values lane's attack programs, `Meaning/Dep/HANDOFF.md` on `spine-dep`: reads, borrows, calls, constructors, `Eq` and `Id` after an index rewrite, joins, recursion, nested values, proofs re-assigned while lent to a call's returned borrow, closures, stuck blocks, closed instances, a false equation as the control, strong updates of every kind of value), and three rows in `StrongUpdate` (`Two`, `OtherTree`, `OtherTreeWhole`). Count 2,610 → 2,744 (on main after D106 and D107). On main, 19 of the new rows were accepted, every one a rejected shape or its run (`PayloadFirst`, `ReadAfter`, `LentUsedAfter`, `CallAfter`, `ParkNumber`, `EntryKnown`; `L1Nat`, `L2b`, `L3`, `L4`, `L5`, `F1`, `W1`, `W2`, `W3` and the runs `L1NatRuns`, `L3Runs`, `F1Runs`, `W1r`); every other new row has the same verdict there.
  - `lake exe sweep` and `--probes`, main against this change: every clause's and premise's count identical once the states of the new and changed blocks are set aside (the totals differ by exactly the counts of `LeafFields`, `OpenAttacks` and `StrongUpdate` swept alone on each side; (WF8) gains 52 states there, all in those blocks). `--pushes`: no value in flight without a type in a typed run. The probe files under `probes/`: the same output on both sides.
  - Cost: instructions retired, pinned to one core, on `HashMapTests` 60.79 → 60.81 billion (+0.03%), on `Quicksort` unchanged (169.82 billion, within 0.01%).
  - A prototype deciding the same rule from the written value's computed type changed no verdict of the suite either; the landed rule reads declared readings only (`declOf`, `typeDecl`), as decisions must.
  - RULES [Open] and the history line, and the appendix's [Open] paragraph, say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.
- **For the model** (`spine-dep`): a leaf is read at its field's declared type at the current earlier fields, which the rule now makes its type; a tree at its own record. An open value is a tree whose fields are each moved, a tree open at its own type, or a leaf of its declared type (`Meaning/Dep/OMem.lean`); the reference run must mirror the invalidation in its write and borrow clauses and at a match's entry.
- **Amended (2026-11-21): typing reads through a mark.** A field lent to the borrow a call returns holds a mark over its content (`lent_g`, D90, D99). Paths and the repack read through a mark (`lentUnknown`), but `valType?` had no case for one. Found by the red team on main at 14aeb9cd, before D112: a field so lent took its declared type at a rewritten index through the fallback D112 then closed (`A2 : Void := (let c = MkV(S(Z), O); match c { MkV(n, x) => (let r = Keep(&x); n := Z; let k : Void = x; k) })`, with `Keep (r : &One) : &One := r`; a closed proof of `False` through `Boom := AbsurdV(A2)`). After D112 the same blind spot rejected ordinary reads of such a field, one of them with D112's internal assertion (`LeafLegit`, `LeafLegit2`: "INTERNAL [Open] the leaf field p of c holds lent_1 loan_1…"; `TreeLegit`: "[Open] c.x holds lent_1 loan_1, whose type cannot be read…"), a regression of D112. **Rule:** a mark is its content for typing, as for paths: `valType?` reads through it (as it follows a loan to its borrow's content), `packedErr` checks the content it marks, and `depFieldType` types the field by the content under its marks (`Value.unmark`). The plain borrow and the call's returned borrow then take one path: `A2` and `Direct` are rejected as ordinary type mismatches ("the ascribed term has type One, expected Void"), the three reads are accepted with their values (5, 6, `O`, by `refl`), and an honest index write (`TreeHonest`) is accepted as it is with `&x`.
