  - **The census** of the content a typed run leaves in a dependent field, and whether its type is read: a constructor value (its recorded parameters; every constructor value of a typed run records them, the sweep's (WF10)); a number, the unit value, a closure (`Nat`, `Unit`, its Π; leaf fields only since D112); an unknown (its stored type); a stuck program (`sealedType?`, from its text where it holds a loan, D97); a loan (its live borrow's content; every loan outside a sealed program is live, (WF2)); a mark, a mark over a fill, a mark over a mark, a loan under a mark (through, now); `⋆` (a proof or leaf field only: its declared type; in a tree field the internal error D110 left, unreachable since no strong update stores it); `⊥` (its declared type, any read of it an error). So no form a user-writable program can put in a tree field lacks a readable type, and the remaining "[Open] … whose type cannot be read" is an assertion with this census behind it.
  - **Evidence.** New asserted block `DependentFields.MarkedFields` (60 rows; count 2,752 → 2,812): the red team's nine; each pair of a plain borrow and a call's returned borrow for a read, a write through it, a re-borrow and a move of the field, each with and without an index write, in a tree field and in a leaf field, the two columns identical in verdict and message; census rows for a stuck program, a fill of a stuck call's returned borrow, an unknown and a mark over a mark, each read after an index write at the type its content determines. A dump of every row (verdict, message, location, note count, both located modes) against main 6ec72c40: every earlier row identical but for locations later in `DependentFields.lean`. `lake exe sweep` over main's 125 blocks: identical output; `--pushes` clean.
  - **The whole table** (tests only, landed after D114; lane checker-marks). The differential `MarkedFields` was checked against, every row asserted: `DependentFields.MarkedTableTree1`, `…Tree2`, `…Leaf1`, `…Leaf2` (a tree and a leaf field, nine operations, four index writes, five ways of lending the field: a plain borrow, a call's returned borrow, the first and the second argument of a two-borrow call that returns it, and a call that lends it but returns another borrow), each rejection by its message and each acceptance with its value by `refl`; and `MarkedNested`, a census row `MarkedFields` lacked: a dependent constructor value under a mark (a field of a field), read whole, read after the outer index changes, and opened through the borrow, which its end repacks. 618 rows; count 2,908 → 3,526. On main before typing read through a mark, 35 of the 72 rows of the table's first four columns differed between the plain and a returned borrow, every one a wrong rejection; on main now none differs, in verdict, message or value. `lake exe sweep` over the five blocks: (WF1) to (WF12) hold on all 14,368 states; the only clauses that fire are those that fire on `MarkedFields` too ((WF8)'s borrow type in a value in flight at a call's exit, and the planted live-fill clause). Cost: about 12 seconds of checking.

## D108. A generalisation record is a key: it is looked up exactly (2026-11-21) [lane: exact-lookup]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the proof lane's reading of the checker). Where the checker looks a sealed program up among its generalisation records (`recordedNeutral`, `canonNeutral`), and where `substV` replaces a sealed program by its record's value, it compared with `==` (`Value.beq`). That equality ignores the parameters a constructor value records and binder names (and a field's printed name). This is right inside conversion, where both sides have one type and binder names are α-equivalence. It is wrong for a key: `Nil` recorded at `List(Nat)` and at `List(Unit)` are `==`, and mean different things. The proof's lookup lemmas are about exact keys.
- **Rule.** `Value.exact` (with `Term.exact`, `Place.exact`, `Hint.exact`, `FieldRef.exact`; `Syntax.lean`, beside `Value.beq`, structural and total) compares everything, recorded parameters, binder names and field names included. The three sites use it: `neutralLookup` (read by `recordedNeutral` and `canonNeutral`) finds a record whose program is exactly the one in hand, and `substV`/`substT` find the target they replace exactly (for an abstract value or a loan as the target, `exact` and `==` coincide). Every other use of `==` is unchanged. `StabilitySig` gains `sigPres_neutralLookup`; `StabilityDecide.substV_tPi` reads `exact`.
- **Measured: no lookup changes its answer.** An instrumented checker computed both answers at all three sites, at every lookup and every substitution step, and rejected with "D108-PROBE" wherever they differed. Three kinds of difference were checked: a record found under `==` and missed exactly, a different σ found, and a target occurrence or equality seen by one comparison and not the other. Over the suite (2,574 rows on e5cd2f80), `lake exe sweep --probes` and the 24 probe files under `probes/`, no instance fired: the dump was identical to the uninstrumented one. So no program in the suite generalises a sealed program twice where `==` merged it into one record. The proof lane's instrumented count (19,937 + 4 + 16,578 matches, every one already exact) agrees.
- **The other uses of `==` on values or programs outside conversion that decide something** (left unchanged, as briefed; whether equality up to recorded parameters could matter):
  - [Rec]'s candidates (`recCheck`: an argument must be in `strictSubterms` of the entry value, `List.contains`). Two values equal up to recorded parameters are the same data with the same size, so termination is unaffected.
  - The comparison stack (`convStack.contains`, in `convPi` and `convFn`, D103). It is part of conversion, and a pair found there yields `false`, so a match up to parameters can only reject.
  - η-refinements (`etaRefs.lookup σ`), the records' σ side (`neutrals.any`/`find?` by σ), `refs.lookup σ`, loans (`Value.loans`, `liveLoansIn`): keyed by numbers, exact already.
  - Footprints and observation (`Obs`: `insertPos`, `out.contains q`, `fieldOf`), the join's capture and grain lists (`blockCaptures`' `acc.contains q'`, `stepEq`): places, whose `==` ignores only a field's printed name (the constructor and index are compared), so up to recorded parameters cannot arise.
  - Comparisons with constants (`== .proof`, `== .bot`, `== .unit`, `== .ref .unit`) and on declared readings (`DeclInfo`, which has no names or recorded parameters): exact already.
- **Evidence.** A dump of every row's verdict and message, main against this change: identical on all 2,610 rows at 14aeb9cd and again on all 2,744 rows at 59e9bee7, after rebasing over D112. `lake exe sweep` and `--probes`: identical outputs from the first `states checked` line on, at both (77,517 and 538 states at 59e9bee7). The 24 probe files under `probes/`: identical output (at 14aeb9cd). No new rows, since no program of the suite or the probes distinguishes the two equalities at these sites. RULES [Split] and the history line, and the appendix's refinement-and-generalisation paragraph, say so; the Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D109. A loop in the checker is bounded by the fuel; nothing the checker runs is opaque to the kernel but `termAddr` (2026-11-21) [lane: fuel-loop]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the proof lane). `blockCaptures`' granularity loop (K1(g): a capture of which some arm moves out a part is split into its fields, place by place) was a `while`. Lean builds `while` on `Loop.forIn`, whose `repeatM.impl` is an opaque constant: the kernel cannot see into it, so nothing about a stuck match could be proved while it stood. It broke the checker's own standard (all checker code total, `ochr/core/CLAUDE.md`). The 2026-10-28 totality census looked for `partial def`, `unsafe`, `implemented_by` and `opaque` written in the checker, and missed it: the loop had been there since 0b8a68f0 (2026-09-30), and a `while` is none of those words.
- **Rule.** The loop is `for _ in [0:fuel'] do if todo.isEmpty then break; …`, followed by `unless todo.isEmpty do exhausted "out of fuel"`. It is structural in the fuel. Running out is the usual "I don't know", never caught and never deciding anything. Each round takes one place off the list, and a place adds only its fields, so the list empties after as many rounds as there are sub-places of the captures. The bound is the depth fuel left, about two million at the default.
- **Never hit.** Not in the suite or the case studies: `lake build` asserts every verdict, and an undecided one fails it. The dump has no undecided row. Not in `lake exe sweep --probes` or the 24 probe files under `probes/` either: their outputs are identical to main's. If it is hit, the declaration is undecided, neither accepted nor rejected. The editor reports "X: undecided (out of fuel): the checker ran out of fuel or past a depth limit, so it neither accepts nor rejects" at the declaration, and every later declaration that mentions it is undecided too (`checkDefs`).
- **The search, everything reachable from `checkProgram`, the parser included.** `Scratch/Reach.lean` walks every constant reachable from `Ochr.Test.checkProgram` through types and kernel-visible values (7,527 constants on this change), and lists the opaque, partial and unsafe ones, and checker constants with an `implemented_by`, each with the chain that reaches it. It is dev tooling, kept so that the next change can rerun it.
  - On main it lists `repeatM.impl`, reached from `blockCaptures` through `Lean.Loop.forIn`. On this change the one checker constant it lists is `Ochr.termAddr` (reached through `Locs.relocate`), the standard's sanctioned exception.
  - The rest are Lean's own library: the string primitives `String.Internal.append`, `length` and `pushn` (messages, panic messages, `Repr`), `Std.Format.be` (pretty-printing for messages), `System.Platform.getNumBits` (hashing a `USize`: the editor's location table in `checkProgram`), and `Std.Internal.idOpaque` with `WellFounded.opaqueFix` (an iterator loop inside `String.replace`, which `closeBlock` uses to print a block's parameter names). None decides a verdict. Strings are built with opaque primitives anywhere in the checker, so they are names and messages to the proof, never something it computes through.
  - The parser: `checkProgram` takes a `Program`, the surface syntax as a Lean value, and resolves it with `Surface.runResolve` (fuel-bounded since the census). There is no text parser on that path. The `ochr` notation's elaborators in `Notation.lean` (`partial def`s in `MacroM`, and the `implemented_by` `evalBlock`) build `Program` values when a file is elaborated; the theorem quantifies over the values, and `checkProgram` never calls them. `RuleGuard.lean` (three `partial def`s) checks the paper's rule names when the library is built; it is imported only by the library root `Ochr.lean`, not by `Run`, `Test` or `Consistency`, so it is not reachable. A grep of the modules `Run` imports (Basic, Catch, Check, Decide, Env, Loc, Located, Machine, Obs, Pretty, Rules, Run, Surface, Syntax) finds no other `while`, `repeat`, `partial`, `unsafe`, `opaque` or `implemented_by`, and every `for` ranges over a list, an array or a range.
- **The standard**, `ochr/core/CLAUDE.md`, now names `while` and `repeat` beside `partial def`, and points to `Scratch/Reach.lean`. `notes/totality-census.md` records the miss.
- **Evidence.** A dump of every row's verdict and message, main (86ca5f4b) against this change: identical on all 2,752 rows. `lake exe sweep` and `--probes`: identical from the first `states checked` line on (77,607 and 538 states). The 24 probe files: identical output. No new rows: a block with millions of sub-places to capture is not a test anyone can run.

## D111. A borrow is never left without its end: asserted where values are overwritten, dropped and read (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: checker-borrow-root]
Decision (the orchestrator, from D110's hunt, its `ViaLoan`). Overwriting a place whose old content held a borrow below its root ended nothing: `assignPlace` ends the old content's borrow only when the content IS a borrow, and `findBorrow`/`takeBorrow` look only at a whole position, so the borrow's lender kept its loan forever, inert and dangling, readable as data of any type. D110 removed the one known way to put a borrow there (a strong update of a borrow-typed value). That is not a reason to rest on it: the invariant it broke is the proof's.
- **The invariant asserted.** A borrow is a whole position's value, never below a position's root (D48), and every loan outside a sealed program is live (its borrow is in Ω) and occurs once: the meaning spine's (WF1) and (WF2).
- **The census** (every way a borrow could come to sit below a root in a typed run, one program each, all rejected on main at 9eea122f): a constructor with a borrow field (`MkB1(r : &Nat)`: "no borrows inside data"); a pair, a box or a pair of two borrows (`Mk(&w, Z)`, `MkBox(&w)`: "Pair at &Nat: no borrows inside data"); an annotated `Box(&Nat)`, a function returning or taking one, a hole of that type, `Eq(&Nat, …)` ([D48]); `S(&w)` (a type mismatch); a closure capturing a borrow ("closures capture no borrows") or returning one ([D44]). A call's result is a borrow at its temporary's root (D99), a match's too; [Close]'s cells and fills, η, generalisation and marks hold loans, marks or `⋆`, never a borrow. Untyped runs run accepted code only, whose type arguments are never borrow types (D48). So the strong update was the one route, and D110 closed it.
- **Rule: cannot occur, an internal error where it would.** The overwrite (`assignPlace`'s old content), the drops (`dropTopBind`, `dropValue`) and so the end of scope (`popFrame` drops through `dropTopBind`) reject, as an internal error, a value with a borrow below its root (`Value.borrowBelowRoot`); a read (`readPlace`) rejects, as an internal error, content that still holds a loan outside a sealed program whose borrow is not in Ω after the access ended the live ones (`Value.outerLoans`, the spine's). An internal error is a rejection: it must never fire on an accepted program. The build shows it does not on the suite, the case studies and the probes; the sweep at every call depth is what tests the invariant itself on every state, and is the next step.
- **The dependency.** The census rests on a borrow-typed right-hand side being an ordinary checked write in the dependent-field code (D110's `strongStorable`), which D112 keeps (a borrow, proof, type or function is a checked write in tree and leaf fields alike); a later change there must keep it too.
- **Rows.** `StrongUpdate.DanglingRead` (the dangling loan read as data of another type, `let v : One = w; match v { O => 0 }`; rejected by D110 at `x := &w`, and by this change at the read had it been reached); `Leak` and `ViaLoan` stay rejected rows of D110.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main (D112) against this change: identical on every earlier row (locations later in `DependentFields.lean` shift by one row). The new internal errors never fire.
  - `lake exe sweep`: every clause's violation counts as on main ((WF1) and (WF2) hold on all 77,618 recorded states; the 11 more are the new row's). `--probes` the same; `--pushes` clean.
  - Cost: instructions retired on `Quicksort` +0.3%, `HashMapTests` +0.2%.
  - RULES [Drop] and the history line, and the appendix's well-formedness condition on borrows, say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D113. `Id` observes what a re-pointed borrow points to, as it observes a borrow result (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: checker-obs-ref]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the red team's finding 1, `A4`/`A5` on main 9c4f47b1). A borrow binding that both sides of `Id` re-point ([Obs-ref], D84) was observed by writing one fresh value `σ_y` through it (`*y := σ_y`) after the side ran and before its borrows ended, so that the owners showed where it pointed. The write overwrote what the side had left in the new target, unobserved. `Hide : Unit := (let x = 0; let w = 0; let y = &w; let h : Id(Unit, y := &x, (y := &x; *y := 5)) = refl; ())` was accepted, although the same continuation, `y := &w; x`, gives `0` after the left side and `5` after the right (`ObsRef.HideL`, `HideR`). The sibling rule [Obs-borrow] (D38) observes a borrow result the other way round: its content first, as a component, then the fresh value through it. Not a route to `False`: an `Id` type is its computed conjunction from the moment it exists, and no rule replaces one side by the other; but `Id` then meant less than it says, and D84's resource lemma ("where each re-pointed binding points: the same") said nothing of what it points to.
- **Rule.** [Obs-ref] treats a re-pointed binding `y : &T`, holding `borrow_k c` after the side, as [Obs-borrow] treats a borrow result: `c`, what `y` points to as the side left it, is taken out of the borrow and held as a temporary, one more component of the observation; `σ_y` is put in its place (`borrow_k σ_y`); the observation's ends then carry `σ_y` to the owners of `loan_k` (where `y` points) and fill the holes of `c`. `Id` compares the result, the owners, and then each re-pointed binding's component at `T` (`Eq(T, c, c')`), each repacked as the others are. `Id(Unit, (y := &x; ()), (y := &z; ()))` now computes to `Eq(Nat, σ_y, x) ∧ (Eq(Nat, z, σ_y) ∧ Eq(Nat, x, z))` (`Moves84.RePoint`'s message gains the third equation).
- **No access.** The content is moved out of the borrow, not written through it. A write `*y := σ_y` accesses `*y`, which ended a second re-pointed binding's reborrow of `*y` before the observation reached it: `(y1 := &x; y2 := &*y1)` on both sides was rejected, "no such place *y2" (`ObsRef.TwoSame`, `TwoBoth`; a wrong rejection, in the red team's `A5.Two`). Now `y2`'s reborrow stays live until the ends, where it fills `y1`'s component with what `y2` leaves, so a side where `y2` points into `y1`'s target and one where it points elsewhere are still told apart (`TwoApart`).
- **The class, hunted before fixing.** The root: the observation trusted that the target of a re-pointed binding held nothing the continuation could see. Every way a side can leave something there, each beside its honest twin (the same sides without the hidden write, accepted on main and here) and the twin that writes on both sides (accepted): a write through the binding (`Hide`), through a reborrow `y := &*z` (`Re`), a detour that writes the binding's original target and re-points it away and back (`Detour`, with `DetourL`/`DetourR`: `0` and `5`), a stuck call's fill (`Fill`, generic and at `n = 1`; at `n = 0` the call writes nothing and the `Id` is `⊤`), a parameter (`Param`, with a closed call of the honest twin), a second binding's reborrow (`Two`), a field (`Fld`), different writes to one target (`Diff`; `DiffRoutes`, the same write before and after re-pointing, is `⊤`), a target `Pick` chooses (`Pick`, generic and at `n = 0`), and a refill with another value after a move through the binding (`RefillHide`; `RefillSame`, refilled with what was moved out, is `⊤`). On main eleven of these were accepted; every one is rejected now, with the equation it fails. Every other component an observation reads was checked for the same fault: the result ([Obs-borrow] observes first), the owners (read after the ends), [Conv-fun]'s generic calls (the lender cells of borrow parameters are read after the ends, and a borrow result goes through [Obs-borrow]): none writes before it reads. A target left moved through the binding on both sides (`OpenBoth`) is `⊥` in both components, equal, as before (the type-state compares it, `OpenOne`).
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main with the new block copied in, against this change, at 86ca5f4b and again after rebasing onto 6bdcecf0 (D111, D112 amended; 2,864 rows): identical on every row but `Moves84.RePoint` (its message gains the third equation) and the new block, where the eleven hiding rows accepted on main are rejected, `TwoSame` and `TwoBoth` are accepted, and `TwoHide`, `TwoApart`, `ApartTargets` change message.
  - New asserted block `Equality.ObsRef` (51 rows); count 2,752 → 2,803.
  - `lake exe sweep`, at both: every clause's and premise's count as on main once the new block is set aside (the totals, 80,305 states against 80,266 at 6bdcecf0, differ by exactly its counts swept alone on each side: 39 more states, all from the rows that now get past the observation). `--probes` identical; `--pushes` clean.
  - The probe files under `probes/`: identical output but `d84/A4.lean`'s `RN`, the same third equation. Its known gap (D84: [Obs-ref] cannot see where a borrow of `Unit` points, `RU`) is unchanged: what such a borrow points to is `()` after either side.
  - Cost: not measured; the new code runs only where the sides of an `Id` re-point a borrow binding, and it replaces a write (an access and a drop) by a swap.
  - RULES [Obs-ref], the observation, `Id` computes, the type-state's *Why* and the history line, and the paper's [Obs-ref] (body and appendix) and [Id] say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.
- **For the model.** D84's resource lemma said "where each re-pointed binding points: the same, because the footprint's owners, written through by [Obs-ref], are equal"; with D113 the conjunction also says what each points to is the same, which is what a continuation that re-points it away and reads the target needs (`HideL`, `HideR`).

## D114. A side of `Id` may end a borrow it consumes, on every path (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: checker-id-consumed]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the red team's finding 2, `I1`/`I2` on main 14aeb9cd). [Id-typestate]'s no-ending clause (D84) rejects a side that ends a borrow a binding or a temporary outside the `Id` holds. It asked who held the borrow as it ended (`endBorrow`, while `obsFrame` is set), and that depends on the path. `A1 (n : Nat) (x : &Nat) : Id(Unit, match n { Z => Eat(x), S _ => () }, …) := refl` with `Eat (r : &Nat) : Unit := ()` was accepted, and so was `A1(0, &c)`, but `A1(1, &c)` was rejected: "[Id-typestate] a side of Id ends the borrow x". The side consumes `x` on every path: at the generic call the stuck match's block takes it by move and the block's parameter ends it; at `n = 0` it is moved into `Eat`, whose parameter ends it; at `n = 1` the arm that does not use it leaves it whole and the join drops it in place, in the frame the check watches. An accepted theorem with a rejected closed instance (the suite's own `Equality.IdThroughRet` had one, at `C10`) is the disagreement between the checker's paths this project removes. Fail-safe (a rejection), never a wrong acceptance.
- **Rule.** The check is decided by the side's consumed set, which the type-state already computes from syntax and declared types (`Decide.typeState`): a borrow held by a binding the side consumes may be ended by the side, on any path and by any route (a drop, a move into a call or a block, an access of its lender); the clause flags only a borrow whose binding the side does not consume, and a borrow a temporary around the `Id` holds, as before. `observe` receives the side's consumed bindings and records their positions with the watched frame (`obsFrame`'s fourth component); `endBorrow` skips them. A consumed binding is moved on every path, so no continuation uses it after either side, and the type-state makes both sides consume it.
- **Why that is the whole class.** A binding the side does not consume is never dropped by it (the join drops only what an arm moves, and a move of a borrow binding is a consumption), so its borrow is ended inside the side only by an [Access] of what it borrows, and since D90 every run makes the same accesses at the same points. The other checks that ask who holds a borrow as it ends were read for the same fault: D106's (`endTopLoan`: a temporary's borrow ended by an access), D65's at a drop and at the join (`endLoansOfTopBind`, `endLoansOfTopTemp`, `dropAtJoin`, a stuck block's arm), and F4's internal assertion. Each asks whether the holder is a value in flight, which a borrow is between its evaluation as an argument and the call, on every path alike: a stuck block's arguments are taken after its entry has made every access (D91), and a direct run's arm reaches the same accesses after the same entry. Probed by path (generic, `n = 0`, `n = 1`), inside a side that consumes the borrow and outside `Id`: an argument in flight ended by a later argument's access, an arm's result borrowing a place another arm moves, a consumed borrow moved in one arm with the other arm's result a reborrow of it; inside and outside agree row for row, so none of them asks the question differently by path in a way `Id` adds.
- **The differential** (scratch, 1,180 definitions in 14 blocks): 119 theorems whose two sides consume a borrow parameter, or a local borrow, in some arms of a match on a `Nat` (nine kinds of arm: a move into a call that drops it, into one that returns a borrow, into a `let`, a write then a call, a reborrow, a returned borrow written through, a nested match, nothing) or on a three-constructor type, each checked generically, at each constructor of small arguments (`n`, `m` in 0..2), in another definition's check (generic and at `n = 1`), and over a local borrow (generic and at each value); and 35 where an arm reads or writes the lender instead. On main 53 theorems had an instance whose verdict differed from the generic one, every difference the no-ending message on an instance of an accepted theorem. Here, nothing accepted on main is rejected, 183 rejected definitions are accepted, 20 change message, and the 8 theorems that still differ are the shape `match n { Z => Eat(x), S _ => (let a = clone(c); ()) }` over `x = &c`: the match's entry reads `c`, ending `x`, before the arm that moves `x` runs, so the generic call and `n = 0` are rejected ("[Read] x was moved out …") and `n = 1` accepted, exactly as the same match outside `Id` (`IdConsumed.Outside`, `Outside0`, `Outside1`); on main they were rejected on every path by the no-ending clause. All twelve of their differing instance verdicts are in that direction (a rejected theorem with an accepted instance, never the reverse): fail-safe, and asserted beside the same match outside `Id` (`LenderRead`, `LenderRead0`, `LenderRead1`).
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main (75e79291, with D113) with the new block copied in, against this change (2,908 rows): identical on every row but the new block's, where the twenty instances rejected on main by the no-ending message are accepted (`A1s`, `A1ss`, `A1in`, `A2s`, `ByLet1`, `WriteEat1`, `Second0`, `Nested01`, `Nested10`, `Three1`, `Three2`, `Local1`, `ThroughRet10`, `LenderRead1`) or rejected for the ordinary reason (`LenderRead`, `LenderRead0`: "[Read] x was moved out …"). No suite or case-study row changes.
  - New asserted block `Equality.IdConsumed` (44 rows); count 2,864 → 2,908.
  - `lake exe sweep`: every clause's and premise's count as on main once the new block is set aside (the totals, 80,942 states against 80,869, differ by exactly its counts swept alone on each side, 637 against 564). `--probes` identical; `--pushes` clean.
  - The probe files under `probes/`: identical output.
  - Cost: not measured; the change adds a list lookup where a side ends a borrow its frame holds.
  - RULES (*No ending* and the history line), the paper (the body's type-state sentence, the appendix's type-state paragraph) and `notes/two-paths-census.md` (site 28) say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D115. No value the machine holds lives only in the checker's variables: a call's arguments stay in Ω until its frame binds them (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: checker-call-args]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the sweep at every call depth, `lake exe sweep --deep-private`). `evalCall` pushed a call's head and arguments as temporaries (D104 typed them), then took them off (`popTemps`) and handed them to `callFn` as Lean values, so from there until `runBodyCore` bound the parameters they were in no position of Ω. In that window a borrow argument's label had no holder, and its lender's loan read as a loan of an ended borrow: the meaning spine's (WF2), "every loan outside a sealed program is live and occurs once", was false on 662,366 recorded states of 770 declarations, all inside private copies at call depth above 0 (smallest `Drops.Bad5Run`: inside `runBodyCore`'s `paramTypes` copy for `TailM(&b)`, `b : Nat = lent_2 loan_1` with `borrow_1` held nowhere). RULES [Call] already said the arguments are temporaries whose contents the frame takes; the checker did not do it. D104's sentence, "every value the machine holds has a stored type", did not reach them either.
- **Rule.** No value the machine holds lives only in a variable of the checker's own code while anything runs or maps over Ω. A call's head and arguments stay the last temporaries of the caller's frame (`Env.callTemps`), at their types, through everything the call does before its frame binds them: its type ([Call-type], `declCallType`, whose private copies now take a borrow argument from its temporary, `takeArgBorrow`), [Rec]'s check, the [Lend] marks (after which they are read again), a proof call's ends (P5, `endBorrowArgs`, each where it is held) and [Close], which consumes each borrow argument (`⊥` in its temporary, as [End] takes a borrow before it fills its loan) as it fills its loan, and takes the rest into its programs. `runBodyCore` moves them off the caller's temporaries as the frame binds them, with nothing run in between (`takeCallTemps`). Every caller of `callFn` pushes them: `evalCall`, `cong`, `J`'s motive, [Conv-fun]'s generic calls (`convFnRun`). A call's result is held as a temporary while D99 renames its label (`lendEnv (.into k g)`, which now renames the held borrow itself), a stuck block's call's at the block's type. Two more windows of the same kind, found by the sweep after the first build: [Conv-fun]'s generic arguments while the result's type is computed (`convFnRun`), and [Conv-pi]'s while the second Π-type's parameter types are evaluated (`convPi`): both now hold them as temporaries of frame 0 until a frame binds them. The four other places that held a value off Ω while code ran (the class hunt below): a constructor's fields until its value is built (`evalCtor`, through `fieldTypesOf` and the field checks), an assignment's right-hand side from its evaluation (`assignPlace` now takes it from the top temporary), a borrow's value while [Open] invalidates (`openWrite`), and a borrow-typed match's result while the join lends (`joinEnds`).
- **The class, hunted before fixing** (each a row of `Functions.CallArgs`; none an accepted wrong program). What runs in the window, and whether the arguments should have seen it:
  - [Call-type], `declCallType`, `paramTypes`, `argHint`: private copies; what survives them (records, names) leaves Ω and the arguments alike. Inside, the later arguments were unbound and lenders' loans read dead: the 662,366 states. No parameter type can reach the caller's frames (F4).
  - P5's ends: a sibling could miss a fill only by holding the ended borrow's loan, which is never formed: outside copies D106 rejects (`S1`, `S2`), in a statement's copy the ⊥ check does (`S3`). Its `.ended` list was computed with the siblings out of Ω (moot for the same reason). `UseP2`, `UseP2R`, `UseP3`.
  - [Lend]'s marks: an argument is never a lender (`M1` to `M3`, with runs).
  - [Close]'s fills: every lender is in Ω; but its precondition assertion ("every argument's content is loan-free") counted a sibling's borrow as dead, so it was weaker than stated. Now complete. `C1` to `C3`.
  - [Rec]'s check reads the arguments and the refinements only (`R1` to `R3`); a call as another call's argument runs inside the outer window (`N1`); no refinement of the real Ω happens in the window (the body runs untyped); a copy's discard and `restoreNames` restore only the copy.
  - The same pattern elsewhere: a constructor's fields (copies only), [Assign]'s right-hand side (copies), [Borrow]'s value (`openWrite` accesses leaf fields on the real Ω; the borrowed index was lent to a borrow in no position), `joinEnds`' result (a stale mark inside it was possible only if a borrow the block takes by move lent a place inside the result's content; the entry's access of every capture ends such a borrow first: `JE`, `JEU`, `JER`, rejected there), `observe`'s and `Eq`'s sides (each its own copy), `cong`'s and `J`'s values (an erased evaluation restores the environment), the closure during `checkFix` (a fresh environment). All invariant-only.
- **D106.** With the arguments in Ω, an access in the window that ended an argument's borrow would now be rejected. None does: the dump is identical row for row.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main (6b2ed5d1) with the new block copied in, against this change (3,564 rows): identical on every row.
  - New asserted block `Functions.CallArgs` (38 rows: the hunt's programs and their runs, and `Pass`/`PassRun`, the window's shape) with `callArgsLive`: at every state at every call depth, private copies included, every loan outside a sealed program is live. On main it is false. Count 3,526 → 3,564.
  - `lake exe sweep --probes` and `--deep`: byte-identical outputs from the first `states checked` line on; `lake exe sweep`: every clause's count identical, one premise up by 7 states ("a typed run holds a temporary": the held generic arguments). `--deep-private` (2,152,765 states, the same on both sides): (WF2)'s "a loan of an ended borrow" from 665,874 states in 1,119 declarations (main at 6b2ed5d1; 662,366 in 770 when the sweep first ran) to none: 0 states (the first build left 559 states in 18 declarations, all [Conv-fun]'s and [Conv-pi]'s generic arguments, which led to the last two windows above). (WF2) now fails only by its other half, "a loan occurring twice" (22 states, the field-type scaffolding, to be D117). What else moves, all premises or clauses already violated on main: (WF8) "a borrow type inside a value, at a temporary" 580 → 1,326 states (a call's head whose Π-type has a borrow parameter is now a temporary while the call runs its scaffolding); the planted "a live fill" 238,291 → 282,093; the premise "inside a private copy, a temporary at a borrow type holds ⊥" 38,535 → 245,079 (a copy that binds the arguments now takes each borrow from its temporary, `takeArgBorrow`, as its comment said it should).
  - `--pushes`: no temporary but `⋆` pushed with no stored type in a typed run. Reported, not violations (D82: an untyped run computes no type it does not read): 1,320 borrows pushed by [Borrow] across `openWrite`, 1,802 call results across [Lend]'s renaming and 1 join result, in untyped runs at call depth above 0.
  - The 24 probe files under `probes/`: identical output.
  - Job A's fact, re-run on this change (scratch): no data write at a place with a live loan or mark on its path, 0 of 48,804 `setPlace` writes per dump mode and none in the probe files. The join's mark write over the loan of the result it is lending (`joinEnds`, now that the result is in Ω) happens 6 times; by the orchestrator's ruling the fact is about data writes (a mark records lending and changes no content), and that one site is excluded by name.
  - Cost: instructions retired on the compiled checker, pinned to one core, main against this change: `HashMapTests` 27.05G → 27.36G (+1.2%), `Quicksort` 74.06G → 74.61G (+0.7%), at the landed commit. The constructor's fields alone (the change without them: 27.355G and 74.595G) cost +0.03% and +0.02%; the rest is the call path (the arguments stay in Ω, so every copy that binds them finds and takes a borrow, and every map over Ω visits them).
  - RULES ([Call], the stored-types bullet, the history line) and the appendix's call point and [Close] figure say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D116. `clone` of a proof is that proof (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: checker-clone-proof]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the red team's mutation test, finding 5, on main defe1cea). The erasure pre-pass decides whether a term is erased and a proof from its declared reading (`Decide.declOf`), which reads `clone(p)` as `p`; after the run, `evalAt` classifies the term again by its form and asserts the two agree. Its table had no case for `clone`, so a `clone` term fell to the default, data. So `clone` of any place holding a proof was an internal error, in every position: `def K7 (h : ⊤) : ⊤ := clone(h)` gave "INTERNAL [pre-pass] clone(#0): erased/proof = (true, true) by its declared type, (false, false) after running (please report)", and so did a `let` of it, an unused one, one under a data call, a constructor, `S`, a pair or an assignment, in a closure's body (where the captured proof is inlined, `clone((⋆ : ⊤))`), in a side of `Eq` or `Id`, as a match's scrutinee, as an argument of `symm`, `trans` or `rewrite`, a proof field (`clone(h.0)` of `h : ⊤ ∧ ⊤`) and an arm of a non-tail match. No wrong program was accepted: an internal error is a rejection, of the legitimate uses as of the ill-typed ones.
- **Rule.** A proposition is a copy (D100), so `clone(p)` of a place holding a proof is that proof: its value is `⋆` (as reading the place gives it), and the run classifies the `clone` term by its operand, whose evaluation sets the flags (`.prim "clone" _ => getFlags` in `evalAt`'s table). The run then agrees with the declared reading for a proof and for data alike. The legitimate uses are accepted (`K7`, `K10`, a `let` of it, a field, a closure's body, an arm, both sides of `Id`, `symm(clone(h))`, `rewrite clone(h) in refl`), and an ill-typed one is rejected by the ordinary type rule, as the same program without `clone` is (`K3 (h : ⊤) : Nat := clone(h)`: "the body of K3 has type ⊤, but the goal is Nat", as `K9 (h : ⊤) : Nat := h`).
- **The opposite rule** (reject `clone` of a proof outright) was not taken: `clone` is the explicit copy of any place (P3), every proposition is a copy, and the declared reading already reads `clone(p)` as `p`; a separate rejection would be a rule for one former, where this is the table's missing row.
- **The class, hunted before fixing.** Every term former in `evalAt`'s after-run table against `declOf`'s reading of it: the forms the table sends to the default (data) are those `declOf` reads as data, a sort or `other` (`&p`, `p := t`, `S`, `Z`, `()`, the type formers, `peek` and `inplace`, both read `other` on both sides), except `clone`, which `declOf` reads as its operand; `split` and `splitArms` are read as their body by `declOf` but never run through the table (they are rejected outside tail position before it). So `clone` is the only former whose run could flag its result differently from its declared reading. The red team's two batches agree: 33 and 24 programs, only `clone` of a proof place reaches the error; every other former gets a rule's error (a proof, a proposition or a type where data is expected, and the reverse; `clone(⊤)`, "clone takes a place").
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main (226dcdd6, D115) with the new blocks copied in, against this change (3,643 rows): identical on every row outside the new blocks. In them 34 rows change, every one from the internal error (or from "unknown constant", a row whose callee hit it): 24 to accepted, 10 to the ordinary type error their twin without `clone` gets; no row is accepted on main and rejected here.
  - New asserted blocks `Recursion.CloneProof` (20 rows: one per position, with closed runs), `CloneK2` (31) and `CloneK3` (28), the red team's two batches verbatim, every rejection with its message; count 3,564 → 3,643.
  - `lake exe sweep`: every clause's count identical (96,693 states against 96,566: the 127 more are the new rows that now get past the error). `--probes` identical; `--pushes` clean. The probe files under `probes/`: identical output.
  - RULES P3 and the history line, and the appendix's declared-type paragraph (`clone(p)`'s is `p`'s), say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D119. A split in tail position ends with the join, as a known constructor's arm does (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: checker5]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the consistency proof's simulation, whose reference run could not relate the generic call's end states to an instance's; the three behaviours are pinned on the proof line by `Meaning/Regress/TailJoin.lean`, spine-lead14 b4d63034). A match that is the last thing a body does meets the checker on three paths: on a known constructor (`checkTailAt`'s `.zero`/`.succ`/constructor cases: the arm runs with `joinCont`, which drops what the join says before the continuation), in a call's run of the body (`runBodyCore` sets `tailNext`, and the match goes through `joinEnds … tail`, which skips only [Entry] and still calls `joinDrop`), and on an unknown at the generic call (`checkTailAt`'s split: each world's arm ran with the bare continuation, no drop). With `def Ag (n : Nat) : Nat := match n { Z => Z, S m => m }` the generic call ended its worlds with `n ↦ Z` and `n ↦ S(⊥)`; the other two paths, and the proof's reference run, end with `n` moved. RULES [Join] already said "matches in tail position … join the same way"; the split did not.
- **Rule.** In tail position each world's arm of a split runs with `← joinCont fuel' t k` in place of `k`, for `Z`/`S` and for a declared inductive, computed in the world after the refinement, so all three paths drop the same places at the end of every arm. A match on a proof (by type) is unchanged: it has no join on any path (`evalMatchByType` has none, nor has the known case in `checkTailAt`): with one arm there is no other path to agree with, and with several its arms are proofs, whose statement moves nothing outside its copy (D68). A match on a value of a type with one constructor still has no join (D93). The other uniform choice, no drop at the end of a body on any path, was the fallback had this one rejected an accepted program that is not at fault; it rejected none.
- **The class, hunted before fixing** (`probes/split-join/Hunt.lean`, four blocks, about 250 rows of differential twins, one row per body shape and per path: the generic definition, the same body on a `let`-bound known constructor, a call with known arguments, and values by `refl`). Shapes: arms that move different parts; a dependent return type that mentions the matched parameter; a result that is a borrow passed in by a parameter (whole, reborrowed, through a `let`, through a field, with a local reborrow alive); an `Id` result; nested tail matches; tail matches under `let` and `;` with local borrows and reborrows; `matchInd` with fields, through a borrow and with a dependent field; a match on a proof; closures in arms; a stuck scrutinee, generalised and split; then, at the orchestrator's pointer, an arm whose result borrows a place another arm moves, and a lambda's body. On main no accepted program was wrong, every value held, and each definition's paths had the same verdict (except where a known constructor's other arm is never checked, as at any instance). What differed was the end states and, on rejected programs, the reason: a result borrowing a local another arm moves (`SplitJoinLent.L1g`) was rejected by the frame's pop ([Drop]) at the generic call and by the join ([Join]) on its known twin; now both by [Join].
- **Messages that change** (no verdict changes; on each row the generic call's rejection reason is now its known twin's, so the two paths' reasons agree):
  - `ReturnedBorrows.JoinPrefix.TU` (`let r0 = &*x; match n { Z => r0, S m => (let a = (*x).1; y) }` over borrow parameters, the unknown twin of `TK`): before, [D53] in world `S` ("a borrow ends while its content is partly moved out"); now `TK`'s [Access] (D106) in world `Z`, where the join's drop of `(*x).1` would end the result's borrow. Its asserted message and the block's comment are updated.
  - `Borrows.PartMove` and `PartMoveOpaque` (`match *x { Z => (), S p => (let v = p; H(x, n)) }`): before, [D53] in world `S`, "argument 1 of a call that closes off is a borrow whose content is partly moved out (S(⊥))"; now world `Z`'s join drops `*x` (arm `S` moves its predecessor, so `*x` whole by the grain) and `x`, and `x`'s end gives [D53] "a borrow ends while its content is partly moved out (⊥)", the message of the same body after `*x := Z`, on main and here alike.
  - `HashMapTests.BLookupMoves` (a lookup that moves the value out of a bucket it only borrows): [D53] "a borrow ends while its content is partly moved out" in both, now with `(⊥)` from world `Nil`'s join, which drops `*b` (arm `Cons` moves out of it), where before world `Cons` showed `Cons((σ5, ⊥), σ4)`.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main (255b8ec0) with the new blocks copied in and softened, against this change (3,873 rows, before `SplitJoinLent` and the lambda shape were added): identical but for the four rows above (their messages and locations) and, in located mode, editor-note counts: 123 rows gain notes (the [Join] notes of the generic call's splits), and the four rows above lose some (they now stop in their first world). The rows added after it (`SplitJoinLent`, the lambda shape `M`) were dumped alone on both sides with `JoinPrefix`, `SplitJoin` and `SplitJoinInd`: no verdict differs; `L1g`, `L2g`, `L4g` change from [Drop] to [Join], as above.

On main 30 of the 33 shapes' generic definitions drop nothing and the guard is false. `SplitJoinLent` (23): the borrowed-result shapes, every rejection's message asserted (false on main for `L1g`, `L2g`, `L4g`). Mutation-tested: a wrong value row and a wrong guard each fail the build. Count 3,643 → 3,903.

## D118. A declaration names only declared constants and inductive types, checked before anything runs (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: checker5]
Decision (the orchestrator, ruled 2026-11-21: a dead arm of a match on a known constructor is never looked at). A name was looked up only when a run reached it (`lookupGlobal`, `lookupInd`), so a constant that is not declared, or declared later, or whose declaration was rejected, was accepted in code that no run reaches, while the same name in a live position was rejected. `def H4 : Nat := (let z = 0; match z { Z => 0, S n => NoSuchNameD })` was accepted and `H4L`, the same with `let z = 1`, rejected; so was `inductive Q4 := MkQ4(p : P(λ(y : Nat) : Nat => (let z = 0; match z { Z => 0, S n => NoSuchNameE })))`. No proof of `False` came of it (the unreachable code never runs), but which programs are accepted depended on which arms a run takes, and a name's meaning on whether it is reached.
- **Rule.** A declaration ([Def], [Const], an inductive declaration) is rejected before anything runs if any of its terms (parameter, field and result types, body) names a constant or an inductive type not in Σ at that point, read from syntax and Σ alone (`Check.undeclaredCheck`, `Term.undeclared`): `unknown constant c` or `unknown inductive type D`, the messages a run gives, located at the innermost written term around the name (a call's head by its call). An inductive type's own name in its fields is allowed; a function's own name in its body is its bound `self`, not a constant, and a constant's own name is not declared (`SelfC`, as a live `def X : Nat := X` already was). A match with no arms names no type. The check comes after the declared readings (D55: `declOf`, `typePos`), which run nothing either and keep their own messages.
- **The class, hunted before fixing** (one program each, `Functions.Undeclared`; every one accepted on main, rejected now): the other arm of a match on a known constructor (`H4`, `K1`, `K2`, a closure's body `Clo`), a match an outer split already decided (`RS`), a dead arm in a parameter type (`PT`), a result type (`RT`), an inductive's parameter type (`IP`) or a field type through a λ (`Q4`), a type function's arm under a Π (`TP`) or a value at a computed function type (`TLv`), a statement (`ES`) and an `Id` side (`IdS`), a `split` target (`SC`), a constant's own name (`SelfC`), a constant declared later (`Later1`), a rejected declaration (`UsesBad`), and an inductive type declared later, through a match on its constructors (`LT3`; a constructor value, `LT2`, was already rejected by the declared readings). Each has an accepted twin with a declared name (`H4K`, `Q4K`, `RSK`, `IdSK`, `Later1K`, `LT2K`; and `SelfRec`, `OwnL`, `NoArms` for recursion, an inductive's own name and a match with no arms). The live twins (`H4L`, `H4U`, `H4Lam`) were rejected before and are rejected now with the same message.
- **Messages that change** (no verdict changes outside the new block): `ReturnedBorrows.Boom` (`Q(leak)` with `Q` rejected), from its parameter's [D44] to `unknown constant Q`; `FrozenReadings.KeepTFSplit` (it calls the rejected `KeepTF`), from the [D77] its run reached to `unknown constant KeepTF` (its guard is updated). Both stay rejected: each names a rejected declaration, which is now caught before the declaration's own checks.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, in both located modes, main (c77b5d1a) with the new block copied in and softened, against this change (3,940 rows, before `LT3` was added; `LT3` and the touched blocks were then dumped alone on both sides): outside the new block, identical but the two messages above; in located mode four rejected rows that were unlocated ("unknown constant" in a type) are now located at the name (`ClosingOff.MoveBorrowSplit`, `ArmLocalBoom.T7`, `LentIndex.Boom2Pf`, `JBlocks.LenPf`), and 76 rows rejected for an unknown constant have fewer editor notes (they are rejected before anything runs). In the new block, the 18 rows above go from accepted to rejected.
  - New asserted block `Functions.Undeclared` (38 rows), every rejection's message asserted; mutation-tested (a changed message and an undeclared name in a control each fail the build). Count 3,903 → 3,941.
  - `lake exe sweep`: no clause gains a violation; 100,075 → 99,515 states, 3,795 → 3,682 declarations with a recorded state (the rows now rejected before they run record none), and three clauses lose the few violations those rows had (1,462 → 1,459, 5,160 → 5,157, 44,121 → 44,110). `--probes` identical, `--pushes` clean (no push with no stored type).
  - The probe files under `probes/`: identical messages; in three files (`d84/A1`, `A3`, `A5`) ten "unknown constant" errors are located at the name instead of the declaration.
  - The build is green with no new `sorry`: no stability proof breaks.
  - RULES ([Def]'s declared names, the history line) and the appendix's [Def] paragraph say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D120. A variable mentions what its declared type mentions: no term off a field type's spine reads a value of the type being declared (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-22) [lane: checker6]
Decision (the orchestrator, on the data model lane's request, docs/34 §18.6, exists5; Lean's kernel rule "nested inductive datatypes parameters cannot contain local variables"). Two shapes of declaration were accepted that Lean's kernel rejects and that the data model's construction cannot build. A nested occurrence whose value argument is an earlier field of the type itself: `Pt (A : Type) (a : A) := MkPt` with `D2 := D2Leaf | D2Node(x : D2, p : Pt(D2, x))`, where a value argument carries the model's record of `D2` into `Pt`'s (`Exists/KnotCE.knotOld_false`: the knot as first stated is false at it). An equation, or any term off the field type's spine, reading an earlier field whose type mentions the type, directly or through a parameter nested at later: `LD := LDLeaf | LDNode(xs : List(LD), e : Eq(Nat, match xs { Nil => 0, Cons(h, t) => 1 }, 1))`, and `W (A : Type) := WL | WN(ws : List(A), e : Eq(Nat, match ws { … }, 1))` with `UW := MkUW(w : W(UW))`, where whether the field's proposition is defined depends on the record. The checker's test was syntactic (`Term.mentionsType`): `x` and `xs` do not name `D2` or `LD`, though their values are members of it.
- **Rule.** In a field type a variable counts as mentioning everything its declared type mentions, transitively (`IndDecl.deepVars`: a field's type in the constructor's scope, a parameter's type in the scope of the parameters before it). A field type's spine is the type itself and, inside an inductive `m(…)`, its arguments at type parameters. Off the spine (an equation, a call of a type function, an argument at a value parameter of any inductive, the type's own occurrences included) no term reads a variable whose declared type mentions the type being declared (`Check.readViolation`, after the existing field checks, so no earlier rejection changes its message). In the nestable set (D107) the non-nestable positions are read with the same closure (`Term.nestOk`'s `vars`), so a parameter read off the spine through a variable (`ws : List(A)`, a field `y : A`, a value parameter `a : A` or `l : List(A)`) is not nestable. K4's message names the reason: the old text when the parameter is not nestable in the set computed as before D120 and D121, the D120 text otherwise (`nestReason`), so no existing message changes. Decided from syntax and declarations only.
- **Hunted before the change, for a closed proof of `False`; none found** (each program written and run on main e81757a3, in the lane's report): large elimination of `PP(D)` into data (rejected by D45); recursion through the proof or through the nested occurrence's index (rejected by [Rec] or by [Read]); recursion through the nested occurrence's own field (accepted, a real subterm); a lambda over the type as a value argument (rejected by D107); a value parameter typed by a type function of the nested parameter (`NF (A) (n : Nat) (f : NegF(A, n))`, accepted, but only a term written in the declaration, which cannot name the type, can stand there); an equation arm reaching an element (`match h`, rejected by D107) or a call naming the type (rejected by D36); `LZ := LZNode(xs : List(LZ), e : Eq(Nat, match xs { … }, 1))` with `LZ → False` (accepted, and true). A field type in these shapes sees only a container's shape, or carries the type's values through as positive data: the shapes obstruct the model's construction, they are not holes.
- **The class, hunted on the rule** (asserted, `Inductives.NestReads`, 40 rows, each rejection's message asserted, mutation-tested). Positions: a nested occurrence's value argument (`R1`), a value argument of a declaration the type is not nested in (`R2`; `R3` by a match), an equation (`R4`, `R4b`; `R7` beside an unrelated field), a type function's argument (`R5`, closed already by D36: an argument is a variable, a numeral or a first-order type, and no earlier type function takes the type's values without naming it), the type's own value parameter at its recursive occurrence (`R6`), the type nested at its own parameter read through a variable (`R8`). Ways: directly, under a container, through a parameter nested later (`W1`/`U1` an equation on `List(A)`, `W2`/`U2` a field `y : A`, `W3`/`U3` a value parameter `a : A`, `W4`/`U4` a value parameter `l : List(A)`), through two declarations (`W5`/`U5`). Accepted neighbours: `MkK3(n : Nat, x : Fin1(n))` (a dependent field indexed by an unrelated field), a container of the type nothing reads (`K3b`), an invariant on an unrelated field (`K4`), a type function of an unrelated field (`K5`), an unrelated value at the own occurrence (`K6`), the parameter not read (`K8`), a container of the parameter not read (`WK`/`UK`), a nested occurrence with an unrelated or closed value argument (`K1`, `K1c`; Lean accepts `Pn(E, 3)`), and `DF` below.
- **Where it differs from Lean.** Lean's kernel rejects every row D120 rejects (checked with the local toolchain, each row written as a Lean declaration: `R1`–`R8`, `U1`–`U5`, `EOnce`, `T1`; Lean's elaborated terms name the type where Ochr's `let`, `clone` and inferred parameters do not, and its indices may not mention the type being declared, which covers `R6`). D120 is weaker than Lean's rule: Lean rejects any local variable in a nested occurrence's parameters, so it rejects `K1 := KL1 | KN1(n : Nat, p : Pn(K1, n))`, which D120 accepts (the variable read has a type that does not mention `K1`); the model's grammar admits it.
- **Open, not ruled.** The model's grammar has a third condition, (B2): a value argument does not stand at a parameter whose declared type mentions an earlier parameter whose argument varies. It is not implied by D120 and D121: `NF (A : Type) (n : Nat) (f : NegF(A, n)) := MkNF(y : A)` with `DF := LeafF | NodeF(p : NF(DF, 0, ()))` is accepted, as by Lean (asserted in `NestReads`), and the hint of `()` is `NegF(DF, 0)`.
- **Rows that change**: three, from accepted to rejected, each a probe of this boundary, each rejected by Lean: `Inductives.PositivityRoutes.T1` (`G1 (A : Type) (a : A)`, `T1 := Leaf1 | Node1(t : T1, g : G1(T1, t))`, D107's hunt's "positive and accepted"; its new twin `T2` nests at a `Nat` value argument), and D107's twins `PositivityDeclaring.EOnce` (`Tag(let x = b; 0)` with `b : EOnce`) and `EClone` (`Tag(let x = clone(b); 0)`); D107's accepted twin `EOk` stays accepted.
- **Evidence** (with D121's; both on one branch).
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, main (9992ba73; same checker as e81757a3) with the new blocks copied in and softened, against this change: in unlocated mode all 143 blocks, in located mode the 130 blocks of the tour; identical but 27 rows, all accepted → rejected: the three above, and the 24 rows of `NestReads` and `NestProps` that are rejects (`R5` is rejected on both sides). No case study's row changes.
  - New asserted blocks `Inductives.NestReads` (40 rows) and `Inductives.NestProps` (28), every rejection's message asserted; mutation-tested (a twin flipped to `reject` in each block, and a changed message, each fail the build). With `T2`, count 3,941 → 4,011.
  - `lake exe sweep` over every block, and `--probes`: identical from `states checked` on; `--pushes`: no push with no stored type in a typed run, counts identical.
  - The probe files under `probes/`: identical output.
  - The build is green with no new `sorry`: no stability proof reads `checkInd`.
  - RULES (the declarations' paragraph, the nesting paragraph, the history line) and the appendix's [Ind-decl] and nesting paragraphs and @fig-why say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D121. A nested occurrence passes only through declarations of its own sort (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-22) [lane: checker6]
Decision (the orchestrator, on the same request, docs/34 §18.6, first as "no data-sorted declaration inside an argument at a `Prop` parameter", extended the same day after a further finding of the existence lane, exists6, `Exists/CrossSortCE.lean`; Lean's kernel rule "mutually inductive types must live in the same universe"; docs/32). `PP (A : Type) : Prop := MkPP(x : A)`, `Q (P : Prop) := MkQ(h : P)` and `D := MkD(q : Q(PP(D)))` were accepted: `Q`'s record at its parameter holds the proposition "`D` is inhabited", which no depth bound fixes. The boundary is crossed one declaration further in by `E (A : Type) := MkE(p : PP(A))` with `DE := MkDE(e : E(DE))`, and the other way by propositions: `Wrap (P : Prop) := MkWrap(h : P)` with `QW : Prop := MkQW(w : Wrap(QW))`, and `E2 (P : Prop) : Prop := MkE2(w : Wrap(P))` with `QE : Prop := MkQE(e : E2(QE))`, where the truth-first construction of a `Prop`-sorted declaration needs every shape a nestable `Prop` parameter reaches to be a proposition (`mono_prop`'s hypothesis). Lean rejects each. The model's knot for data-sorted declarations was found not to need the extension (it models `DE`); the construction for `Prop`-sorted ones does, and the rule is kept uniform because it is Lean's. No closed proof of `False` came of any: data cannot be eliminated out of `PP` (D45), recursion through a proof is not structural ([Rec]), and `D` is empty and provably so.
- **Rule** (the model's spec, `Knot/Grammar.lean`'s (A*), `occSortB` and `NestSortB`, on spine-exists6). (i) In a field type of `D`, an inductive `m(…)` one of whose arguments mentions `D`, at any depth, has `D`'s sort (`Check.sortNestViolation`, after D120's check). (ii) A parameter of `D` passed (as D120 reads, through variables' types) to a declaration of the other sort is not nestable (the first round of `nestableSet`, `Term.passOther`), so the type cannot reach that position through a later nesting. A proposition nested through propositions (`P : Prop := MkP(h : And(P, True))`) and a type of data through types of data stay accepted, as by Lean; so does a proposition about data, or data in a proposition, at no occurrence of the type (`MkWithPP(n : Nat, p : PP(Nat))`, `PWith (A : Type) : Prop := MkPWith(l : List(A))`). Decided from syntax and declarations only.
- **The class, hunted on the rule** (asserted, `Inductives.NestProps`, 28 rows, each rejection's message asserted): data through a proposition directly at a `Prop` parameter (`D1`), deeper (`D2`, `Q(And(PP(D2), True))`), through propositions only (`D3`, `QQ(PP(D3))`), at the type's own `Prop` parameter (`D4`), directly in a proposition at its `Type` parameter (`D5 := MkD5(h : PP(D5))`), through a parameter nested later (`E`/`DE`, `F`/`G`); a proposition through data directly (`QW`, `E3 := MkE3(q : Q(E3))`) and through a parameter nested later (`E2`/`QE`, `FP`/`EP`). Accepted neighbours: `P1`, `P2`, `B1` (same-sort nesting), `WithPP`, `PWith`/`UsePW` (the other sort at no occurrence of the type), and `E`, `F` at a closed type (`eNat`, `fNat`).
- **Two variants, measured.** The uniform rule above, and the least the model needs (the rule for `Prop`-sorted declarations only, with the first ruling's "no data-sorted declaration inside an argument at a `Prop` parameter" for data-sorted ones, which the knot's grammar asks, `SpineArgs.type`). Every row the second rejects the first rejects too (an argument at a `Prop` parameter that mentions a type of data is an applied proposition mentioning it, or an equation or a call, which D36 rejects), and the first changes no row outside the new block. So neither loses a program, and the uniform rule is the one adopted. On the first ruling's version `D5`, `DE`, `QW`, `E3`, `QE` and `EP` were accepted; on this one they are rejected, as by Lean.
- **Rows that change**: none outside the new block.
- **Evidence**: D120's.

## D117. The frame in which a dependent field's type is computed binds what a loan stands for (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-21) [lane: checker6, from checker4's handoff]
Decision (the orchestrator, 2026-11-21, ruled RESOLVE on checker4's finding; deferred by a budget cut and landed 2026-11-22). To type a field whose type mentions earlier fields (`x : Fin1(n)` in `V := MkV(n : Word, x : Fin1(n))`), `fieldTypesOf` binds the earlier fields in a frame of its own and evaluates the type there (D64). It bound each field's stored value. A field lent out (`let r = &n`) holds a loan, so the frame held a second occurrence of the loan, and reading `n` there (the type `Fin1(n)` reads it) ended `r`'s borrow on the type's private copy, with [End]'s side checks. Inside a side of `Id`, that ending is [Id-typestate]'s, so `def SId (c0 : V) : Unit := (let c = c0; match c { MkV(n, x) => (let r = &n; let h : Id(Unit, (let y = clone(x); ()), ()) = refl; ()) })`, whose side only reads `x`, was rejected ("a side of Id ends the borrow r"), and with it its closed instance. RULES [Field] already said "each earlier field bound to the current content of `p`'s field"; the frame bound the loan instead.
- **Rule.** The frame binds what each earlier field stands for (`Machine.resolveLoans`): a live loan its borrow's content, recursively, a mark the content it marks, through successors and constructor values, as `valType?` reads a loan and a mark for typing. A loan whose borrow is not in Ω stays as it is. Computing a field's type then ends no borrow. A side of `Id` that reads the lent index itself still ends `r`'s borrow and is rejected (`SIdReadN`). Value-dependent by design, as D64's field types are.
- **The class, hunted** (`DependentFields.ScaffoldLoans`, 18 rows, asserted): every frame the checker builds from stored values, reached with the index lent: the field's type in an `Id` side (`SId`, `SIdRun`, the finding; `SIdFree` and `SIdReadN` its twins), outside `Id` on an owned value and through a borrow parameter (`SRead`, `SReadB`), with the index written through the borrow between two reads (`SReadW`), an equation's field types (`SEq`), a constructor's hint (`SCtor`), a closure's captures (`SClo`), a call's parameter types (`SCall`), a stuck block's captures (`SClose`), a second match on the value (`SRematch`), and closed instances. Before: `SId` and `SIdRun` rejected; every other row accepted; and 51 states in 11 of the block's declarations held a loan twice (`sweep --deep-private`'s WF2), every one through the field-type frame. After: `SId`, `SIdRun` accepted, the others as before, and no state holds a loan twice. The other frames (a constructor's hint, captures, parameter frames, [Close]'s cells) bind no loan: their values are temporaries, or accessed first, which ends the borrows inside them.
- **[Frame] F4.** `endTopLoan` allows a loan from below inside a private copy because the scaffolding's copies may hold one. With F4 made strict there too (a scratch build), rejected rows of `OpenAttacks` and `DepVec` change their reason to F4's internal error, so other private-copy frames still copy such loans; the exception stays, and its comment says so.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, main (7fd3139a) with the new block copied in and softened, against this change: both located modes over the 131 blocks of the tour, unlocated over the case studies. Identical but the new block's `SId` and `SIdRun` (rejected → accepted) and, in located mode, two rows that lose one editor note each (`DepFields.CellGet` 42 → 41, `DepFields.GetLentOk` 47 → 46: the note of the frame's read ending the index's borrow on its copy). No verdict outside the new block changes.
  - `lake exe sweep` over every block: every clause's count identical outside the new block (the whole difference, 17 states and 6 of the by-design clause "the type of an unknown holds an unknown", is `ScaffoldLoans`'s two newly accepted rows, checked by sweeping the block alone on both sides). `--probes` identical; `--pushes` clean, identical. `--deep-private` over the tour: WF2 "a loan occurring twice" 73 states → 0 (22 in the suite before the new block, in 12 declarations: `DepFields.LieLent`, `GetLent`, `RefillLentLive`, `CellGet`, `GetLentOk`, `LeafFields.LentIndexLie`, `OpenAttacks.C3a`, `C3b`, …); over the case studies it did not finish in ten minutes and was not run to the end.
  - New asserted block `DependentFields.ScaffoldLoans` (18 rows), `SIdReadN`'s message asserted; on main it fails at `SId` and `SIdRun`. Count 4,011 → 4,029.
  - `StabilitySig`: `resolveLoans` is outside the machine's mutual block; `sigPres_resolveLoans` proves it keeps the signature and is registered for `sp`, so `refSP`'s `fieldTypesOf` case goes through unchanged. No new `sorry`.
  - RULES ([Field], the history line) and the appendix's typing-by-position paragraph say so. The Conclusion ends on page 25.

## D122. `clone` and `peek` take a place, in the core as in the surface; the checker inlines `clone` of a captured proof as the proof (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-22) [lane: checker7]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the consistency proof's simulation, whose reference run has no copying run for an arbitrary term; pinned on the proof line by `Meaning/Steps/MasterGuards.lean`, `bodyCL`, on `spine`; ruled (b) of the two options below). The surface accepts `clone` of a place only ("clone takes a place: clone(p)"), and `peek`, the final read of a sealed program ([Close]), has no surface syntax. But the core checker ran `clone(u)` and `peek(u)` of any term in copying mode, where every read of a place leaves it in place, so the core program `let x = S(Z); let y = clone(S(x)); x` was accepted, `x` still there after the clone; without `clone` the read of `x` in `S(x)` moves it and the last read is rejected. The reference runs a place operand of `clone` in place and any other operand by its plain run, which moves `x`, so its simulation is false at that program.
- **Rule.** `evalCore` rejects `clone(u)` and `peek(u)` when `u` is not a place, for every core term, written or built: "[D122] clone takes a place: clone(…)", "[D122] peek takes a place: peek(…)". Decided from syntax.
- **The producer, found before the change.** The checker built one such term itself. `capture` (a closure, `fix`, and a Π-type, `pi`) inlines each read of a captured proof binding as `(⋆ : T)` (`Term.inlineReads`; a proof's value is `⋆`, so its declared type is all it records), so `clone(h)` in the text of a closure or a Π-type that captures a proof `h` became `clone((⋆ : T))`. Measured with a variant that rejected `clone`/`peek` of a non-place with a marker: over every example block, the marker was reached at one accepted row, `Recursion.CloneProof.KClo` (`def KClo (h : ⊤) : ⊤ := (let c = (λ(u : Unit) : ⊤ => clone(h)); c(()))`), and one rejected row's reason (`CloneK2.P16`); and in a hunt of 17 programs, every one accepted on main, at eleven: a λ's body, its codomain and a parameter type, a Π-type's codomain and domain, a Π-type as a result type, nested λs, a `let`-bound proof, a λ as an argument, a λ in a stuck match's arm, a side of `Id` (and two closed runs calling them). Not reached: `clone(p.0)` of a captured proof (only whole-variable reads are inlined), captured data (a place inside the closure), a captured proof read without `clone`. Every other place the checker builds terms was checked and builds no such term: [Close]'s fills (`peek` of a cell or of `*r`, both places), sealed programs and their re-runs (`substT` rewrites only embedded values), closed-off blocks (`mapFreePlace` maps places to places; `inplace` of a place), the observation in `Id`, the surface's desugaring (`withCallRoot` gives `let x = f(ā); clone(x.path)`), the dependent-field scaffolding (`let fname = (⋆ : T)`, so `clone(fname)` stays a place), and D116's clone of a proof place.
- **The producer, changed.** `Term.inlineReads` replaces `clone` (and `peek`) of a read of the inlined variable by `(⋆ : T)` itself, not `clone((⋆ : T))`: the clone of a proof is that proof (D116), applied at the inlining. The value (`⋆`), the erasure flags (`(true, true)`, an ascribed proof) and the declared reading (`clone(u)` reads as `u`) are those of the old term, so every program of the hunt keeps its verdict and value. `peek` cannot reach `inlineReads` (it is built only by [Close]'s fills, which hold no closure or Π-type), and is handled the same way so that no built term depends on that.
- **The other option, not taken.** (a) The rule for written terms only, checked on a declaration's terms before anything runs (as D118), with `evalCore` still running `clone((⋆ : T))`: the core would then accept a form only the checker produces, and the proof's simulation, stated for core terms, would still fail at exactly those built terms.
- **What compares stored text sees the change.** The stored text of a closure or a Π-type that clones a captured proof changes at those reads, `clone((⋆ : T))` becoming `(⋆ : T)`. Conversion, the observation of `Id` and the footprints read stored terms. Checked: two closures differing only by such a `clone` (`λ(u : Unit) : ⊤ => clone(h)` against `λ(u : Unit) : ⊤ => h`, and the same returning data, `(let q = clone(h); 0)` against `(let q = h; 0)`), by `Eq` and by `Id`, two function types so differing (`Π(u : Unit). Eq(⊤, clone(h), h)` against `Π(u : Unit). Eq(⊤, h, h)`, in `Prop`, and `Π(e : Eq(⊤, clone(h), h)). Unit` in `Type`), by `Eq` and by `Id`, and a typed `let` whose declared function type clones where its value's does not, and the reverse: accepted before (conversion of functions and of Π-types compares what they compute, D30, D48), and accepted after, where the two texts are now the same.
- **Evidence.**
  - A dump of every row's verdict, message, location and editor-note count, main (c93b420e) with the new block copied in and softened, against this change: unlocated over all 145 blocks (4,060 rows), identical; located over the 132 blocks of the tour (3,642 rows), identical but the editor-note counts of 18 rows, each one fewer (the note of the `clone((⋆ : T))` node, now `(⋆ : T)`): `CloneProof.KClo`, `CloneK2.P16` and 16 rows of `CloneCaptured`. `CloneK2.P16` (`(let f = (λ(u : Unit) : Nat => clone(h)); 0)` with `h : ⊤`) keeps its verdict, message and location, before and after: "the body of _ has type ⊤, but the goal is Nat" (the closure's body is `(⋆ : ⊤)` where it was `clone((⋆ : ⊤))`). No verdict changes anywhere.
  - New asserted block `Recursion.CloneCaptured` (31 rows: the hunt, closed runs with values by `refl`, and the comparisons above), every row accepted on main and here; and core guards (`Ochr.Examples.CloneCore`): `clone(S(x))` and `peek(S(x))` rejected with the D122 messages (false on main), `clone(x)` accepted with `x` still there, `S(x)` without `clone` rejected. Mutation-tested: a wrong value row (`KDataRun` at 6) and a wrong guard each fail the build. Count 4,029 → 4,060.
  - `lake exe sweep`: every clause's violation count identical but the by-design clause "a stored type holds an unknown", 44,408 → 44,404, and 100,930 → 100,892 states: the whole difference is the four clone blocks (`CloneProof`, `CloneK2`, `CloneK3`, `CloneCaptured` swept alone: 1,262 → 1,224 states, that clause 29 → 25; one evaluation fewer per inlined `clone`). `--probes` identical; `--pushes` identical and clean.
  - The probe files under `probes/` (25): identical output.
  - The build is green with no new `sorry`: no stability proof reads `inlineReads` or the `clone` case of `evalCore`.
  - RULES (P3, the history line) say so. The paper already writes `clone(p)` of a place (its grammar and [Clone]), so its text is unchanged.

## D124. The untyped run hands a hint exactly where the typed check does (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-22) [lane: checker7]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the consistency proof's audit of every hint site, docs/31 D172 on `spine-lead24`: every sub-run of the reference's run with the hint the typed check, the end-of-body check and the untyped run hand down; ruled first for the tails, then widened to every site where the typed and untyped runs differ). A constructor whose parameters are not written takes them from its hint, the type its position expects, before its fields (D46, D89). The typed check hands a hint only at some positions, decided from syntax: a call's argument, a constructor's field and an assigned value when the term is a constructor (or an embedded value, a `rewrite`, a hole), an ascription's term, and, in tail position, the goal (`checkTail`). The untyped run (a called body, a sealed program's re-run, the runtime) decided otherwise: a `let`'s and a `;`'s tail and both matches' arms got the hint at every tail flag (`if typed then none else hint`); a call's argument, a constructor's field and an ascription's term got it by D89's test (`needsCtx`: the term builds a constructor its fields do not determine) instead of typing's; a called body likewise (`runBody`, against `checkFix`'s goal); an assigned value never; a match on a proof never, also in tail position. So a run of checked code could read a hint its check never had (`let y = Z; N` out of tail position, at the hint `L(Nat)`, recorded `N[Nat]` in a run, and the typed check rejects it), and could miss one its check had (an assigned `N`, the one arm of a match on a proof in tail position, recorded no parameter). The proof's reference follows the typed run at every such site, so its untyped steps needed the two to agree.
- **Rule.** At each site the untyped run follows the typed check's decision, read from syntax: out of tail position a `let`'s and a `;`'s tail and the arms of a match (on data or, by type, on a proof) get no hint, in tail position the hint (`checkTail` hands the goal); an assigned value gets the place's type when it is a constructor or an embedded value; a call's argument gets its parameter's type when it is a constructor, an embedded value, `rewrite`, `rewriteR` or a hole; a field of a constructor of a parameterised type gets its type when the field is a constructor, `rewrite` or `rewriteR`, and any field when it is an embedded value or a dependent field; an ascription's term gets the annotation; a called body gets its declared codomain at the arguments. A hint is computed only where the term would read it (`Machine.readsHint`: a constructor of a parameterised type with its parameters not written; an embedded value, `rewrite` and `rewriteR`, whose type the untyped run takes from the hint, only where that type is read, a borrow argument (its type is its cell's at [Close]); through `clone`, `peek` and `inplace`; and in tail position through `let`, `;` and match arms; nothing else in an untyped `evalCore` reads it, a hole being stuck), and a called body's declared codomain is handed down unevaluated (`hintLater`) and evaluated where it is read (`forceHint`: a constructor with its parameters not written, an embedded value, `rewrite`), from the arguments as they were at the call, unless an argument is a borrow (then at once). No run can tell either from computing every hint at the hand-down, and nothing is computed that could fail where nothing reads it. D89's test (`needsCtx`, `ctorNeedsCtx`) is no longer read by the machine; it is kept because the proof line's files name it.
- **Two sites of the audit are not in this entry**: `J`'s transported term, which the typed check handed `P(a)` where `J` is stuck (closed off as a block's arm) and nothing where it computes (ruled later the same day: `P(a)` on both typed paths, and the run where the term reads it; see "D124, `J`" below), and an embedded value's type (typed: `valType`, the hint only for a proof or a loan; untyped: the hint; made to agree the same day, see "D124, an embedded value's type" below).
- **The class, hunted before the change** (`Inductives.UntypedHints`, 211 rows, generated): a constructor `X` that needs its hint (`N`), whose fields determine it (`C(n, N)`, the inner `N` by its field type) or written (`C[Nat](n, N[Nat])`), under a wrapper (bare, a `let`'s tail, a `;`'s tail, a match's arms) at a position (the end of a body; a field of a constructor with no parameter and of a parameterised one; a call's argument; a `let` with a declared type; an assigned value; an ascription as an argument; a dropped argument), each a generic definition (the typed check) and its closed instance (the untyped run of the body at a call) with its value by `refl`; and a match on a proof in tail position, a body that needs no hint, and `J` stuck, computing, and with a constructor its fields determine or written.
