Pinned on states (`Examples/Units.lean`, `SelfMarks`, read at every recorded state of each body): a non-recursive global's binding of itself carries a mark and the [Rec] stack is empty; a recursive global's mark is the stack's one entry's; a local function's binding of itself is marked and it has no entry. **Evidence.** No verdict changes: the full dump against main (a3bac2015), unlocated and located over 5,462 rows, identical, messages, locations and editor-note counts included; `lake exe sweep`, `--probes` and `--pushes` identical (no count moves: the sweep does not print the mark); the 25 probe files identical. Mutation: with the mark again only for a function with `by`, the guards for the non-recursive global and the local function fail the build, the recursive global's holds. Cost (instructions retired on `cpu_core`, one core (10), interleaved, three runs each, medians): `Quicksort` 81.943G → 81.945G (+0.002%), `HashMapTests` 28.8436G → 28.8465G (+0.01%), `Arrays` 1.9719G → 1.9724G (+0.02%). The build is green with no new `sorry`. RULES and the paper say which calls are recursive, not which bindings are marked, so neither changes. Missed at D136's landing because the deletion was judged by what the checker reads (an instrumented build looked for calls whose mark and value disagree), and nothing the checker decides reads the mark of a function without `by`.

## D137. [Close] makes a fill `()` at a `Unit` place in the data row only: the documents were wrong, the checker right (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-27) [lane: checker12]
Finding (the theory lane, `ochr/docs/18-principles.typ` `<sec-rd>`, "The `Unit` row: the code keeps the hole, the paper drops it"). The paper (appendix A.2, [Close]: "$f_i$ is `()` when $c_i$'s place is declared `Unit`") and `RULES.md` (§3 [Close]'s η paragraph, and §4's position (3)) applied η at `Unit` to a fill in both rows of [Close]; D80's R3 says it without a row. The checker's `closeCall` has always applied it in the data row only (`fills.getD i false` in its `| _ =>` branch): in the borrow row every fill is the sealed program with the returned borrow's hole `loan_k`, `Unit` places included. The code's choice is the one consistency needs (docs/18's resolved determination: the caller's places, once the borrows between them have ended, determine what was written through a borrow). Under the documents' rule, `g(&u)` with `g : Π(u : &Unit). &Nat` leaves its hole in no place, writes through its result are observed nowhere, and with `G5 (x : &Nat) (e : Id(Unit, *x := 0, *x := 1)) : False := e`, `QU (g : Π(u : &Unit). &Nat) : False := (let u = (); G5(g(&u), refl))` would be accepted: a refutation of a type safe Rust inhabits with a leaked borrow, the fault D44 excludes for `Π(n : Nat). &Nat`, with D44's guard (a borrow-returning function type takes a borrow) met by a `&Unit` that carries nothing.
- **Rule** (no change to the checker): [Close]'s fill at a place declared `Unit` is `()` when the call's declared result type is not a borrow; in the `&T` row it keeps its hole. RULES (§3 [Close], §4's position (3), the history line) and the paper (A.2's [Close] paragraph, with the reason; A.3's list of η positions) now say so. A comment at `closeCall`'s borrow row says it in the code.
- **Pinned** (the asserted block `ClosingOff.UnitFill`, 13 rows): `QU`, rejected on main before any change (a3bac2015) with "argument 2 (e) has type ⊤, expected Eq(Unit, ⌈let c1 = (); let r = σ0(&c1); *r := 0; c1⌉, ⌈let c1 = (); let r = σ0(&c1); *r := 1; c1⌉)", the message docs/18 records; docs/18's variants, each rejected with its message pinned: two `Unit` places (`QU2`), a borrow parameter declared `&Unit` (`QU3`), an annotated `let` (`QU4`), a `Unit` value parameter (`QU5`), a type function every arm of which is `Unit` (`QU6`), a field of a pair (`QUp`); the twin `QUOk` (the true statement through the same call, accepted). At the `Unit` place itself the two rows side by side: `QD (g : Π(u : &Unit). Nat) : Id(Unit, (let u = (); g(&u); u), ()) := refl`, accepted (the data row's fill is `()`), and its twin `QB` with `g : Π(u : &Unit). &Nat` and `let r = g(&u)`, rejected: true, since `u` has one value, and the price of keeping the hole. Every rejection's message pinned; mutation-tested (a flipped row and a wrong message each fail the build).
- **Evidence.** With the documents' rule built into `closeCall` (η in the borrow row too), `QU`, `QU2` to `QU6`, `QUp` and `QB` are accepted and the build fails at those rows only: every other module of examples and case studies builds (only the registry and the root import `ClosingOff`), so nothing else in the suite tells the two rules apart. The dump of this change against the previous commit, unlocated: the 13 new rows only. Count 5,462 → 5,475. The paper's Conclusion still ends on page 25. D80's R3 ("a lent place's fill is `()` exactly when the place is declared `Unit`") is to be read with this restriction.

## D138. A match on a proof binds its data fields as locals of the arm: the documents were wrong, the checker right (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-27) [lane: checker13]
Finding (the fifth referee, `ochr/docs/18-principles.typ` `<sec-referee5>`, objection V2). The paper ([T-Match-prop], [T-Match-erased] and [Tail-prop] in appendix A.3 and the paragraph before them: arm $i$ is checked from $Omega_1 [p |-> ty("C")_i (overline(v); overline(w))]$, each data field "a fresh abstract value") and `RULES.md` ("its single arm binds … data fields as places holding fresh abstract values") say a match on a proof whose constructor has data fields writes a constructor value at the scrutinee. The checker never has: in a typed run `bindDataFields` (`Machine.lean`, applied by `evalMatchByType` to a one-arm and a several-arm match and by `checkTail`) rewrites the arm, binding each data field as a local `let g = σ`, σ fresh (for a dependent constructor every field, in order, a proof field to `⋆` at its type from the earlier ones), and reads every place at or under the field's place in the arm, inside nested matches too, as the local; the scrutinee stays `⋆`. The two differ on an accepted program: a nested match on the same proof names the same field places, so its pattern variables are the outer arm's locals and see its writes. With `inductive Ex : Prop := Wit(w : Nat, h : Eq(Nat, w, w))`, `Nest (e : Ex) : True := match e { Wit(w, h) => (w := 5; match e { Wit(w2, h2) => (let q : Eq(Nat, w2, 5) = refl; refl) }) }` is accepted; under the documents' rule the inner match would write a fresh constructor value at `e`, `w2` would be a fresh unknown, and `Nest` would be rejected. The checker's choice is kept: by reading both are sound (the arm is a declared proof run on a private copy, and nothing computed from the fields leaves it), the code has always done it, and the consistency lane's model is to follow the locals (V2's "what would settle it").
- **Rule** (no change to the checker): the documents now describe the locals: the paper's paragraph before [T-Match-prop] (the arm checked is $t_i^*$, the arm with its fields bound as locals, from $Omega_1$ itself, with `Nest` as the example) and the three rules' premises ($Omega_1 tack.r t_i^*$), and `RULES.md`'s paragraph on matching on a proof and its history line.
- **Pinned** (the asserted block `Subsingletons`, beside `Sq`): `Ex`, `Nest` accepted, and its control `NestNo`, the same arms claiming `w2` holds 6, rejected with its message pinned ("the ascribed term has type ⊤, expected False": `w2` is the local holding 5). On main before any change (ec8d578db): `Nest` accepted, `NestNo` rejected with that message, and, run beside them, `TwoW` (`Eq(Nat, w, w2)` across the two matches) accepted, the same nested match without the write rejected with "expected Eq(Nat, σ0, 5)" (`w2` is the outer arm's unknown, not a fresh one), `Boom` (a write to an index data field, then a match on the proof field) rejected with "[Match] on h, whose type Eq(Nat, σ1, S(σ2)) is not an inductive type" (a proof field's type is fixed when the arm starts), and `LocalForm` (an `Id` over a data field, `Eq(Nat, 0, 1)`) accepted, as V2 reports. Count 5,475 → 5,478.

## D139. A function being checked is named by its global in a closure's code, and is not captured (orchestrator, user to acknowledge, 2026-11-28) [lane: checker15]
Decision (the orchestrator, from the proof lane: `origin/spine-exp-block`, `ochr/docs/31-meaning-spine.md` D237, and docs/32, "A function being checked is named by its global wherever its code is captured"). **The property, in one sentence: a closure or Π-type formed while a function's check is under way never keeps that function among its captures; its code names the function by its global, as the head of a call, the only place fix L1 lets it occur.**
- **The gap.** With the `Nat` prelude, `def CL0r (m : Nat) (n : Nat) : Nat by n := (let g = (λ(j : Nat) : Nat => match n { Z => Z, S(k) => CL0r(j, clone(k)), }); g(m))` is accepted, and `g`'s value was `fix _ (j : Nat) : Nat := match κ2 { Z => 0, S _ => κ1(j, clone(κ2.0)) } where κ1 = CL0r, κ2 = σ1`: the closure captured the function under check, recorded at its declared type `Π(m : Nat) (n : Nat). Nat`, and so did its Π-type. The consistency proof reads a function still under check as "some function good on smaller arguments" (its stage closure), which is not a member of that type (kernel-checked in the proof lane, `StageBlock.capsOk_stage0`), so the capture had no meaning there and the proof's reading of `g` failed where the direct run gives a number. Nothing a program observes depended on it, except one verdict (below).
- **Rule and code.** Stated once, in `capture` (Machine.lean), which forms every closure and every Π-type: a captured variable whose value is a function under check (`underCheck`, the [Rec] stack's: since D136 the top-level definition being checked, whose value is its global) is not kept; its reads in the code (call heads, by fix L1) become the global `.val (.gfn f)`, and the captures, their recorded declared types and the closure's Π-type leave it out. An internal error guards the claim that only reads remain. The variable is still taken as every capture is (accessed, and moved unless its type is a copy), so the state after the closure is formed is the same as before (`MV`: a direct call after a λ took the binding is still rejected, "[Read] MV was moved out"). It is recognised by its value, not by D78's mark, so that it also holds where the function reaches a λ through a stuck block's parameter at the block's re-run (`BL`). The global reads exactly as the variable did: its declared reading is the definition's Π-type's (`valueDecl` of a global), the same reading the binding of itself carries, and a call through it is tested by [Rec] through value equality with the entry, which since D136 is exact.
- **The hunt** (`scratchpad/checker15/HuntBlocks.lean` and a scanner, `ScanLib.lean`/`GfnScan.lean`, that lists every place the value of a function under check rests in a recorded state, at every call depth and inside private copies; table in `scratchpad/reports/checker15.md`). Programs, each on main and with the rule: a λ called and at a hole (`CL0`, `CL0r`); a λ calling it from inside its own check (`CLH`); nested λs (`NL`); λs in a type (`TyL`); a Π-type value (`PiC`); a λ's parameter type and codomain, a Π-typed annotation (`LP`, `LC`, `PiH`); a constructor's field (`BX`); an argument of a top-level higher-order function (`HoL`) and of the function itself (`FA`); a deferred type, the binding of an untyped `let` of the λ's call (`DT`); a generalisation record (`GR`); a sealed re-run (`SR`); a comparison of function values (`CV`); the head of a sealed call and its generalisation (`SC`, `SG`); the function as a value other than a call's head (an argument of a higher-order call or of a λ, a `let`, `clone`, `&`, an assignment, an ascribed head, a field, an `Eq` side: all rejected by fix L1, as on main); stuck blocks (`MB0`, `MB0r`, `BL`). On main the function rested, besides its own binding and the head of a call being made, only in closures' and Π-types' captures (and what holds them: bindings, fields, arguments, deferred types, generalisation records, the comparison stack), in capture bindings marked with its [Rec] mark, and in stuck blocks' parameters. Over the whole registry (2,641,644 recorded states, the same on both sides) the same: captures in `Recursion`, `UnderCheck`, `LocalRec`, `ProofCopies` and `Quicksort`, block parameters in `ClosingOff.NonTailRec`, `UnderCheck.BlkRe`, `BlkSel`, `ProofCopies.C1`, `SelfArm`. With the rule every capture and every capture mark is gone; what remains is the head of a call and the block parameters.
- **Left to the decision on blocks** (not changed here): a stuck block takes the function as a parameter, is applied to it, and its call's deferred parameter types hold it (`MB0r`; docs/32's first form of D139 and the experiment `exp-block-text`).
- **D78's marks, what they still decide: nothing.** A mark is carried by a function's binding of itself (D136's amendment) and, through `capMarks`/`markCapRec`, by a closure's capture of a marked binding. Only a recursive definition's body can name its binding of itself (D31), and there it holds the function under check, which is no longer captured; so no capture carries a mark. Measured on an instrumented build (a marker wherever a kept capture carries a mark, or a call is recognised by its mark and not by its value) over the suite and the case studies (5,522 rows), the hunt's programs and the 25 probe files: no marker. A call from a closure's code is now recognised by its value only (its head is the global, which has no binding). Kept, as ruled: the mark on the binding of itself is read by the consistency proof; `capMarks` now reads the marks of the captures kept.
- **One verdict changes, rejected to accepted (a widening, messaged to the orchestrator).** A λ nested in a λ, the inner one calling the function under check: `NL (m : Nat) (n : Nat) : Nat by n := match n { Z => Z, S(k) => (let g = (λ(u : Unit) : Nat => (let d = clone(k); let h = (λ(j : Nat) : Nat => NL(j, clone(d))); h(Z))); g(())), }` was rejected by D53 ("a closure's body moves a captured value (κ) into a closure; clone it"): the outer λ captured `NL` and the inner λ's capture of that capture moved it. There is no such capture now, and `NL` is accepted, with `NL(3, 2) = 0` by `refl`. No λ nested in a λ could call the recursive function before. Its calls are tested by [Rec] as any other's.
- **Evidence.**
  - Asserted block `Recursion.NamedByGlobal` (44 rows: the hunt's routes, with closed runs; `MV`; fix L1's nine rejections; seven rows whose message prints a closure's code or a Π-type, each showing the function by its name and not among the captures, e.g. "fix _ (j : Nat) : Nat := match κ1 { Z => 0, S _ => CL0Code(j, clone(κ1.0)) } where κ1 = σ1"), every rejection's message pinned, and a guard over every state the block's checks record (1,476 states, at every call depth, private copies included): no closure or Π-type anywhere in a state keeps a function under check, and no capture binding holds one or carries its mark, while 477 states hold a closure whose code names one. Mutation-tested: with the rule off, `NL`, the printed rows, the state guard and `UnderCheck.ConvFn` fail the build; a flipped row and a wrong message each fail it. `UnderCheck.ConvFn`'s pinned message updated (it prints a closure). Count 5,478 → 5,522.
  - Dump, main (05292097d) with the new tests copied in and softened, against this change: unlocated and located, every row of the suite and the case studies identical, messages, locations and editor-note counts included, except `UnderCheck.ConvFn`'s message, which prints the closure (`ConvFn(clone(κ1.0)) where κ1 = 1` for `κ1(clone(κ2.0)) where κ1 = ConvFn, κ2 = 1`; same location and note count); in the new block, `NL` and `NLRuns` rejected → accepted, `NLCode` rejected by its printed code instead of D53, and the six other printed rows with the function by its name.
  - `lake exe sweep`: every difference is the new block's own (equal to a sweep of `NamedByGlobal` alone on both sides: 49 more states, from `NL`), except one premise count in the five blocks where the function was captured ("a type mentions a run-time value": 9,833 → 9,824 states, the closures' types no longer holding the function); no clause gains a violation. `--probes` identical; `--pushes`: the same census, no temporary pushed without a stored type. The 25 probe files under `probes/` identical.
  - Cost (instructions retired on `cpu_core`, one core (10), interleaved, three runs each, medians): `Quicksort` 81.942G → 81.955G (+0.02%), `HashMapTests` 28.8464G → 28.8461G (−0.00%), `Arrays` 1.97302G → 1.97270G (−0.02%). A first measurement showed +0.7% on `Arrays`: the state guard's census had been written as a constant, which every executable importing the examples (the bench, `tests`, `sweep`) evaluates when it starts; it is a function now.
  - The build is green with no new `sorry`. RULES ([Closure], [Rec], the history line) and the paper (§4's closures paragraph, one sentence on the line it ends; the appendix's [Capture] and [Rec]) say so; the Conclusion ends on page 25, at the same place.
